# M.O.T.H. — design

**Status (2026-11-02, MO1, Opus 5.5 `claude-opus-5-5`): Session 1's design, final for the build.** Measured at `9a2d7023` (code equal to `91d696b0`), designed in six areas, each design attacked by a security and a correctness reviewer, then every finding checked against the code (taken, refuted or partly, with the evidence) and the six resolved designs integrated into one, with her answers applied. Section 1 is what the build implements, in its migration order; section 2 keeps each area's resolved design and its findings' dispositions as the design review's record. Nothing here is built yet. The build's own two review rounds come after it. The design round's raw record (the six drafts and twelve reviews) is commit `e0e72cac`.

## 0. Her settled answers (PLAN_MOTH §9, verbatim, 2026-11-01)

- **Q1 Models:** *"Opus for all sessions"*
- **Q2 History:** *"Show graph structure and metadata from Moth’s first recorded baseline onward, retain 90 days, and label periods with no recorded history; file bodies are not historical copies."*
- **Q3 NAS:** *"Refresh metadata when a project is opened on desktop and through a clear Refresh action; show when it was last checked. (Recommended)"*
- **Q4 Long notes:** *"Use the existing save-and-merge behaviour for long notes in this release, state that limit plainly, and add live co-typing after release."*
- **Q5 Plan/cap:** *"Keep the current plan with the spend cap enabled; record its name and remaining usage before live-board acceptance. - note: claude will know the type of plan"*
- **Q6 Performance projects:** *"use the sample project the debug mode has. also the local saved version of the wilson tool locally and use the saved test project. claude will know this project"*

Her brief's rulings (`briefs/MOTH_PLANNING_BRIEF.md` §8, §12 and §12a) bind every section below and are not restated.

### Her answers to the design round's six questions (2026-11-02 UTC, this session's chat)

Asked as multiple choice in plain words; her choice verbatim in italics, then the option's own text as it was offered. **Where an answer differs from a design below, the answer wins**; the synthesis carries it into the design.

1. **A private project's picture or video moved into a shared project or into a personal space** (those bodies live only on the computer they were added from): *"Copy it up from there"*: allowed only from the computer that holds it; the file is copied up to the company's storage as part of the move. (The design round had recommended refusing; §1's and §3's refusals are superseded.)
2. **A file moved between two projects that live only on one computer:** *"Move the real file"*: the file moves into the other project's folder on the same computer (nothing is uploaded), checked before WILSON says it is done. (The design round had recommended deferring; superseded.)
3. **Personal-space files of a company whose storage is an office server** (hidden inside WILSON, findable by anyone who opens the server directly): *"Yes, say so (Recommended)"*: kept on the office server, and people are told this in plain words when they add files to their personal space.
4. **An admin opening someone else's personal space from the Files page:** *"Yes, admins see it (Recommended)"*: a record goes into the admin activity list, where other admins can see it.
5. **Deleting, in WILSON, a file that lives on the office server** (offered: list it again after the 30-day bin empties, or never list it again), her own words: *"deleting a file in wilson should delete it in the folder. remember it acts like a file manager. for this also make sure to ask them to confirm they want to delete before doing so."* Read with her §12a ruling 5 (confirmation, the usual undo, a trash view): after the confirmation the desktop moves the real file into a bin folder on that server, so it leaves its folder; undo and restore bring it back; after 30 days it is removed for good; in a browser, which cannot reach the server, the delete says it is done from the desktop app (as previews already do). That reading was told to her in the chat when the answer was recorded. (§4's "WILSON never touches the file on the server" is superseded.)
6. **When Moth's other tables are built:** *"Each when used (Recommended)"*: views and layouts in Session 2; tags, links and shared notes in Session 3; boards and sticky locks in Session 4. (This changes `PLAN_MOTH.md` §4's allocation and Session 1's "all section 4 schemas"; Session 1 builds the personal space, safe moves and deletes, the office-server list, the privacy repairs and the history record.)

### Her answers to the integration's four questions (2026-11-02 UTC, this session's chat)

Asked as multiple choice in plain words; each answer is the recommended option, verbatim, then the option as offered.

1. **Who may run the office-server check** (it lists what the person checking can open, and everyone on the project then sees those names): *"Admins, managers (Recommended)"*: only company admins and managers and each project's own managers, and the Storage page says the list shows what the person checking can open.
2. **A leaver's personal files:** *"Yes, once they've left (Recommended)"*: an admin may move a former member's personal files into a project, only after that person is no longer an active member.
3. **The storage notice everyone sees** (its total now includes personal spaces): *"Full or nearly full (Recommended)"*: members see only whether storage is full or nearly full, with no amounts.
4. **Moth data for a project kept only on one computer:** *"Wait for the cloud (Recommended)"*: its layout, tags and boards wait until the project moves to the cloud (Session 2 onward).

What these four change in section 1, which was written before them (**the answers win**): answer 1 is the integrated default for who runs the office-server check, so nothing changes. **Answer 2 changes the move operation:** an admin may move a former member's personal-space files into a project once that person is no longer an active member (through the same checked move, refused while the person is active, recorded in the admin activity list as answer 4 of the first set requires); section 1's "admins can open and download, never move" is superseded. **Answer 3 adds work not yet placed in section 1.4:** members' storage notice shows only "full" or "nearly full", no amounts (the member path of `workspace_storage_usage()` coarsened, the admin views keep the figure; OUTSTANDING S4b-06 closes); it lands with the personal-space migration (0110), when personal bodies start counting, or as its own file beside it, decided when 0110 is written. Answer 4 keeps the deferral already in section 1.13.

## 1. Session 1, integrated: what the build implements

### 1.1 Summary

Session 1 is fourteen migrations, 0107 to 0120, built in order, and 0121, which the whole-session review added. 0107 to 0109 are the privacy repairs: write-side project hops, one projects owner guard, a same-project reference guard, a privacy arm on the money gate and on edit history, workspace-channel suppression, the per-project topic split and a topic epoch. They land before anything that creates or moves a personal or private row. 0110 adds the personal space as an ordinary private projects row: personal_owner_id, owner-only writes through the four restated project helpers, ensure_personal_space, and the admin activity line for answer 4. 0111 adds Moth's read path and the one money/Legal classifier, which every later area calls. 0112 adds the enum value network_share and nothing else. 0113 adds the move journal (file_moves) and the composite folder keys. 0114 holds the stable-body foundation: a private schema that the API does not expose, ONE files column guard with ONE list of trusted writers, rekey_file_body, the storage_path indexes and the id-reuse arm. 0115 restates the eight base storage policies so that authority follows the file's current project, adds the signer predicate and fixes the meter. 0116 is the single shared restatement of the file_events CHECK (13 terms), the event capture and the project broadcast. 0117 builds the office-server catalogue and its file-manager operations (journal, settle, bin folder). 0118 builds the one move operation moth_move_items, its undo and the Moth trash wrappers. 0119 builds moth_history last. 0120, which switches on s3 moves, is committed only after the new storage-presign is verified deployed. A move has one contract. The server plans every item as metadata, rehome, copy_up or share_move, mints every destination key itself, journals the operation and admits the row change only for that journalled item. Answer 1 (copy up a private project's media from the computer that holds it), answer 2 (move the real file between projects kept on one computer, through a desktop route) and answer 5 (the desktop moves the real office-server file into a bin folder on the server; undo and restore bring it back; it is removed at 30 days; a browser says to use the desktop app) all run through this one design. Session 1 creates four tables (file_moves, network_share_checks, network_share_ops, moth_history), so RLS_TABLES goes from 61 to 65. Views and layouts wait for Session 2, tags, links and notes for Session 3, boards and sticky locks for Session 4. The Moth page is a lazy-loaded registry page. It reads only through Moth RPCs that already exclude money, Legal and other people's personal spaces, and its Move to, Delete, Trash and undo all go through server-checked paths. Three of the owner's questions affect what is built or shown this session: who runs the office-server check, what admins may do with a leaver's personal files, and whether members should see the company's storage numbers. The build uses the safe default for each until she answers. A fourth, about local layouts, can wait until Session 2.

### 1.2 Conflicts between the areas, and how each was settled

- **bodies SB-8 vs moves MV-5/SEC-MV-11 vs NAS NS-D3 vs personal PS-6 (files column guards, item a)**: Three guards on public.files with three marker schemes. Bodies uses session_user NOT IN (postgres, supabase_admin) plus a boolean wilson.checked_move='on'. Moves binds the marker to a journal row checked by fn_checked_move_admits. NAS uses wilson.network_share_writer='check:<pid>'|'op:<fid>'. Personal adds a separate provider guard. Bodies' move-folder/money arm also refused rows that the shipped desktop-to-cloud migration creates.
  - *Settled:* ONE function, public.fn_files_body_guard, with trigger trg_files_body_guard BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON files, created in 0114. Not a definer; REVOKEd. Arms: (1) client roles (current_user IN authenticated, anon) may never change project_id, workspace_id, folder_id, storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url, is_financial, share_missing_since or share_bin_path (IS DISTINCT FROM, so resending the same values passes). A client INSERT needs provider IN (supabase, s3, local_server), rabbit_private.file_key_admissible and a derived picture. (2) Bound definers, meaning current_user is not a client role but COALESCE(current_setting('role',true),'') IN (authenticated, anon), the repairs R5 test with 0049's role-GUC precedent: workspace_id never changes. project_id, folder_id (to non-NULL) and the locator columns change only when public.fn_checked_move_admits(NEW.id, NEW.project_id, NEW.folder_id, NEW.storage_provider::text, NEW.storage_path) is true, or wilson.body_rekey = 'file:'||NEW.id with the provider unchanged, or, for network_share rows only and never project_id, wilson.network_share_writer bound to that row or project. folder_id → NULL always passes (RI). (3) On a network_share row, size_bytes, source_modified_at, mime_type, deleted_at, deleted_by, share_missing_since and share_bin_path change only under the NAS marker. Generic soft_delete_row and restore_soft_deleted are therefore refused for office-server rows, and their own RPCs are used. (4) The personal provider rule is folded in: a row in a personal project may never be local_server or google_drive, for every bound caller. (5) Unbound sessions pass: migrations, cron, service_role Edge Functions, pgTAP setup as postgres. session_user is dropped: in pgTAP session_user is postgres, so bodies' planted-definer probe could never bite. The rehearsal must confirm the role GUC reads 'authenticated' inside a client-called definer (probe in 115). Bodies' money-folder arm is dropped (moves MV-R1). runMigration instead files non-money rows out of locked folders (SFB-07).
- **bodies (move_files, p_rehome client-built key, third segment 'files') vs moves (moth_move_items, plan/commit, server-minted key under 'project/<dest>') vs NAS (separate network_share_move with its own op journal) (item b)**: Two move names and two re-home mechanisms, and NAS moves were outside the move RPC. Bodies has the client build the destination key; moves has the server mint it; the two use different key shapes.
  - *Settled:* ONE operation: public.moth_move_items(p_file_ids uuid[], p_dest_project uuid, p_dest_folder uuid DEFAULT NULL, p_plan_only boolean DEFAULT false, p_bodies jsonb DEFAULT NULL) RETURNS jsonb, with the internal core fn_moth_apply_moves. Files only in Session 1, no folder sources. The plan answers how per item: 'metadata' (supabase, or s3 once switched on, whose key's origin is a shared project); 'rehome' (supabase or s3 whose key's second segment names a private project or a personal space other than the destination, bodies SB-12); 'copy_up' (a local_server body, answer 1, desktop that holds it only); 'share_move' (network_share). The SERVER mints to_path: projects/<dest>/files/<file_id>/<epoch ms>-<name sanitised to [A-Za-z0-9._-]>, bodies' allowlisted 'files' segment, or nas:<dest space folder>/<folder path>/<leaf> for office-server targets. The commit re-validates everything. For rehome and copy_up into company storage it checks p_bodies against the minted pattern, file_key_admissible and that the object exists. The core calls rabbit_private.files_body_follow/files_body_retire for the body contract and does the project, folder, link-clearing and locator change in ONE UPDATE under the journal marker. Office-server items are folded in: the commit moves the row and writes a pending network_share_ops 'move' (or 'adopt' for a copy-up into an office-server company). The desktop renames or copies the real file, then calls network_share_settle. 'not_moved' replays the origin through a journalled reverse move. NAS's separate network_share_move and network_share_undo are dropped. Answer 2 (between two desktop-only projects) is the desktop route POST /api/rabbit/files/move-between-projects, not the RPC. Undo is moth_undo_move(p_move_id, p_plan_only), which replays through the same core. It refuses with 'needs_copy' ('move it back with Move to') when the way back would itself need a fresh copy.
- **bodies (refiled for cross-project, moved_out/moved_in dropped) vs moves (moved_out/moved_in for cross-project, refiled within a project) vs NAS (catalogued) (item c)**: The two areas crossed their vocabularies, and three migrations each planned to restate the CHECK, the capture and the broadcast.
  - *Settled:* ONE widening in 0116: file_events_event_check DROP + ADD with the 0093 nine (uploaded, downloaded, moved, relinked, trashed, restored, purged, upload_abandoned, viewed_remote) plus catalogued, moved_out, moved_in, refiled = 13. A cross-project change writes moved_out under OLD.project_id and moved_in under NEW.project_id, with old_path and new_path NULL and details '{}', never NULL. A folder change within one project writes refiled {from_folder_id, to_folder_id}. 'moved' fires only for a locator change with the project unchanged. An insert under the 'check:' marker writes catalogued. A purge certificate of an office-server row carries details.body 'company server bin' or 'left on the company server'. Local and office-server deletes reuse trashed, restored and purged, so no new term is needed. fn_file_events_capture is restated ONCE in 0116 from its latest body (the build greps for the latest; the designs say 0074). fn_realtime_broadcast is restated in 0109 (the repairs' generic per-side split) and once more in 0116 to add the NAS 'check:' early return. The moves area's own files split is dropped.
- **bodies files_storage_path_key_uidx vs NAS files_network_share_locator_key (item d)**: Bodies' unique index covers every trash state for key providers; NAS's covers live rows only, so a new file can be listed at a deleted file's old name.
  - *Settled:* Both exist, in disjoint namespaces. files_storage_path_key_uidx is UNIQUE (storage_path text_pattern_ops) WHERE storage_provider IN (supabase, s3, local_server), every trash state (0114). files_s3_namespace_uidx normalises '.jpg' for s3. files_network_share_locator_key is UNIQUE (workspace_id, lower(storage_path)) WHERE storage_provider='network_share' AND deleted_at IS NULL (0117, after the enum commits). files_network_share_namespace_chk ((provider = network_share) = (left(path,4) = 'nas:')) keeps a 'nas:' path out of the bodies index and a 'projects/' key out of the NAS one. 0114's preflight refuses any non-key-provider row whose path starts 'projects/'; 0117's refuses any 'nas:%' row before the CHECK. Bodies' storage-claim helpers never consider network_share rows.
- **personal (own trg_projects_personal_guard, folder_slug 'Personal_<hex>') vs repairs (one owner guard) vs bodies (separate id-reuse guard) vs NAS (separate folder-nesting guard, '_WILSON_PERSONAL/<uuid>' folder) vs tables (expects fn_projects_identity_guard) (item e)**: Up to four BEFORE triggers on projects with overlapping rules, and two different office-server folder names for a personal space.
  - *Settled:* ONE projects guard, fn_projects_owner_guard with trg_projects_owner_guard, created in 0107 and restated from its latest body in 0110 (personal arms), 0114 (id-reuse arm) and 0117 (office-server folder claims and folder_slug rules). Each post-condition names every earlier arm. The name sorts before trg_projects_populate_workspace, so it reads COALESCE(NEW.workspace_id, current_workspace_id()). 0110's personal arms: personal_owner_id is immutable; on a personal row, is_private, created_by, deleted_at, folder_slug and folder_root never change, for admins too. The tables area's preflight names fn_projects_owner_guard. The office-server personal folder is NAS's '_WILSON_PERSONAL/<owner uuid>', computed only by network_share_space_folder. ensure_personal_space sets no folder_slug, and the desktop add route checks that locator against the signed-in user. Ordering: the repairs' workspace broadcast suppression, the project_members_select hop and the edit_history arm (0107 to 0109) all land before 0110, so the first ensure_personal_space INSERT is never broadcast, its seat is never visible to members, and its history row is creator-and-admin only (captured_private).
- **tables (moth_history WHEN lists, classifier names) vs moves vs NAS (item f)**: History capture needs the move's single UPDATE, the NAS columns and the classifier helpers to exist. It expected moth_file_row_locked from the personal area under a different guard name.
  - *Settled:* moth_history is the last migration (0119); its preflight requires projects.personal_owner_id, fn_projects_owner_guard, _moth_target_eligible, moth_can_read_target, moth_file_row_locked, moth_folder_path_locked, trg_folders_lock_guard (all 0110/0111), the composite FKs (0113), files.share_missing_since (0114) and the 0116 vocabulary. The move core changes project_id, folder_id, the links and the locator in ONE UPDATE, so history writes exactly one 'move' row. A copy-up's locator switch is in that same row, and the projection never stores storage_path. A rekey is skipped as non-structural. Office-server catalogue inserts are captured like any files row ('missing' boolean). Capture ignores every marker. _moth_target_eligible's file arm calls moth_file_row_locked, so history redaction and Moth's live gate cannot drift. moth_local_scopes and file_body_locations are not created.
- **NAS (server-first trash, bin '<space>/.wilson-bin/<id>/<leaf>', certificate-driven purge) vs moves MV-11 (bin '<project folder>/.wilson-bin/<id>-<leaf>' plus sidecar and sweep) vs trash (moth_trash_files over soft_delete_row) (item g)**: Two bin layouts, two purge mechanisms, and the generic trash path could not change an office-server row under NAS's guard.
  - *Settled:* Her answer 5 as ONE flow. On the desktop, after the confirmation dialog, moth_trash_files (or R.A.B.B.I.T.'s deleteFile) routes network_share rows to network_share_trash. That trashes the row through soft_delete_row under the 'op:<id>' marker (fn_trash_authz unchanged), sets share_bin_path = <space folder>/.wilson-bin/<file id>/<leaf> (bin_ok shape) and writes a pending 'trash' op. The desktop renames the real file there (same share, so the rename never crosses volumes), checks it, and calls network_share_settle('done'). If the rename fails, settle 'not_moved' restores the row and the person is told why. Undo and Restore: network_share_restore (refused 'occupied' if a live row holds the name), then the rename back, then settle. At 30 days the existing purge_soft_deleted removes the row and writes the 'purged' certificate (body 'company server bin'). The next catalogue apply returns that id in purge_ok, and the desktop removes the bin folder. Sidecars and sweep-bin are dropped. moth_trash_index lists these rows with needs_desktop = true. In a browser, Delete and Restore are GatedActions with the sentence 'Do this from the WILSON desktop app'. pending_mine() finishes any interrupted operation at sign-in.
- **tables (one table this session) vs NAS (two tables) vs moves (journal table) vs the plan (all section 4 schemas) (item h)**: Which tables Session 1 creates, and the RLS_TABLES count.
  - *Settled:* Session 1 creates file_moves (0113, suite 114_file_moves.sql), network_share_checks (0117, 116_network_share_checks.sql), network_share_ops (0117, 117_network_share_ops.sql) and moth_history (0119, 121_moth_history.sql, ENABLE without FORCE as the edit_history exception; edit_history is already in RLS_TABLES, so moth_history is listed too). RLS_TABLES is MEASURED at 61 in .github/workflows/rls.yml, so it becomes 65. Each RLS_TABLES line lands in the same commit as its suite. Not created: moth_views, moth_layout_positions, moth_prefs (Session 2); moth_tag_types, moth_tags, moth_tag_assignments, moth_tag_nominations, moth_relationships, workspace_notes (Session 3); boards, board_elements, sticky_locks (Session 4); moth_local_scopes and file_body_locations (never).
- **moves SEC-MV-07 (extra edit_history_select clause on diff old project) vs repairs R8 (cross-project rows stamped NULL)**: Two mechanisms for the old side of a cross-project files diff.
  - *Settled:* The repairs mechanism alone (0108): fn_edit_history_money_snapshot sets project_id NULL when an update's diff carries project_id, so the row is admins only. The moves clause and its preflight check are dropped. The moves preflight checks 0108's arm instead.
- **moves (new canOnProject action 'project.moth.write') vs personal CL-1 (no new action)**: Whether Moth adds a permission action.
  - *Settled:* No new action. canOnProject gains ctx.isPersonal and ctx.isPersonalOwner. Moth's Move to, Delete and Restore use 'project.entity.write', with ctx built from moth_islands rows (my_project_role, is_staffed, is_personal, is_mine) and ready kept false until they settle. writeGate.test.js scans the Moth folder.
- **personal (src/tools/moth_v0.1.0/) vs moves (src/tools/moth/)**: Moth tool folder name.
  - *Settled:* src/tools/moth_v0.1.0/, following the measured convention (rabbit_v0.1.0, otter_v0.3.1, deck-outline-generator_v0.514). Every source scan and writeGate assertion uses it.
- **moves cron 04:41, NAS 04:50, tables 04:45**: Cron slots.
  - *Settled:* MEASURED taken: 04:39, 04:43, 04:47, 04:51, 04:55, 04:59, plus hourly :39 and */5. Assigned: wilson-purge-file-moves 04:41, wilson-purge-moth-history 04:45, wilson-purge-network-share-ops 04:50.
- **repairs fn_same_project_refs_guard (skips under wilson.checked_move) vs moves journal marker**: The setting name the repairs guard skips on.
  - *Settled:* There is no skip. The move clears every entity link on a cross-project move and validates the destination folder, so all references stay same-project and the guard passes on its own. The guard keeps its fixed map, and the composite FKs (0113) duplicate the folder entries harmlessly. No 'wilson.checked_move' setting exists anywhere.
- **bodies SB-21 pending-move record vs moves MV-13 local journal**: Two desktop journals for the same crash windows.
  - *Settled:* One journal: <rabbit-data>/moves/<op>.json, written with writeJsonAtomic durable:true. It covers moves between local projects (roll forward if the destination bundle holds the rows, else roll back) and retiring a copied-up local original after the cloud commit. Office-server renames are journalled on the server (network_share_ops plus pending_mine) and are not in the local journal.

### 1.3 Decisions

- **I-1** Migration order is 0107 to 0120, with the privacy repairs (0107 to 0109) first. Each later migration's preflight names exactly what it depends on and refuses without it.
  - *Why:* Her ruling that nothing appears that a person cannot already see. The personal area's P0: without 0107 to 0109, the first provisioning is broadcast and its seat is readable by members.
- **I-2** One bound-caller test everywhere: COALESCE(current_setting('role',true),'') IN ('authenticated','anon'). It is used by the owner guard, the same-project guard and the files guard's definer arm.
  - *Why:* 0049 already reads the role GUC in a trigger. session_user is postgres under pgTAP, so a session_user test would make the planted-definer probe vacuous.
- **I-3** The move marker is wilson.file_move = <file_moves.id written in this txid>, checked only through fn_checked_move_admits. The rekey marker is wilson.body_rekey = 'file:<id>'. The NAS marker is wilson.network_share_writer = 'check:<pid>' | 'op:<fid>'. Each is reset to '' before return.
  - *Why:* Binding each marker to an id makes any unknown or future definer fail closed (bodies SEC-11, moves SEC-MV-11, NAS NS-SEC-09).
- **I-4** Body keys are minted by the server only, under the destination's prefix with third segment 'files'. No client-chosen key is ever trusted, even after validation.
  - *Why:* Never trust caller-supplied kind or id pairs. It also keeps a hidden project's id from reaching the destination through the key (bodies SEC-6).
- **I-5** Office-server operations are server-first and journalled (network_share_ops). The row changes first, then the desktop moves the real file, then settle. The catalogue skips locators named by pending operations.
  - *Why:* A crash never duplicates or loses a row, and a walk during the window cannot list the file twice.
- **I-6** Moves between two desktop-only projects are a desktop route (copy or rename, size and sha1 check, both bundles written, read back) with the local journal. Managed (entity-bound) local files do not move between projects. Local-to-cloud and cloud-to-local moves are refused.
  - *Why:* Her answer 2. Managed files take their folder from their entity, so a cross-project move would have no home for the body.
- **I-7** s3 moves between projects stay off (rabbit_private.s3_body_moves_on() returns false) until 0120 is applied on an environment after its storage-presign is verified deployed. Everything else in Session 1 works on Petal and office-server companies.
  - *Why:* Bodies SEC-4: the deployed storage-presign authorises by key prefix, and a database cannot see what has been deployed.
- **I-8** An admin reaches another person's personal space only from the Files page. It is read-only, and every selection writes app_events 'admin' WIL-4114 through record_personal_space_opened. Moth's islands, the move picker, the trash and history never show another person's space, admins included.
  - *Why:* Her standing ruling and answer 4.
- **I-9** The Moth page is a lazy chunk. App.jsx imports it only through React.lazy(() => import('./tools/moth_v0.1.0')) behind a flag set once currentPage === 'moth'. No other file imports the Moth folder statically, which a vitest pins.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: every page is mounted at boot and nothing in production is lazy yet, so a lazy component inside PageSurface would still load at boot.
- **I-10** Written before her answers in section 0, which win where they differ. Office-server checks are run only by the people who manage a project's team (or the owner of a personal space). A leaver's personal files stay read-only to admins. The storage figures members see are not changed.
  - *Why:* These are the narrowest safe defaults. Each is listed under questions.

### 1.4 The migrations, in order

| # | File | Suite | RLS_TABLES | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0107 | `0107_private_project_write_hops.sql` | 110_private_project_write_hops.sql (new, table-less). Same commit: 83 probe 20 retargeted to lives_ok with a new control; 90 probes 7 and 9; 95 probes 11 and 13. | none | 0106 |

#### 0107 — `0107_private_project_write_hops.sql`

Repairs A. Preflight (0072, 0082, 0092 present). projects.topic_epoch integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 with CHECK >= 0. fn_projects_owner_guard with trg_projects_owner_guard BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON projects (DEFINER, search_path pinned, REVOKEd): created_by and topic_epoch pinned for bound callers; is_private changes by the creator or an admin only; producer_id set to non-NULL needs can_manage_project_roster(OLD.id); topic_epoch +1 on any narrowing, for every caller. projects_update restated from 0013 with the privacy arm. The project hop on every client write clause of tasks, assets, comments (through fn_comment_project_id), folders, scenes, shots, levels, experiences, project_members (select too), files update and delete (restated from 0092), milestones update and delete, task_templates (select too). task_deps_update and phase_deps_update gain a WITH CHECK covering both endpoints. fn_same_project_refs_guard and its triggers on tasks, assets, phases, milestones, shots, folders, files, task_dependencies and phase_dependencies, using a fixed map derived from pg_constraint in rehearsal; only new or changed references are checked, with one 23503 sentence. Post-conditions: catalogue scans S1 to S5 over the current schema (edit_history excepted).

*Constraints:* One transaction, no enum. The rehearsal on wilson-dev, rolled back, produces and pins the exception, anchor and map lists, and records a count of existing cross-project references (not fixed). The WHOLE pgTAP set runs, collected = planned. Applied LF, file by file, with the schema_migrations md5. Re-run after any replay of 0013, 0020, 0040, 0041, 0044, 0061 or 0082.

*Measured on wilson-dev before writing it (2026-11-02, read-only; each query named its server and read 4 workspaces):* (1) **the link map**, from `pg_constraint`: 27 foreign keys from tasks, assets, phases, milestones, shots, folders, files, task_dependencies and phase_dependencies into project-scoped tables. Two already force the same project (`tasks(project_id, scene_id)` → scenes, `tasks(project_id, shot_id)` → shots); the other 25 are single-column and are the guard's map: assets(phase_id → phases, task_template_id → task_templates), files(asset_id, experience_id, folder_id, level_id, phase_id, scene_id, shot_id, task_id), folders(asset_id, experience_id, level_id, parent_id, scene_id, shot_id), milestones(phase_id), phases(parent_phase_id), shots(scene_id), tasks(asset_id, phase_id), task_dependencies(predecessor_id, successor_id), phase_dependencies(predecessor_id, successor_id). (2) **Existing references that cross projects: 0 of 160** across those 23 link kinds (task_templates counted only where the template has a project), so the guard meets no old row on dev; staging and production are counted at their own rehearsal. (3) **The policies it restates exist exactly as four each**: `<table>_select/_insert/_update/_delete` on projects, tasks, assets, comments, folders, scenes, shots, levels, experiences, project_members, files, milestones and task_templates, and `task_deps_*` and `phase_deps_*` on the two dependency tables, 60 in all, every one PERMISSIVE. (4) **Scan S2 run on today's schema** (every permissive clause on a client-privileged project-scoped table that neither hops to projects, nor hops through its anchor parent, nor calls a gate allowed for that table only): **48 clauses fail**: tasks, assets, folders, scenes, shots, levels, experiences and comments (insert check, update using and check, delete using: 32), project_members (the same four and its select: 5), task_templates (the same and its select: 5), files and milestones (update using, delete using: 4), and two that 0107 leaves: `edit_history_select` (0108 gives it the arm) and `upload_reservations_select` (an own-row arm, `created_by = auth.uid()`: the pinned exception). So 0107 fixes 46, and its post-condition's exception list is those two. A first draft of the scan let `can_access_project_money(project_id)` count as a gate on every table and so missed files' two clauses: the gate exemptions are per table, as R3 says.

*As built and applied on wilson-dev (2026-11-02; md5 `a9c68e8598c17e937b46389135df65c0`; suite 110, 45 checks):* four things differ from R2 to R6, each measured. (a) The guard's map has two links R6 did not list, both found by the full `pg_constraint` scan: `assets.task_template_id` (a workspace template, project_id NULL, also passes) and `budget_actuals.line_id` (the trigger is on budget_actuals too, so ten tables carry it). (b) 🚨 **The guard judges only a row whose project the caller can see** (for budget_actuals, whose money they may read) and leaves every other refusal to RLS. BEFORE triggers run before RLS, so the draft answered "another project" for a wrong id and RLS's refusal for a right one, an existence oracle over hidden ids; suite 44 probe 10 found it in the whole-set rehearsal, and suite 110's probe 15 pins the fix (plant P8 bites). (c) 0092 joins the re-run list (it defines files_update and files_delete). (d) Four shipped suites change with it: 83 probes 20 and 24 (the column-free UPDATE now runs and never reaches the private file), 54 probe 20 (the composite FK now exercised as postgres, since the hop refuses a client first), 90 probes 7 and 9 and 95 probes 11 and 13 (their whole-text pins gain the hop). The whole set with 0107 prepended: 104 suites, collected = planned in every one, all green but suite 74's #34 and #35, which fail the same way on dev without 0107 (dev's own auth_events rows). Seven planted breakages, each bites: P1 (no hop on the seven tables) and P5 (no files trigger) are refused by the post-conditions; P2 (no privacy arm on projects_update), P3 (no producer rule), P4 (no epoch bump), P7 (no dependency check) and P8 (no visibility pre-check) turn suite 110 red. A first suite draft named its intruder rows by id, so the SELECT policies (which already hopped) refused them and the USING hops were never reached: P2 found it, and the probes are blanket writes with a public-row control.

#### 0108 — `0108_private_project_money_and_history.sql`

Repairs B. can_access_project_money restated from 0037 with AND passes_project_privacy(p_project); the COMMENTs on it and on passes_project_privacy are restated. New column edit_history.captured_private. edit_history_project_of and edit_history_project_at (DEFINER, revoked from clients). fn_edit_history_capture restated from 0061 with both sides resolved: a cross-project or split-endpoint change gets NULL, and topic_epoch joins the noise list. fn_edit_history_money_snapshot restated from 0092 to NULL the project on a diff that carries project_id. A backfill through the time-walk. edit_history_select restated from 0092 with the admin-or-topic-reader arm and the captured_private creator arm.

*Constraints:* Preflight requires 0107 and 0092's Legal objects. Backfill row counts are recorded before and after. 0092 is not re-runnable after this (its post-conditions 10 and 12), which is recorded.

*As built (2026-11-02, a cloud session; md5 `89a1b2647799d13a59eb4853a729ab2d`; suite 111, 58 checks; applied on wilson-dev by the house method on 2026-11-02 by a desktop session: 135 history rows, the whole file 483 ms in the rehearsal, `project_id` NULL 119 before and 30 after (rate cards' rows), md5 read back, suite 111 58/58 on the applied schema, the whole set with 0108 prepended 105 suites and 3,713 checks, collected = planned, all green but suite 74's #34 and #35 and suite 25's #13, which fail the same way on dev without it):* rehearsed on a local stack built from 0000 by the CLI CI pins (2.119.0): the whole set with 0108, 105 suites, 3,713 checks, green; CI green on each form. Two adversarial review rounds (reviewers, then a skeptic per finding): round 1 found twelve, all taken; round 2 confirmed six (all minor) and refuted two as outside 0108 (below); seven things now differ from R7 and R8, each measured. (a) 🚨 **A project deleted for good leaves its history to the admins**: `trg_projects_edit_history_gone` (statement-level, transition table) NULLs the rows written before the delete, and the capture and the files snapshot NULL a project that no longer exists (the cascade's own rows are written after the statement trigger, measured). Without it, whoever created the id again (`projects.id` is the client's to send) read the old project's private-era rows through the creator arm and its invoices' history through the money gate. A project whose id is changed is treated the same (`trg_projects_edit_history_rekeyed`, statement-level on UPDATE: transition tables cannot ride a column list). A comment, which has no foreign key to its subject, whose subject was deleted after the comment was first written belongs to no project even if the subject's id comes back (the capture and the walk, from the history, which the client cannot forge). Both triggers are created after the backfill with CREATE OR REPLACE: a DROP TRIGGER on projects would have blocked every read of projects for the whole backfill. A row with no project is always `captured_private` true, on every path. (b) `captured_private` is **DEFAULT true**, not false: a writer that leaves it out (the capture of a write blocked behind the migration's lock resumes with 0061's column list) fails closed. (c) A third walk function, `edit_history_value_at` (a column's value at a moment), beside `edit_history_project_at`, which gained `p_as_parent`: a child whose parent was already gone at that moment is NULL, as the live capture (a cascade's parent delete row is written first, measured). One entity's rows are ordered by history id, not by `created_at` (each transaction's start); the walk's last step reads the latest row at or before (a delete's old, an update's new, a create's new), never the create row alone, which handed a moved-then-deleted entity back to its first project. (d) The backfill is a revoked function, `edit_history_restamp()`, so suite 111 can run it over crafted pre-0108 history: every row is re-derived, files included (0088 stamped a reused file id's old rows from the live row); rows of a deleted project, and of an earlier holder of a reused project id (before that project's own latest create row), are NULL; a projects create or delete row's flag comes from its own diff. Re-running it changes nothing. (e) Two indexes, `idx_edit_history_entity_walk (entity_type, entity_id, id)` and `idx_edit_history_project`: without the first, the walk scanned the table once per row. A re-point of a child inside one project keeps its project in the backfill as in the capture (both sides through the walk). **The backfill costs about 0.13 ms per history row while `edit_history` is locked** (214,022 synthetic rows: 28 s, with an `OFFSET 0` fence so the walk runs once per row); each environment's rehearsal records its count and time, and an apply above 30 s goes in a quiet window. (f) `edit_history.project_id`'s COMMENT is restated (the design said so; the first form missed it). (g) Post-condition 7 checks what it says: no move names a project, rate cards carry none, no row names a deleted project or an earlier holder, no files create or delete row names a project other than its own diff's, no private project's create or delete row is stamped not private. Twenty-six planted breakages from the final source (sha1 `46ca49f49d5f`): 24 turn suite 111 red, 8 of them also refused by the post-conditions; DEFAULT false is refused by post-condition 2 on a fresh stack; the projects delete row's own-diff rule is redundant behind (a) and does not bite (kept as defence). **Refuted as outside 0108, and owed to 0110** (which restates `fn_projects_owner_guard`): a deleted project's id created again in the same workspace also re-opens its leftover money objects by path, its `file_events` and its live orphan comments, all of which predate 0108; the root fix is the guard refusing a client INSERT of a project id that already has history (`edit_history`, `file_events` or comments naming it) in this workspace, not only in another (SB-18). Suite 95 probes 17 and 167 follow the intended change; suite 110's S2 drops `edit_history_select`'s pinned exception.

#### 0109 — `0109_private_project_channels.sql`

Repairs C. project_topic (DEFINER) and project_topic_matches (INVOKER). The 0016 realtime.messages read and presence policies restated with project_topic_matches, inside a guarded DO. fn_workspace_realtime_broadcast restated from 0018: each side is judged fail-closed first, a payload-free AUDIENCE goes out on a visibility flip, one-sided changes are downgraded to INSERT or DELETE shape, and the filters apply only to the sides sent. fn_realtime_broadcast restated from 0098 (md5 preflight): per-side projects, a DELETE(OLD) to the old topic and an INSERT(NEW) to the new one when the project changes, the epoch topic, and the files money test first. fn_budget_versions_broadcast restated from 0101 with project_topic.

*Constraints:* Channel behaviour probes are vacuous on CI. The rolled-back wilson-dev rehearsal, with its planted-control results, is mandatory and recorded, and the join cache is measured there. The client changes ship in the same commit; the client tolerates the old server. 0098 cannot be re-run after this.

*As built and applied on wilson-dev (2026-11-02, a desktop session; md5 `949ba769d256a6327dcbfb686e75f303`; `fn_realtime_broadcast`'s body `cb26163e64f063de4da39c4eb316de4b`; suite 112, 40 checks):* the three bodies were read on dev with `pg_get_functiondef` first and are 0018's, 0098's and 0101's word for word. Six things differ from R9 to R11, each measured. (a) `fn_realtime_broadcast` resolves both sides in ONE CASE (each arm sets `v_project` from `v_row` as 0098 did and `v_po` from OLD): suites 94 #5, 99 #14 and 101 #16 count each quoted table name exactly once in the comment-stripped body, and a first form with a second CASE for OLD failed all three in the whole-set rehearsal; for the same reason the projects arm is a flag (`v_self`) and its AUDIENCE payload names the table through `TG_TABLE_NAME`. Suite 95 probe 170's files arm is unchanged text. (b) `project_topic_matches` also requires the `rabbit` and `project` parts. (c) The three broadcast definers' EXECUTE is revoked again; the workspace broadcast sends nothing for a table outside its five. (d) 🚨 **The client's epoch waits for 0109, not 0107** (review round 1, the one major finding): 0107 adds `topic_epoch` and bumps it while the triggers still send to the plain topic, so the adapter probes 0109 itself (an RPC to `project_topic_matches`; PGRST202 means absent) before `listProjects` reads the epoch; on 0107 without 0109 every channel joins epoch 0. (e) The provider's revalidation is answered by the first list landed from a read started after it was asked (`revalidateFromSeqRef`), compares the project open when that read started, and retires an in-flight load when it closes; ProjectsPage reads the seat only when the roster is the open project's (review round 1). (f) The Producer hint says why the select is disabled. Rehearsal on dev, rolled back: suite 112 40/40 (realtime.messages present, rows landing); five planted breakages: no projects privacy test (suite red: 20, 21, 26-28), AUDIENCE replaced by the row (refused by post-condition 1; without the post-conditions red: 6, 26-28), no child EXISTS (red, 8 probes), no side comparison (refused by post-condition 2; without them red: 4, 34, 35, re-run on the final form), the read policy without the epoch (refused by post-condition 4; without them red: 10, 19, the policy measured as a member reading realtime.messages under a stale and a current topic). The whole set with 0109 prepended: 106 suites, 3,753 planned = collected, green but suite 25's #13 and 74's #34 and #35 (dev's own rows). One review round (two reviewers, a skeptic per finding, her instruction to save allowance): 11 findings, 5 confirmed and taken (all client), 6 refuted (the 3 server ones among them). **Owed:** the join-cache measurement (join, flip, write, observe) needs a signed-in client on a live socket and committed writes on dev, so it was not run; by construction a narrowing moves every write to the new epoch's topic, so it bears only on R15's role-demotion and deactivation residual. Bundle bytes were not measured (the client change is a few hundred lines with tests).

#### 0110 — `0110_personal_space.sql`

projects.personal_owner_id uuid (no FK); projects_personal_shape_chk (personal ⇒ is_private AND created_by = personal_owner_id); partial unique projects_personal_owner_uniq on (workspace_id, personal_owner_id). fn_projects_owner_guard restated from 0107 with the personal arms (owner immutable; on a personal row is_private, created_by, deleted_at, folder_slug and folder_root never change, admins included). projects_insert and projects_delete restated from 0013 plus personal_owner_id IS NULL. projects_update restated from 0107 plus (personal_owner_id IS NULL OR personal_owner_id = auth.uid()). can_write_project, can_comment_project, can_manage_project_roster (0013) and can_edit_shot_lists (0084) restated with the personal arm first (owner for write, comment and shot lists; false for roster); grants re-asserted. fn_project_members_personal_guard (one seat, the owner as manager). ensure_personal_space() (DEFINER, BYPASSRLS-owner preflight, ON CONFLICT on the partial index, no folder_slug). record_personal_space_opened(p_project) (admin only, another person's personal row, app_events 'admin' WIL-4114, context ids only).

*Constraints:* One transaction, no enum. Rehearsed rolled back. 35_platform_audit stays green. The client ships first (probe-guarded). The personal-space suites must run after 0107 to 0109 are applied.

*As built (2026-11-04, a desktop session; file md5 `c8794b769aca23cebda43783532fff40` at `eccd1d2e`; the guard's body `b6b800f4448eb9723f4afab2e6d97ec0`; **applied on wilson-dev 2026-11-04 17:12 UTC** on her yes, after the rolled-back rehearsal below; not on staging or production):* the bodies it restates were read on dev with `pg_get_functiondef` first. Seven things differ from §1.4 and §2.2, each measured. (a) The CHECK is NULL-safe: `created_by = personal_owner_id` alone passes when created_by is NULL (a CHECK accepts NULL), so it reads `created_by IS NOT DISTINCT FROM personal_owner_id`. (b) The id-reuse refusal owed by 0108's review is here, not only 0114's other-company arm: a client INSERT of an id that no row holds and that this company's edit_history, file_events or 'project' comments name raises 42501; a live id still answers 23505, so the migration runner's insertOrSkip is unchanged; it is judged only for a caller projects_insert admits (an active admin or manager here), so it tells nobody else that an id was used. (c) A client never changes a project's id (review round 1: a changed id took a used one over as surely as an INSERT). (d) The seat guard is AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE, so it judges only rows RLS let through (review round 1: as BEFORE it answered for projects the caller cannot see). (e) 🚨 **The four helpers do not reach every write** (review round 1, the major finding): the five money tables are gated by the money gate alone and pinned task templates by `can_write_task_template`, both admitting an admin to any private row, and the money storage writes by the money and Legal gates; so 0110 adds `fn_personal_space_write_guard` (INVOKER, BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE on the six tables: a client writes nothing into someone else's personal space; a project the caller cannot see is left to RLS) and restates 0092's six money storage write clauses with `AND can_write_project(<the path's project>)`, which narrows only a personal space (suite 95 probe 15 follows). Left as designed (CL-1): `set_active_shot_list`, `archive_shot_list` and `archive_edit` keep their admin and manager legs on a personal space. (f) A personal row's deleted_at is pinned by the guard, but an owner's `soft_delete_row` is refused first by `fn_trash_authz` (only admins bin projects) and an admin's by `can_write_project`'s personal arm (P0001): the guard's arm is what stops a plain UPDATE of deleted_at. (g) Suite 111's reused-id probe now has the runner create the id (a client can no longer). Rehearsal on dev (rolled back): the extended suites green (80 47/47, 18, 25, 84, 110, 01, 95, 111); ten planted breakages, each bites (no unique index, no personal arm, no seat guard, 0013's projects_insert, an admin leg in the personal arm, no id-reuse arm, no personal pins, no money write guard, no storage write gate, no id pin); the whole set with it prepended 106 suites, 3,807 planned = collected, green but suite 74's #34 and #35 (dev's own rows). One review round (two reviewers, a skeptic per finding): 12 findings, 4 confirmed and taken, 8 refuted. Applied on dev: both md5s read back, no row has a `personal_owner_id` yet, the whole set on the applied schema the same as the rehearsal. *The client's half (2026-11-04, the same session; no database change):* §1.9's personal-space items are built: listProjects' filter, projectIsPrivate, `src/cloud/personalSpace.js`, both merge sites, Team, Takeout, the Files entries and their record call, canOnProject's personal arm, the four adapters' methods, `missingFunction`, WIL-4114 in errorCodes, and the signed-out desktop's space (`electron/personalSpace.cjs`). Nine things differ from §1.9, §2.2 and §1.8. (1) `personalSpacesAvailable` answers true, false or null (not now), and the readers that must keep spaces out fail closed on null: listProjects refuses, Team reads nothing. Answering false for a network blip would have listed everyone's spaces to an admin for one read. (2) Takeout reads the personal set itself rather than through the probe: 42703 means none, and any other failure stops the takeout. (3) POST and PATCH strip `personal` and `personal_owner_id` only, not folder_slug, folder_root and files_dir as §1.8 and DT-1 say: an ordinary project needs those three, and both routes already guard them (S35, S40). On the personal id itself PATCH is 409, and so is a POST naming it (which would have written over the bundle). (4) The desktop's guards are mounted ahead of the project routes from `electron/personalSpace.cjs`, not written into their bodies: several tests lift those bodies, and the guards are tested on a real Express app there. relink-apply is refused as well, or a relink would point the space's bodies elsewhere. (5) A bundle that exists and cannot be read is a 409, never written over. (6) `project-patch` no longer merges a private row either, as §2.2 PS-8 says; it re-reads the list, as the workspace path does since 0109. Only a personal row is deleted from the list at once: deleting a private row would flicker the project someone has open. (7) The owner is told 'A personal space has one seat, its owner's.' about the roster, not §2.2 CL-1's sentence, which is false of them. (8) PSA-10's canSeeLegal is unchanged, since it reads only Add as Legal, a tab control a personal space never shows; the file window's canSeeMoney widens to the owner. (9) Files notes the record on screen for the admin ('Opening it is noted in Logs, where other admins see it.'), which her answer 4 implies and §2.2 does not ask for. Eleven planted breakages, each bites (the admin short-circuit ahead of the personal arm, listing before the record, an admin writing another's space, listProjects without the filter, the old projectIsPrivate, both merge sites merging a personal row, Takeout keeping every row, Takeout guessing none on a failure, Team drawing a personal seat, the desktop letting the row change, the migration saying nothing). Seen in the running app on the dev fixtures: the picker's entries, an admin's read-only view with the sentence and greyed notes, the owner's own space, and Team with no personal seat. Not reviewed by a separate round: the session's two rounds at the end cover it. Not in this half, each with its owner: `ensurePersonalSpace` has no caller until the Moth page (§1.10), so no space exists yet on any environment; the office-server add route (PS-7) goes with `electron/networkShare.cjs` and 0117; the Moth read methods and the MATRIX lines (CL-2) go with 0111; the rule that a personal project takes no local_server or google_drive body (PS-6) is 0114's, folded into fn_files_body_guard (§1.5), so until then only the client routes it; CHANGELOG's Added line and RELEASE_TESTING's personal-space check wait for the Moth page, since nothing can be seen until then.

#### 0111 — `0111_moth_read_path.sql`

expenses_file_ids_gin. moth_folder_path_locked(text) IMMUTABLE. fn_folders_lock_guard with trg_folders_lock_guard BEFORE INSERT OR DELETE OR UPDATE OF path, slug, parent_id, project_id, id ON folders: refuses a change of locked status, a change of a folder's project or id, and a non-root folder whose first path segment differs from its parent's. *As built (2026-11-04, desktop sessions; file md5 `69b3f148bc77d6af241115bc008d8072`, changed by the review round of 0112 and 0113; suite 113, 290 checks; **applied on wilson-dev 2026-11-04 21:47 UTC**, on her word):* the bodies it calls were read on dev first. Seven things differ from §1.4, §1.6 and §2.2, each measured. (a) 🚨 **`moth_file_row_locked` takes the row's project: `(p_workspace, p_project, p_file, p_flag, p_path, p_tags, p_folder)`**, seven arguments, and its receipt arm is `e.project_id = p_project` with no read of `files` (review round 1, the one major finding): as designed the arm joined the live row, so 0119's AFTER DELETE capture of an unflagged receipt would have read nothing, answered "not locked", and written the receipt's name into history. **0119 passes OLD's and NEW's project, and its preflight names this signature.** CL-2's `e.project_id = f.project_id` reads `e.project_id = p_project`. (b) A third helper, `moth_folder_chain_locked(p_workspace, p_folder)`, so the walk has one body for the folder arm and the classifier. A chain that cannot be walked to its end (deeper than 64 steps, or a loop) answers locked. (c) `fn_folders_lock_guard` is INVOKER: a BEFORE trigger runs ahead of RLS, so a definer would answer "wrong first segment" about a parent the caller cannot see; as the caller a hidden parent reads as no row, and the same-project guard answers as it does for a random uuid (suite 113 pins the three answers equal). A top-level folder's money slug is part of its standing, so a renamed category's slug cannot be changed away. (d) The guard also refuses a client's delete of a money or Legal folder or of a project's root (review round 1), since a delete nulls `folder_id` for every file filed there and the walk then has nothing to read; a cascade passes (`pg_trigger_depth()`), so a project's own delete still works. No shipped writer deletes one (`deleteFolder` has no caller). **Still open until 0114:** a client can clear or change `files.folder_id` by a plain patch, which 0114's arm 1 closes; pin it in suite 115. (e) The per-row helpers are plpgsql, not SQL: a non-inlined SQL function is planned per call site, and the page's gate sits behind a fence (`OFFSET 0`), because the planner otherwise ran it over every row of the project before its sort. Measured on dev, rolled back, 3,000 files in 400 folders four deep: `moth_islands` 4 ms, `moth_project_folders` (302 rows) 144 ms, `moth_project_files` first page of 500 475 ms, a page of 1,000 770 ms, a later page of 500 303 ms (a page of 500 took 3.4 s before these two changes). (f) The three reads return enums as text; `moth_project_folders` gives `parent_id` in the positive form too. (g) The client: `moneyFolderChainLocked` lives in `storage/index.js` beside `isMoneyKey`, so the fixtures can use it (it imports nothing; `devFixtures.test.js` allows it). The desktop's `listMothFolders` leaves money folders out, its reads answer an empty list for a project it no longer holds, `listMothFiles` lists managed files marked `managed: true` (they are ordinary files rows in the cloud), and an unreadable personal bundle costs the space and not the projects. The `src/tools/moth_v0.1.0/` lane is in `typeScale.test.js`. (h) The review round of 0112 and 0113 added one arm to the guard: a client's write never changes a folder's `project_id` or `id`, with one fixed answer whatever the folder holds. A foreign key answers past RLS, so left to the keys that write says whether a file the caller cannot see is filed there. An `id` change already said so before 0113, through the old key. No shipped writer changes either, and resending the same values passes. Rehearsal: suite 113 290/290; the whole set with 0111 and 0113 prepended 108 suites, 4,149 planned = collected, green but suite 74's #34 and #35 (dev's own rows). 43 planted breakages: 42 bite (some also refused by the post-conditions; the fence by the post-condition alone, since it changes time and not answers); one does not, by construction (dropping `n.kind <> 'root'` from the walk: a root's path is empty by CHECK and it has no parent, so neither money arm can fire on it). Run twice in one rolled-back transaction, it completes. One review round on **Fable 5.1** at high effort, by her ruling (three reviewers, one checker per finding, 11 agents): 8 findings, 8 kept, all taken; the 0113 fixture one is the DROP CONSTRAINT line below.

#### 0112 — `0112_storage_provider_network_share.sql`

ALTER TYPE public.storage_provider ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS 'network_share'; plus a pg_enum post-condition. Nothing else.

*Constraints:* Its own migration and its own transaction. Nothing uses the value in that transaction. Every guard written before 0117 compares the provider as text.

*As built (2026-11-04; committed, rehearsed rolled back on dev, labels 4 before and 5 inside the rehearsal; reviewed with 0113; **applied and committed alone on wilson-dev 2026-11-04 21:48 UTC**):* exactly as above. It must be applied and committed alone before 0114 to 0117 are rehearsed.

#### 0113 — `0113_file_moves.sql`

folders UNIQUE (id, project_id). folders.parent_id FK replaced by a composite (parent_id, project_id) → folders(id, project_id) ON DELETE CASCADE. files.folder_id FK (0043, found by catalogue) replaced by a composite (folder_id, project_id) ON DELETE SET NULL (folder_id); both added NOT VALID, then VALIDATE. Table public.file_moves (id, workspace_id, actor_user_id DEFAULT auth.uid(), txid DEFAULT txid_current(), items jsonb array of 1 to 500, undo_of, undone_at, audit columns) with trg_file_moves_audit BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE; ENABLE + FORCE RLS, no policies, REVOKE ALL from client roles. fn_checked_move_admits(file, project, folder, provider, path), DEFINER STABLE, revoked from clients. fn_moth_body_provider_for(ws) (the active provider, so mode 'byos' only and otherwise petal: petal→supabase, s3→s3, network→network_share). purge_file_moves(interval) with cron 'wilson-purge-file-moves' at '41 4 * * *'.

*Constraints:* Rolled-back counts of cross-project parents and folder_id rows on dev and staging; stop if any is non-zero. server_version is measured, since ON DELETE SET NULL (column list) needs PG15 or later. 0043's post-conditions are re-asserted. The replay list and both rlsSuiteLists tail pins move in the same commit.

*As built (2026-11-04; file md5 `31423c4e126cd2d9ef25cb799a91d395`; **applied on wilson-dev 2026-11-04 21:49 UTC**):* measured on dev: PostgreSQL 17.6; cross-project parents 0, cross-project folder_id rows 0; 04:41 free. Three things differ. (a) `fn_checked_move_admits` is plain EXISTS, never NULL. (b) A second index, on `undo_of`, for the FK. (c) Suite 113 drops `files_folder_same_project_fk` inside its own transaction for its two foreign-folder rows, so the read's positive form keeps a behaviour probe (review round 1 of 0111). Suite 114, 48 checks; 05 gains three probes, 52 one; RLS_TABLES 62. The whole set with 0111 and 0113 prepended 108 suites, 4,149 planned = collected, green but suite 74's #34 and #35 (dev's own rows). Twenty-two planted breakages: 21 bite. The one that does not is the preflight's count, which needs a cross-project row that 0107's guard and this file's keys forbid. **One review round on Opus 5.5** at high effort, by her ruling *"Opus for reviews from now"* (three reviewers, one checker per finding, 12 agents, 12 minutes): 9 findings, 6 distinct, all kept, all taken. (1) `fn_moth_body_provider_for` read only the provider, so a company back on Petal, whose row keeps its old provider as a remembered and inert setting, answered its old bucket or share; it now reads the mode. Latent until 0118, and the result would have been a refused copy-up rather than a leak. (2) The file said it was re-runnable and a second run stopped at its first key statement (measured on dev, rolled back): the composite keys now drop first, and run twice in one rolled-back transaction it completes. (3) The key's refusal said whether a hidden file was filed in a folder: closed in 0111's guard, note (h). (4) Suite 05's resend probe ran on a row whose folder was already cleared, so it never reached the key: it now runs on a filed row and checks the row. (5) Suite 114's purge probe counted the whole table, so a real journal row past 30 days would have turned it red: it now names its rows. (6) A client can write `storage_provider = 'network_share'` between 0112's commit and 0114; nothing reads it as a location, but such a row would stop 0117 or be left for an operator: **0114's and 0117's preflights refuse any such row, by count.** "A planted definer that moves a row with no journal" (§1.11) belongs to suite 115: nothing calls the admission until 0114.

#### 0114 — `0114_stable_file_bodies.sql`

Preflight (refuses under RLS; LOCK files; the refusing counts and NOTICE counts of bodies' preflight). CREATE SCHEMA rabbit_private (USAGE to authenticated and service_role only; never exposed). New columns files.share_missing_since and files.share_bin_path (nullable, written only by the NAS writers). Indexes files_storage_path_key_uidx, files_s3_namespace_uidx, files_moved_body_ws_idx, storage_gc_queue_claim_idx, file_events_purged_path_idx and file_events_project_any_idx. rabbit_private.storage_object_claimed, storage_object_current_project (INVOKER), file_key_admissible, files_body_follow, files_body_retire, s3_body_moves_on (false) and project_id_tied_elsewhere; every definer has row_security off and an owner with rolbypassrls asserted. The ONE fn_files_body_guard with trg_files_body_guard (arms as in conflicts_resolved). rekey_file_body (sets and resets wilson.body_rekey='file:<id>'). fn_projects_owner_guard restated from 0110 with the id-reuse arm (bound INSERT whose id has no projects row and is tied to another workspace raises 42501).

*Constraints:* One transaction, no enum use (provider compared as ::text). Rolled-back rehearsal of the migration plus 115 and every re-pointed probe, collected = planned. The adapter changes (updateFile sanitise, rewriteFileRow and moveReceipt through rekey_file_body, runMigration refused-upload handling and locked-folder filing) ship in the same release.

*As built (2026-11-04; file md5 `fadc97846fe7cf6de10b169f7f48e137` at `20547f2f`; suite 115, 448 checks; **applied on wilson-dev 2026-11-05 01:49 UTC**, on her word):* measured on dev first: 16 files, all on Petal; third segments assets, invoices, levels, project and shots; every refusing count zero; no non-money row in a locked folder; no open queue row naming a shape no row names. What differs from §1.4 and §2.1:

- (a) `storage_object_current_project` is plpgsql, not SQL, because a storage policy calls it once per object (0111's trap). Its s3 arm reaches its row through the key index (measured with EXPLAIN).
- (b) `project_id_tied_elsewhere` is closed to `authenticated`: `fn_projects_owner_guard` is a definer and calls it as its owner. It has a third source beyond SEC-1's two: any stored copy under the id in Petal's two buckets, because a dead project's rate card and manifest name no row and are authorised by their key alone. A false refusal costs a new project.
- (c) `rekey_file_body`'s marker is `'file:<id>'` (§1.2 over SB-8 and SB-10) and admits only the locator columns, never a project or folder change. The picture may stay unchanged, so a re-file can run in two phases, except when money grows. A key change on s3 is refused. A picture-only change on an s3 row passes, since the picture can only be NULL or the key's own `.jpg`.
- (d) An extra helper, `file_key_shape_ok`, IMMUTABLE, for the key's shape alone. `file_key_admissible`'s folder rule is `moth_folder_chain_locked`.
- (e) The preflight also refuses any `network_share` row (the round of 0112 and 0113) and any key or path over 2000 bytes, which an index cannot hold.
- (f) The guard has one arm the design does not: "A file keeps its id", for clients and bound definers, as folders and projects do. Unbound sessions pass. Arm 2 judges changes only: **a definer's INSERT is not judged, so any new definer that inserts into `files` validates the row itself** (0117's `network_share_record_added`).
- (g) Arm 3 covers UPDATE only, so a client could hard-delete an office-server row. No such row can exist before 0117, whose guard owns it.
- (h) `files_body_follow` holds SB-12's third segment to exactly `files`, and clears a blank picture with nothing retired.
- (i) Suite 80 needed no change when measured, though §1.4 names it. Suite 35 now counts `rabbit_private` too. Suite 83's fixture makes its project before its stored copy.
- (j) The client half asks first: `rekeyFileBodyAvailable` asks the database once, so a new build below this file says so, moves nothing, and writes no row for a refused body. So a new build may run before this file, which also meets 0110's client-first constraint; what must not run against this file is an old build's desktop-to-cloud migration (the file's header and the runbook row say why). A receipt whose cloud copy is at neither key goes back up from the computer that holds it.

The whole set with 0114 prepended and on the applied schema: 109 suites, 4,604 planned = collected, 4,602 passed, only suite 74's #34 and #35 red (dev's own rows, 7,825 now). Run twice in one rolled-back transaction it completes. CI all six green at `20547f2f`. Three hundred and eleven planted breakages: 305 bite. Six do not, all redundant clauses: rekey's `NOT FOUND` and `v_ws IS NULL` (later clauses raise the same sentence), its `p_file` to admissible (a changed key never equals its own row), the money-growth money-access clause (admissible or the gate answers first), the picture's `p_to_thumb IS NULL` (three-valued logic skips the raise), and claimed's `p_name IS NULL` (every arm compares false). No suite can pin `FOR UPDATE` and `LOCK TABLE` (they need a second session), the preflight's refusals, or the effect of `row_security = off` (catalogue only). Suite 115 drops and restores one index and flips `s3_body_moves_on` inside its own transaction.

**One review round on Opus 5.5** at high effort (four reviewers, one checker per finding, 21 agents, 25 minutes, 3.47M subagent tokens; all 837 responses read `claude-opus-5-5`): 17 findings, 16 kept (3 confirmed, 13 in a narrower form), 1 refuted; 15 taken, 1 recorded. Taken in the database: (b)'s third source; money cannot grow while its picture stays behind; (e)'s lengths; the blank picture; the s3 index path; the two NOTICE counts §2.1 lists; arm 2's comment. Taken in the client: (j)'s probe; the receipt that is gone; a stranded receipt counted apart, with its own sentence; the next step on `FILES_IN_CLOUD_BIN`; a plain reason for a row binned meanwhile; the absent-function sentence; the source scan sees `upsert`, quoted brackets and a held `.from('files')`. **Recorded for 0115:** a picture named only by `thumbnail_url` (a legacy picture, or a re-file stopped between its phases) is not claimed, so it stays under the prefix rule: either add `f.thumbnail_url = p_name` to the two thumbnails arms with a partial index, or accept it and say so. For later: a purge still leaves a dead project's row-less objects. Its id is now refused, but the objects stay.

#### 0115 — `0115_storage_follows_file.sql`

DROP + CREATE of the 8 base storage policies (rabbit_files_* from 0042, rabbit_thumbnails_* from 0053) with the claimed/current-project CASE; rabbit_files_delete_own gains NOT claimed. can_presign_object(p_path, p_op) (INVOKER). workspace_petal_committed_bytes restated from 0073 with the moved-copies arm. md5 pins on the 8 money policies, the bin-poster policies and petal_storage_quota_insert (unchanged).

*Constraints:* After 0114. EXPLAIN ANALYZE of a 100-object list and a createSignedUrl on dev, before and after, recorded in the hand-off. The Edge Function deploys (storage-presign, read-urls, gc, teardown) follow this migration on each environment.

*As built (2026-11-05; file md5 `3112f0606e409598fd35442c489743f5` at `0465b454`; suites 115 (615 checks), 61 (89), 63 (25), 66 (37), 78 (62); **applied on wilson-dev 2026-11-05 05:59 UTC**, on her word in the session that applied it):* What differs from §1.4 and §2.1:

- (a) **A picture named only by `thumbnail_url` is claimed** (the question 0114's round left here), with `files_thumbnail_url_idx`, in `storage_object_claimed` and `storage_object_current_project`. **Only under the row's own project** (the review's blocker): until 0114 a client could write any text in that column, and 0114 froze the value without judging it, so a name under another project would have opened another company's picture, for reading and for writing. Such a name now claims nothing and answers for nothing, and it blocks nobody's key. **The pre-flight refuses them** (the second look): a purge queues what the row names (0054's trigger) and a move retires it (`files_body_follow`), so it could still remove another company's picture. No client can write a new one since 0114, so clearing them once closes it for good. A name not under `projects/` names no object anyone holds, and an s3 row's picture is in its own company's bucket: both pass.
- (b) **One picture has one namer.** `storage_object_current_project` counts rows as the caller, so a caller who could see only one of two namers would be answered for both. The pre-flight refuses both shapes (two rows' `thumbnail_url`; one row's key and another's `thumbnail_url`), and `file_key_admissible` is restated from 0114 with one arm: no new `supabase` or `local_server` key whose `.jpg` another row's `thumbnail_url` names. So no client can make such a pair. The index is not unique: a client's picture is its own key's, and keys are unique.
- (c) The meter's second arm decides "no project" from each object's own key, not the body's, and takes a set of keys, so a picture two rows name counts once.
- (d) `can_presign_object` treats a NULL op as refused (a bare `NOT IN` would have fallen through to the write answer). `has_active_membership` in its claimed arm cannot bite: `files_select` already asks it through `projects_select`. It is kept because §2.1 names it.
- (e) The file sets `lock_timeout` to 15 s and takes `SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE` on `files` first. A one-statement write to `files` cannot deadlock with it; `rekey_file_body` can, because it reads `storage.objects` before it writes `files`. One side gets 40P01 and rolls back whole.
- (f) **Limits, recorded.** A project's writer can still rename a claimed body off its key and then remove it, or overwrite it, through the UPDATE arm, as they always could: `NOT claimed` stops a direct clean-up and nothing more. The money policies do not read the row (SB-7), so a trashed or queued copy under a money or Legal key stays open to its cleared audience, who can restore it anyway; SB-20 holds for every other key (suite 115 pins both sides). In a company's own bucket a record names a key and that key's own `.jpg`, so an s3 row whose picture has another name stays on the prefix rule, and so does a Drive row's picture in Petal's bucket: nothing writes either shape (0 such rows on dev, and the pre-flight counts them). For later: `file_key_admissible` could also judge a Drive row, if anything ever writes one.
- (g) **The client half.** A re-file or a receipt move whose row was binned meanwhile cannot put its object back (the old key stays claimed), so it leaves it at the new key and says where it is. A file kept with a row whose id is in the bin takes back what the run just uploaded: the list hides the bin's copy, so the run cannot know until the insert says so.

**Measured on dev, rolled back, 3,000 files with bodies and pictures, one in six a moved copy whose old project is gone (bucket about 3,000 objects each):** a person's list by name, 94 ms before and 922 ms after (a picture list 91 and 1,028; as a member 106 and 896), because every object in the bucket is judged; the lookup a signed URL runs, 2.4 ms and 3.1 ms; an upload's insert, 214 ms and 237 ms; the meter, 212 ms and 233 ms with 500 moved rows, its number up by exactly their 505,000 bytes; `storage_object_claimed` about 0.04 to 0.09 ms a call and `storage_object_current_project` 0.29 ms for a body, 0.42 ms for a picture. `storage.search` of a folder of one, which is what `.list()` runs: 448 ms and 3,761 ms.

**🚨 A list reads the whole bucket.** Supabase's `storage.search` runs row security before its name filter, because `LIKE` is not leakproof, so one `.list()` evaluates the policies on every object in the bucket, of every company. That was already so; 0115 makes each object dearer. At about 1.2 ms an object, a bucket of 5,000 objects takes about 6 s a list. The people it reaches: the shot re-file (`store.exists`, several per file), the receipt move (`existsAt`) and the migration's `objectAlreadyThere` (one per file kept with a row). **Owed with the client changes:** the three exact-name checks move to storage-js `exists()`, one index probe, and the stale comment at `supabaseProvider.js` goes with them. `objectAlreadyThere` does not know its leaf, so it keeps the list.

The whole set with 0115 prepended and on the applied schema: with it prepended 109 suites, 4,834 planned = collected, 4,832 passed, only suite 74's #34 and #35 red (dev's own rows, 7,869 now); on the applied schema 109 suites, 4,834 planned = collected, 4,832 passed (115 615/615, 61 89/89, 63 25/25, 66 37/37, 78 62/62). Run twice in one rolled-back transaction it completes. CI all six green at `0465b454`, so pgTAP from 0000 on a fresh stack carries it, and the pinned policy text holds there. One hundred and forty-six planted breakages: 128 bite. Eighteen do not, all clauses that cannot bite by construction: `has_active_membership` in the write policies (8: the project hop asks it through `projects_select`), the money segment and the first segment in `WITH CHECK` (4: any statement with a `WHERE`, the Storage API's included, also holds the new row to the SELECT policy), the CASE inside `rabbit_files_delete_own` (3: `NOT claimed` leaves only its ELSE), `can_presign_object`'s membership clause (1, as (d) says), and the meter's 'moved' filter (2: an unmoved key's second segment is a live project, so `NOT EXISTS` already drops it; it is there for `files_moved_body_ws_idx`). The pre-flight's three refusals and five controls were each run once on dev, rolled back, with a planted row. No suite can pin the lock, the lock timeout or the pre-flight

**One review round on Opus 5.5** at high effort (four reviewers, one checker per finding, 23 agents, 19 minutes, 3.61M subagent tokens; all 522 responses read `claude-opus-5-5`): 19 findings, 18 kept (5 confirmed, 13 in a narrower form), 1 refuted. Taken in the database: (a), (b), (e), the wording of the header and of `delete_own`'s comment. Taken in the client: (g). Taken in the suites: a statement that names no row reaches the write policy alone (a `WHERE` on a column brings the SELECT policy in too), so the write probes now include blanket UPDATE and DELETE read back as the runner; suite 61 proves the destination alone decides a signed read; suite 66 that each object answers by its own key; suite 78 keeps a presence control for its turned probe; SEC-2 in both directions; a real poster's queue row. Recorded, not changed: (f), the list's cost above. **A second look at the corrections** (one agent on Opus 5.5 at high effort, 11 minutes, 275K subagent tokens): 6 faults, 5 taken: a name under another project still acts at a purge and a move, so the pre-flight refuses them (a); a receipt whose row went from view says where its copy and picture sit and what Cleanup would do, and is not said to be open; no "sits at null"; the re-key probe has its object. Left as it is: the pre-flight's twin count also counts row-less shapes, which only a planted row could trigger and an operator can clear. The next run of the receipt move does not repeat the sentence (it records the file as removed in the cloud).

#### 0116 — `0116_file_event_vocabulary.sql`

file_events_event_check DROP + ADD with the 13 terms. fn_file_events_capture restated once from its latest body: moved_out/moved_in (paths NULL, details '{}') on a project change; refiled within a project; 'moved' only when the project is unchanged; catalogued under the 'check:' marker; the purge-detail body for office-server rows; the EXCEPTION idiom and is_financial on every INSERT kept. fn_realtime_broadcast restated from 0109 with the files-arm early return when the NAS marker equals 'check:'||the row's project_id.

*Constraints:* A CHECK, not an enum, so one transaction. Preflight compares the md5 of 0109's fn_realtime_broadcast and of the latest capture body. Post-condition: 13 terms, each once.

**Her call for this file (2026-11-05, in the session that built 0115, asked as a multiple-choice question):** *"A private project keeps a log of what happened to its files (added, renamed, moved, deleted, and who did it). If the project is later opened to the whole company, should the newly admitted people see the log entries from its private time?"* Answered, verbatim: **"Keep them hidden (Recommended)"**, whose description read *"Entries from the private time stay visible only to the people who could see the project then: its creator and admins. Everyone else sees the log from the moment it was opened. The edit history already follows this rule."* So 0116 adds `file_events.captured_private` (boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true, set by a BEFORE INSERT trigger for every writer, the rows already written walked back through `edit_history_value_at`), and `file_events_select` gains `AND (NOT captured_private OR admin OR the caller created the project)`.
