*As built (2026-11-05; file md5 `c492ae8d49ca8362f647beb137f19f05` at `23569f3f`; suites 33 (81 checks), 78 (90), 112 (48), 20 (29), with pins moved in 95, 100, 101 and 115; **applied on wilson-dev 2026-11-05 18:22 UTC**, on her word typed in the session that applied it, md5 read back. The whole set, with it prepended and on the applied schema alike: 109 suites, 4,909 planned = collected, 4,906 passed, red only suite 74's #34 and #35 and suite 25's #13, dev's own rows; 25 is red without the file too. One review round on Opus 5.5 at high effort: 26 agents, all 560 responses `claude-opus-5-5`, 3.58M subagent tokens, 22 findings, 18 kept, 4 refuted. A second look: 1 agent, all 35 responses `claude-opus-5-5`, 4 findings, 3 taken. 60 of 61 planted breakages bite; the 61st, the stamp's trigger not being a definer, cannot, because everything that reaches it is a definer or the runner, and a post-condition pins it.)*

**What it does beyond the text above.**
- **The walk runs once.** `edit_history` keeps 90 days (`wilson-purge-edit-history`, nightly), so a later walk could reopen a row once its flip is purged: review round 1's major finding. `file_events_restamp` (revoked from every role but its owner, 0108's shape) runs only in the run of the file that adds the column; a re-run walks nothing and keeps the trigger's stamps (rehearsed: run twice with the flip purged between).
- **Its arms, in order:** the project gone, closed; older than its project, closed; older than 90 days, closed; no flip in what is kept, the live value with no walk; else walked back, closed when the walk has no answer.
- **Her second ruling (2026-11-05, in the session that applied it, asked as a multiple-choice question):** *"When this update first runs, it sorts the file log entries that already exist. The record of whether a project was private is only kept for 90 days. What should happen to log entries older than that?"* Answered, verbatim: **"Keep them hidden (Recommended)"**, whose description read *"Only the project's creator and admins see them, so nothing private can slip out. The cost: some ordinary old entries also disappear for everyone else. The test database has none this old today, and the live site should have none before December."* So the third arm closes them. Dev had none. Not taken from the second look: an arm opening rows older than 0072's date, since 0074's measurement found no row on any environment on 2026-09-07, four days before it.
- **`refiled` is a person's.** A foreign key emptying a file's folder (a folder, scene or asset deleted) writes nothing, told by the folder it left being gone; a move to the top level with the folder still standing writes `refiled` with `to_folder_id` null. `pg_trigger_depth()` cannot tell them: an AFTER trigger under a foreign key's action fires at depth 1 (the review's correction used it, and suite 33's probe caught it).
- **`moved_out` and `moved_in` are each classified from both sides' flag and both paths**, because neither carries a path for the money trigger to read. They carry no path for `file_event_is_legal` either, so a Legal file's are read by the money audience and admins, not the wider Legal audience: closed, as Legal files do not move between projects.
- **`SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '15s'`.** FileAuditDrawer's labels: the design's three, and *Found on the office server* for `catalogued` (the builder's word), and the foot gains a sentence.

**Residuals, recorded.** A demoted admin reads private-time rows until the token expires, the window every `current_app_role()` arm has (R15), edit_history's arm included. A project created again on an old id (0110 and 0114 refuse a client; service-side callers are not judged) gives its creator the older rows, through the creator's arm. A flip and an event in one transaction share `created_at`, and the walk reads such a row as after the flip.

#### 0117 — `0117_network_share_catalogue.sql`

network_share_locator_ok and network_share_bin_ok (IMMUTABLE). network_share_space_folder and network_share_claims (DEFINER, revoked from clients). fn_projects_owner_guard restated from 0114 with the folder-claim nesting refusal and the admin/manager-only folder_slug change rule. projects_folder_slug_shape_chk (NOT VALID, validated after a zero count). files CHECKs network_share_shape, namespace and extra. files_network_share_locator_key and files_network_share_project_idx. Table network_share_checks (ENABLE + FORCE, one SELECT policy with the project hop, SELECT for authenticated) with the thin fn_network_share_checks_broadcast. Table network_share_ops (ENABLE + FORCE, no policies, no client grants). network_share_catalogue_apply, network_share_record_added, network_share_trash, network_share_restore, network_share_settle and network_share_pending_mine; the adopt and share-move cores (_network_share_adopt_core, _network_share_move_core) are revoked from clients and called only by fn_moth_apply_moves. purge_network_share_ops with cron 'wilson-purge-network-share-ops' at '50 4 * * *'.

*Constraints:* After 0112 has committed. Preflight: the enum label, personal_owner_id, the 0108 edit_history arm, zero 'nas:%' paths, zero network_share rows (a client could write one between 0112 and 0114), md5 of 0116's bodies. The rehearsal includes a 5,000-entry apply, with time and row counts recorded.

*As built (2026-11-06; file md5 `c239e9ee668da5f5c5ad45541f9741bc` at `0dd65742`; `fn_projects_owner_guard`'s body `49ebe6b9f996f75682982dfbe1c842e4`, `purge_soft_deleted`'s `65f3322262d48afa923ccd5f40c69a4c`; suites 116 (38 checks), 117 (25), 118 (397 then; 401 with the probe of 2026-11-06, below), with pins moved in 110 and 115; the add route's test, 40 tests; **applied on wilson-dev 2026-11-06 06:32 UTC from `98d532a4`, md5 read back, 112 history rows**, on her word, *"Yes, apply 0117 through 0119 to wilson-dev, each after its rehearsal and review round."*):* measured on dev first: 0 `nas:%` paths, 0 `network_share` rows, 4 slugs and none with a slash, no `workspace_storage` row at all, 04:50 free.

**Beyond the text, from the build.** (a) The helpers are plpgsql, not SQL, because they run per row (0111's trap), and there are more than this entry names, all closed to clients but the two IMMUTABLE tests: `network_share_path_shape_ok`, `_network_share_mtime`, `_drive`, `_inside`, `_placed`, `_overlaps`, `_claim_taken`, `_folder_refused`, `_assert_folder`, `_may_check`, `_may_write`, `_lock_projects`, `_target`, `_replay_origin`. (b) `projects_folder_slug_shape_chk` holds the shape alone and admits an empty slug: `runMigration` sends the desktop's `fileSlugify(title)`, so this entry's `locator_ok` would refuse a project called Finance, or one whose title has no letters, at its insert. Such a slug claims nothing. (c) The overlap arm runs only where the company is on an office server, or two desktops' same-named projects would refuse in a Petal company; a company that moves there later meets `folder_shared` at its first call. (d) Any change of `folder_slug` by a bound caller needs an admin or a manager (§1.4's reading), not only a change of a set one. (e) `fn_files_network_share_delete_guard` is 0114's note (g): BEFORE DELETE, bound, depth 1. (f) `network_share_record_added` journals `done`: the copy came first. (g) `network_share_settle`'s `not_moved` on a move or an adopt calls `_network_share_replay_origin`, a stub that raises `moves_not_ready`, because no office-server marker admits a project change (§1.5). The cores judge and journal and write no file row: `p_write false` is 0118's plan phase, and the commit calls the core and then its one UPDATE. (h) A walk may omit `m` and `t` on an entry, so a file dated before 1980 is still listed; a present but bad one drops the entry; a dropped entry still counts as seen, so its row is not marked missing. (i) `network_share_checks.run_open`: a walk is several calls, and only the final call moves `last_checked_at` and sends the broadcast. Subdirs are capped at 20,000 a call. (j) PS-7's route takes `?root&folder&name&sizeBytes`, folder = `_WILSON_PERSONAL/<signed-in uuid>` (§1.2 item e over PS-7's `Personal_<hex>`); main learns who is signed in over one new gated channel, `rabbit:set-signed-in-user`, from an `App.jsx` effect (47 registrations, 51 channels); the name is reserved with an exclusive create so it never overwrites, and device names (`CON`, `NUL`, ...) are refused at the route only. (k) Suite 115 lifts the three new CHECKs in its own transaction, because it proves 0114 and 0115 on rows they now refuse; suite 110's S5 probe exempts the journal.

**Beyond the text, from the review round.** (l) A locator also holds at most 2,000 bytes, because the unique index refuses a row over about 2,700, and no Windows short name (`ABCDEF~1`): the drive can answer to one for a longer folder, and every name test would read it as some other folder. A real file so named is not listed; `electron/networkShare.cjs` has the twin. (m) **A folder inside the drive under a name no catalogue may hold (money, Legal, dot-led, a short name), or the drive itself, is refused for a space:** `_network_share_folder_refused`, and the owner guard's sentence *WILSON cannot keep a project's files in this folder on the company server. Choose another.* A stored one that predates it stays a claim on its raw path (`network_share_claims` reads `_inside`, not `_placed`), so its own project reads `no_folder` and nobody else is stopped. (n) **`_network_share_assert_folder`** raises `folders_unplaced` or `folder_shared`, and is asked by the apply, `_target`, trash, restore, the move core's source and `network_share_pending_mine`, not only by a walk: a desktop acts on what those return. (o) `_network_share_claim_taken` is also true for another project's live rows or pending ops under the folder. (p) **`purge_soft_deleted` is restated from 0067** (its body `a8dda2f3dc0b04eca7c216e6a4fcc8bf` is pinned in the pre-flight): its `projects` and `files` arms hold while a pending op names the file, or the purge would take the row an op still answers for. So a project in the bin stays until restored and its op settled. (q) **The apply takes `p_first`** (its last parameter), so a walk's first call starts a new run whatever a stopped one left open. **It marks at most 1,500 rows missing a call**, and a send with more to do returns `more_missing`, adds nothing to the run and does not move last checked: a whole share gone at once cannot run past the 8 s limit. A file renamed or a folder renamed on the server keeps its id, in one call or across a walk's calls, because `gone` reads the rows this call hit plus the rows already marked missing. **That join is the performance trap:** the first correction read every row against the seen set and took the same 5,000 from 645 ms to 5.8 s, and only the rehearsal showed it. (r) Trash, restore and the move core refuse a row outside its space's folder (`no_folder`); `restore_back` omits such rows. (s) Settle's adopt requires a size, and keeps the row's date when the desktop sends a bad one. `_network_share_mtime` also handles a bad zone offset. (t) `network_share_pending_mine` asks the drive (`drive_changed`), the folder (`no_folder`) and the assert, per project. (u) **The route:** `writeBody` replaces `stream.pipeline`, which destroyed the socket on a write failure so the client never read the refusal; a stalled upload ends after 2 minutes; `server.requestTimeout = 0` in `electron/main.cjs`, because Node's 300 s would cut a large copy; a link inside the drive, on the parent or swapped in after the rename is caught by an exact on-disk check; temp files older than 24 hours are swept; and it says plainly when something was left behind on the drive, never a false *Nothing was kept*.

**Limits, recorded and not fixed.** A script on WILSON's own page can name another person's id on the add route: foreign pages, frames and other windows are closed by the gate, other origins by the same-origin check, a wrong drive is refused, and a stale push only clears it, but the route's header says so. A directory over one call cannot read complete until catalogued whole: the client sends parts `complete:false`, then the whole directory `complete:true`, and a directory over 5,000 files never reads complete and the client must say so. A row its folder has left cannot be trashed, restored or moved, and the folder cannot be taken by another project until it leaves; the way out is pointing the project back. A rename across calls can attach an id to a copy (same name, size and time, when the original's directory was not read yet): rows stay true to the files. The `requestTimeout` probe is text only.

**Owed.** *Before the walk ships:* the route takes the id from a verified bearer token's `sub`, and `folderRootRefusal` also refuses `_WILSON_PERSONAL` on a project POST and PATCH. *By 0118:* `CREATE OR REPLACE _network_share_replay_origin`; a replay must not call a core with `p_write` true or while its op is pending, so it needs its own path writing `file_moves` and the one UPDATE, locking `project_id` and `dest_project_id` in order; `fn_moth_apply_moves` takes `_network_share_lock_projects` over every source and the destination before any file row lock (§1.6 and MV-2 step 4); it joins suite 115's list of who names the journal's marker. *By the client:* at most about 1,000 entries a call, because a first listing of 5,000 takes about 10 s against an 8 s limit; send the same call again while `more_missing`; `p_first: true` on a walk's first call; omit `m` outside 1980 to tomorrow; `runMigration` says plainly on *This folder is already used...*; a missing row's delete settles `done`, not `not_moved`, or the row can never leave.

**Proof.** Run twice in one rolled-back transaction: completes, one row, three CHECKs, one guard, one job. The 5,000-entry rehearsal, rolled back, as a real client: first 9,659 ms (over `authenticated`'s 8 s, so a client must batch), the same again 666 ms, 500 changed and 500 gone 2,212 ms, then every row gone at once in three sends of about 2.3 to 2.7 s for 1,500 each. The whole set with it prepended: 112 suites, 5,357 planned = collected, 5,354 passed, red only suite 74's #34 and #35 and suite 25's #13, dev's own rows, taken before suite 118's section N; with section N, suite 118 reads 397/397 in CI's pgTAP from 0000 (`98d532a4`, run 42174973332); on the applied schema: the whole set on the final suite, 112 suites, 5,372 planned = collected, 5,369 passed (116 38/38, 117 25/25, 118 397/397), red only the same three, in 210 s. **Planted breakages, 210:** 172 caught by a failed probe (171 in that run, and 151 since), 32 refused by a database rule the breakage trips, 6 cannot bite, none without an answer, each a green under all four suites. **They ran on a throwaway stack in GitHub Actions (run 42173889164, six shards, the final suite, on a branch since deleted), not on dev,** with the unplanted suites 115 to 118 green first: 95 of them in a row on dev left 2.38M dead rows in `files` (section L writes 49,999 a run, and each plant's lock cancelled the autovacuum, whose last finish was the run's start), and dev stopped answering. The first pass found ten real gaps, closed by suite 118's section N. The six: `network_share_checks_select`'s `deleted_at` and membership arms (47, 48) are doubled by `projects_select`, read through the project hop; a walk's skip of another project's name (88) by the insert's `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, the rename's own test and step 7's project filter; an undelete planted in step 7 (90) by its own `deleted_at IS NULL`; restore's and settle's first look (136, 145) by the same test under the lock, with the same sentence. **Plant 151, a restore's way back losing who, was called one of these, and that was wrong (the controller's reading, 2026-11-06).** `trg_files_soft_delete` (0014's `fn_soft_delete_stamp`) empties `deleted_by` on a restore and writes the caller where a delete finds it empty, so the journal's origin is the only place it survives. It read green only because suite 118's delete, restore and settle were one person, whose id the trigger wrote. Section N now has one person delete and another restore and settle `not_moved`, and the row names the first (#398): unplanted green and plant 151 red, 400/401, on a throwaway stack (run 42251181861, the branch since deleted); suite 118 401/401 on dev's applied schema at 16:25 UTC, once, in two requests. Suite 118's md5 is now `c733b2ad2b840dd88ac44fd531e154d0`. The file did not change. Every plant runs alone, because each rehearsal holds `LOCK TABLE` on `projects` and `files`: four at once contended and could read a lock timeout as a bite. CI's fresh stack carries the file (`LOCK TABLE` sits inside the pre-flight's block, or `db query` passes and CI refuses it).

**Rounds.** One review round on Opus 5.5 at high effort: 30 agents, every one of 568 responses reads `claude-opus-5-5` in its transcript, 4.16M subagent tokens, 34 findings, 26 checked (25 real, 1 uncertain), no blockers, the 8 lowest not checked. Then a second look at the corrections: 1 agent, Opus 5.5 at high effort, all 70 entries read `claude-opus-5-5`, 238K tokens, 9 items; among them a probe that stayed green with its fix removed, because a pending op it needed settled was not. All taken but the limits above.

#### 0118 — `0118_moth_moves_and_trash.sql`

fn_moth_apply_moves (internal), moth_move_items (plan and commit), moth_undo_move, moth_trash_files and moth_restore_files (returning jsonb {done, ops}; office-server rows routed to the network_share_* cores), and moth_trash_index (metadata only, needs_desktop, can_restore, purges_at). Every definer opens with the 0082 membership guard. Error codes 'moth_move:<code>' and 'moth_trash:<code>', P0001.

*Constraints:* Preflight requires: personal_owner_id; _moth_target_eligible; moth_can_write_target; rabbit_private.file_key_admissible; storage_object_current_project named in the four base rabbit_files policies; the network_share label; network_share_folder_of/space_folder; fn_checked_move_admits named in fn_files_body_guard; the 0108 edit_history arm; the 0116 vocabulary. s3 cross-project moves refuse s3_moves_off.

*As built (2026-11-06; file md5 `cc7f0fff6eec371677647539c79843e1` at `01ea8a5d`; `network_share_settle`'s body `3a4e5d614de881b5c80662304603bf05`, `_network_share_replay_origin`'s `f99f94a4b18b38afcb7e98d1d9766f63`, `_network_share_assert_folder`'s `2614188c39f6720c9d0715af004cdc5a`; **applied on wilson-dev 2026-11-06 18:34 UTC** on her word, md5 read back, 113 history rows):* one transaction, no enum, no table, no lock on a table. New: `fn_moth_apply_moves` (closed), `moth_move_items`, `moth_undo_move`, `moth_trash_files`, `moth_restore_files`, `moth_trash_index`, `network_share_stale_moves`, and the closed helpers `_moth_dest_eligible`, `_moth_lock_moves`, `_moth_share_folders_asked`, `_moth_key_leaf`, `_moth_links_alive`, `_moth_share_back`, `_moth_trash_batch`. Restated: `_network_share_replay_origin` (0117's stub), `network_share_settle` and `_network_share_assert_folder`, lifted from 0117's blob with each change marked `0118`. The pre-flight reads twenty bodies by md5.

**Beyond the text, from the build.** (a) `needs_desktop` is raised only by a copy-up's commit with no copy: the server cannot see a client, so a browser's `share_move` is the client's own refusal, from the plan's `how`. (b) *The trash suite's entry above (section 2.3's Tests) said an admin's generic trash succeeds on another person's personal-space file. That is wrong against this design's own invariant I-8 and 0110's applied probes:* `can_write_project` is owner-only there, so `fn_trash_authz` refuses the admin too. The as-built refusal stands and suite 120 pins it; the controller read both, and no question to her was needed. (c) `network_share_settle` locks every op's file project, `project_id` and `dest_project_id` in one ordered call before any row: taken one at a time inside the replay they could cross a walk's. (d) `_moth_share_back`: an undo sends an office-server file to the exact place it left, which `_network_share_move_core` cannot, because a walk files a row in a subdirectory with no folder row. An adopted file's undo stays on the server, through the core. (e) `moth_trash_index` runs a page on to the end of a batch trashed together, because they share `deleted_at` and `p_before` is a strict less-than, so a page can reach 500 plus a batch. (f) Refusals added: `pending` (any file with an unsettled op, a folder change included); `plan_changed` also when a row changed project during the lock wait, and for a key whose only fault is its name part (renamed while the copy was made); `copy_not_needed`; `copy_refused` for a key that is not the minted shape, this file; a re-home in an office-server company, as 0114's follow says. (g) An adopt queues nothing: the copy may yet fail. (h) A way back keeps working after its journal row's 30 days: `network_share_ops.origin` holds the whole from-state, and `undo_of` reads the row only if it is still there. (i) `links_cleared` on an undo counts what it clears at the destination; `links_not_restored` and `pictures_not_restored` say what it could not bring back.

**Beyond the text, from the review round.** (j) **A move into a company's own bucket refuses `s3_moves_off`, plan and commit,** though the file is in Petal's storage or on a computer: `files_body_follow` asks only where the body is now, and `file_key_admissible` looks for an object only in Petal's storage, so a key would be taken on the caller's word and the only body queued for deletion. A move by association still goes. (k) **The residual in section 2.4's risks did not work:** the settle RPC refuses a NULL workspace, so service_role could not settle anything through it. After 24 hours, `not_moved` only, a move or adopt between projects may be called off by whoever passes `_network_share_may_check` on both ends, and `network_share_stale_moves()` is how they find one, because the journal has no client grant. Only its own computer can say a file did move. A personal space stays its owner's. (l) A replay clears `undone_at` on what the undone move's journal row undid, so a failed undo can be tried again, whoever called it off. A batch half back answers `changed_since`. (m) An undo takes the advisory locks (for where each file is now as well as both ends of the journal), then its journal row, then the gate, then the positions: holding the journal row first crossed a settle's replay. (n) `_moth_trash_batch` locks the non-office-server rows and asks the exclusion again before each: without the lock, the rights were read from the project a file was leaving. Files' direct call of `soft_delete_row` keeps that window, bounded and restorable. (o) A foreign-key failure mid-call reads `changed_since`, so no constraint's name goes out; the replay reads its folder `FOR KEY SHARE`. (p) `moth_trash_index` drops trashed projects, others' personal rows and financial rows in SQL before the per-row gate; the gate decides, and these only save its cost. (q) **A move asks the office-server folder claims once per project:** each core asked them per file and each asking scans every project, so 500 files with 200 projects took 9.2 s, over `authenticated`'s 8 s. `_network_share_assert_folder` has one early answer, bound to `wilson.network_share_asked`, which only `_moth_share_folders_asked` sets and `fn_moth_apply_moves` clears; suite 119 pins that only those three name it. (r) Section 7 pins the order of the locks by position and by exact statement, because one transaction cannot race them.

**Limits, recorded and not fixed.** A plain writer on two projects can call `moth_move_items` by PostgREST on office-server files from a browser: the rows move with pending ops while the real files stay, until the 24-hour call-off. Files' direct `soft_delete_row` above. A folder deleted since an undo's move is not named in the return: the row lands with no folder, as its own delete would have left it.

**Owed.** *By 0120, before it turns S3 moves on:* a server-side look at the object in a company's own bucket (S3 to S3 has the same gap), and the plan's `to_provider` compared at the commit, which `s3_moves_off` makes unreachable today. *By the desktop client:* a returning laptop re-reads its operation's state before renaming, or a call-off followed by a late rename leaves the row at the origin and the file at the destination; a browser refuses `share_move` from the plan's `how`. *By the Moth page:* a way to list `network_share_stale_moves` for who may call one off. *By 0119:* the 'move' row of `moth_history`.

**Proof.** Run alone 1.5 s and twice in one rolled-back transaction 1.7 s, on dev. Rolled back on dev, as `authenticated`, with 200 office-server projects: 500 files by association plan 1.0 s, commit 2.7 s, undo 3.5 s; trash 2.9 s, restore 1.8 s; `moth_trash_index` past 5,000 hidden trashed rows 1.6 s, a page of 200 for an admin 0.2 s; on the office server plan 1.2 s, commit 3.6 s, settle 0.15 s, undo 4.4 s, `not_moved` 2.6 s. The whole set with the file prepended: 114 suites, 5,721 planned = collected, 5,718 passed, red only suite 74's #34 and #35 and suite 25's #13, dev's own rows, in 247 s. On the applied schema: 119 269/269, 120 75/75, 118 402/402, 115 615/615, in 42 s. CI's pgTAP from 0000 at `01ea8a5d`, run 42270325148, all six green. **226 planted breakages on a throwaway stack, baseline first, run 42270332239 on the final text:** 131 caught by a failed probe, 75 refused by a database rule (section 7 or the pre-flight on 33, a CHECK or the guard, or the plant's own refusal stopping a suite step, on 42), 20 green under all four suites, 0 without a real answer. The 20, each with the clause that does the same job, checked in the code: the first destination loop and the company count (the second loop under the locks, and the gate's workspace; they only spare a lock); an undo's workspace filter (the actor filter and the gate); `_moth_share_back`'s locator test (the journal's path was judged when written, and the CHECK); its two claim checks (`_moth_share_folders_asked` asked first); its op-arriving arm (the row arm, because rows move first); the replay's own project lock (settle's); its provider test (a provider changes only under a journal, which `pending` refuses, and the key test); settle's `op` and `dest_project_id` tests (each does the other's job: trash and restore carry no destination); stale list's company (`_network_share_may_check` asks it); the batch's guard (both wrappers, and section 7 pins them); its DISTINCT and its provider filter on the rest (the rows are read by `ANY`; a second trash or restore of a row is a no-op); the index's four SQL filters (the gate, these are cost only); the helper's no-server test (`network_share_space_folder` is NULL with no server).

**A cold read since, of the once-per-project claims, written after the second look** (one agent, Opus 5.5, 168k tokens): no defect. Two test gaps closed in suite 119. A row that has left its source folder in a project whose folder overlaps another's now hears `folder_shared` before `no_folder`: refused either way, so the helper's comment overstates. **Plant 139's reason** (no trash, restore or add operation carries a destination) is true of every writer and of no rule: a later migration may make it a CHECK, `(op IN ('move','adopt')) = (dest_project_id IS NOT NULL)`, NOT VALID then validated.

**Rounds.** One review round on Opus 5.5 at high effort: 30 agents, every one of 914 responses reads `claude-opus-5-5` in its transcript, 4.32M subagent tokens, 38 findings, 25 checked (24 real, 1 refuted), 13 past the checkers' cap, read by hand and taken. Then a second look at the corrections by one agent, Opus 5.5, 271k tokens: no blockers, one major (a section-7 word check gone inert, because settle's new select list named the same column as its lock) and five minor, all taken. Not taken, with its reason above: the plan's provider at the commit, owed to 0120.

#### 0119 — `0119_moth_history.sql`

Table public.moth_history (row_key uuid UNIQUE, per-side project, class and owner, projections, locked, the six named CHECKs and the indexes); ENABLE without FORCE, zero policies, no client grants (sequence included). moth_history_projection_ok and moth_history_project (IMMUTABLE). fn_moth_history_capture with 22 triggers on 11 tables (AFTER INSERT OR DELETE, and AFTER UPDATE WHEN the pinned columns change), redact-on-lock, capture_failed restart marks. Baseline marks. moth_history_read and moth_history_window (DEFINER, a single refusal). purge_moth_history with cron 'wilson-purge-moth-history' at '45 4 * * *'.

*Constraints:* The last Session 1 database migration. The preflight (see interfaces) refuses a re-run once a later migration is recorded. Applied off-hours on staging and production, on her go: CREATE TRIGGER briefly takes SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE on 11 hot tables.

*As built (2026-11-06; file md5 `9a4556b460224cb4680a534f98ec841f` at `5020401b`; capture's body `837ea748d9f13341311cd9ef826b8aa3`; **applied on wilson-dev 2026-11-06 19:55 UTC** on her word, md5 read back, 114 history rows):* as the design's section 2.5, in one transaction, with `lock_timeout` 15 s, because the triggers take SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE on twelve tables. Suite 121, 290 checks.

**Beyond the text.** (a) A projects row takes its audience from its own image: after a delete there is no row to ask, and on an update the row reads only as it is now. (b) `moth_history_project`, while its output would pass 4,096 octets, cuts harder in passes (arrays to 20, then 5 and text short, then arrays gone), never an id, so what it makes always passes the CHECK. A writer otherwise could force a restart mark at will. (c) `moth_history_projection_ok` is plpgsql, not SQL, because a SQL function with a SET is planned afresh per row. (d) `_moth_history_folder_locked` classifies a folder from its image as the live walk does: its path, its money slug under a root, any folder above it, or the category its path begins with (for a cascade that has taken the folders between). Asking the path alone recorded the children of a renamed money category, and once deleted they read as nothing's. (e) **A trigger on `expenses` blanks a file's history when an expense names it,** because a file becomes a receipt with no write to the file (0076), and at its own delete it is too late: a project's purge takes its expenses first. This is a twelfth table, and the design's "one exception" holds: it is the same redaction. (f) **Capture never reads the caller's claims.** The design's fallback to `current_workspace_id()` is gone: it could name another company in a cascade, or raise on claims that do not parse, outside the protected block, and fail a person's delete. A child of a project that has gone writes no row, because no side of it can ever pass the gate: T-01's phase in a project's cascade writes nothing, while a phase deleted by itself still does. A file or folder still goes through the lock first. (g) The redaction leaves marks alone, because a planted id equal to the company's would otherwise fail the CHECK and redact nothing. (h) Two sides cut alike do not skip a row. (i) `moth_history_read` goes a batch of 2,000 candidates at a time, newest first, and stops when it has enough: the design's one CTE gated all 90 days first, so one office-server walk put every read in the company past the 8 s. `moth_history_marks_idx` serves the window. (j) Definers carry `SET row_security = off`, as 0117 and 0118 do; two closed helpers beyond the design's; the pre-flight names `fn_projects_owner_guard`, into which the design's identity guard was merged, and pins four bodies. Section 8 also says every company has its marks.

**Limits, recorded and not fixed.** A folder of about 3,000 files is the most a delete fits in 8 s (7.2 s on dev), because the key's SET NULL updates each and each writes a row. A catalogue call of 1,000 new and 1,500 gone is about 6.4 s of writes before the apply's own work: the owed client sends fewer, and treats a timeout by sending less. `moth_history_read` with a cursor at a key hidden from the caller is accepted, as the design says: it brackets a time the holder has usually seen. A file left in a folder deleted under a renamed money category reads as ordinary, live and here alike: the folder guard could refuse such a delete, a later migration's. The purge is one plain DELETE: at a real volume, watch it. `row_key` is random, so its index takes a random page per row.

**Owed.** *By the client that walks an office server:* fewer entries a call. *By Session 2:* the first reader, and its latency at real volume. *Takeout:* moth_history is deliberately in no takeout; the line is in `WorkspaceTakeout.jsx`.

**Proof.** The file alone 2.6 s and twice in one rolled-back transaction 1.7 s, on dev, before its last post-condition. The whole set with the file prepended, on dev: 115 suites, 6,009 planned = collected, 6,006 passed, red only suite 74's #34 and #35 and suite 25's #13, 378 s, on the text before the last five probes. On the applied schema: 121 290/290, 119 272/272, 120 75/75, 118 402/402, 115 615/615, in 65 s, rolled back clean with no history rows or restart marks left. CI's pgTAP from 0000 at `5020401b`. Rolled back on dev with capture on: a 500-file move 3.1 s, undo 3.6 s, on an office server 4.5 s and 4.9 s; `moth_history_read` past 20,000 hidden rows 0.7 s, 5,000 rows 2.4 s, the window 31 ms. **190 planted breakages on a throwaway stack, baseline first, run 42282987082 on the final text:** 156 caught by a failed probe, 19 refused (14 by section 8 or the pre-flight, 5 by the plant's own refusal stopping a suite step), 15 green, 0 without a real answer. The design's twelve controls all bite. The 15, each with the clause that does the same job, checked in the code: a root being classified (it has no root above it and no parent to walk); a gone file or folder's classification (each lock's own write, and the receipts' trigger, have already redacted it); tags not passed (`files_legal_folder_chk` ties a Legal tag to a path that locks); the skip's class and owner tests (each does the other's job); a locked row hiding its entity and locked rows filtered (each does the other's job, and money only grows); the gate's project and other-table arms (the side test asks the same project while the entity is still in one of the row's side projects; for the nine tables other than files, folders and projects the gate is otherwise stricter than H7, not the same test: an entity moved twice, last into a project the reader cannot see, keeps its older rows hidden, where H7's side tests alone would show them as removed. H7 is the rule, so there is no gap); the reader's company filter (the gate asks it); an admin's personal arm in the reader and the window (the gate refuses an admin there); no baseline on CI's empty stack (section 8 refuses it wherever a company exists).

**Rounds.** One review round on Opus 5.5 at high effort: 30 agents, 4.07M subagent tokens, 38 findings, 25 checked, 1 refuted, 13 past the checkers' cap read by hand, the tests' taken. A second look by one agent, Opus 5.5, 206k tokens: two majors in the code, taken, one by a better fix than it proposed, because a probe showed its assumption about the purge's order wrong.

#### 0120 — `0120_s3_body_moves_on.sql`

CREATE OR REPLACE rabbit_private.s3_body_moves_on() returning true, with a post-condition.

*Owed from 0118's review round, before this turns on:* a server-side look at the object in a company's own bucket, because `file_key_admissible` looks only in Petal's storage and a move would take the key on the caller's word and queue the only body; and the plan's `to_provider` compared at the commit.

*Constraints:* Committed and applied per environment only after that environment's storage-presign and storage-read-urls are both verified (functions list plus a source comparison): on the old read-urls, a moved s3 file's picture and preview are refused, because it filters by the key's project. The runbook records the check query and the rule that storage-presign is never rolled back below this version. The build may hand this off as the session's last step if the deploy is not yet verified.

#### 0121 — `0121_moth_repairs.sql`

Added by the whole-session review. It restates eleven functions and adds four, replaces one policy, and changes no table. **Her two integration answers, unbuilt until now.** Answer 2: `rabbit_private.personal_space_of_a_leaver` (the caller an admin, the owner someone else who is not an active member), `moth_leaver_files`, `moth_move_leaver_files`, and an arm in `_moth_target_eligible` and `moth_can_write_target` that honours the marker `wilson.moth_leaver_move` only after asking that helper of the person calling. Answer 3: `workspace_storage_usage()` gains `fullness`, and its figures and `reserve_upload_bytes`' amounts are an admin's alone. **What only the database could repair.** `network_share_pending_mine` skips a refused project's operations instead of raising for the whole call. `_network_share_may_call_off` lets an admin say an operation did not happen once its person has left, in `network_share_settle` and `network_share_stale_moves`. A settled adopt queues its old picture. `purge_soft_deleted` closes an operation left past the retention, so it holds nothing for ever. `moth_project_files` returns `share_missing_since`.

**Round 2's corrections to it.** `moth_leaver_files` offers only a body in Petal's storage that is no asset's version and was not read into its project, fenced as 0111's read is; a body on that person's computer is refused outright (`on_their_computer`), plans included, because on an office-server company the plan would adopt a file that never arrives. A personal space's Petal body is always copied to the project's key, and on an office server, or in a company's own bucket until 0120, that copy is refused, **so such a company is offered nothing, and those files stay until it can be.** The record says how many under the move's id, because a list of ids stopped a move of about 195 at the log's 8,000 characters. `workspace_storage_usage` runs its meter once (`OFFSET 0`), and the plan's size is an admin's to read off the table too. `network_share_pending_mine` says a file's size and an operation's age. `network_share_locator_ok` refuses a device name; `network_share_path_shape_ok` does not, because `projects_folder_slug_shape_chk` asks the shape and a project called Con has that slug. `network_share_catalogue_apply` no longer calls rows under a folder that has turned all-numbered missing, and that folder gets no row beside them, unless it already had one; which folders those are is read once per call, by a join on each row's ancestors, because asked per entry it would scan the project for each frame folder. And for ten minutes it does not add back a file a move has just taken from a path (a delete's row knows it is in the bin, and a new file at that name lists at once).

**On wilson-dev: applied 2026-11-07 04:14:06 UTC from `2836d067`, on her word, md5 `bf8630de2b66e5f2abe90135714e6761` read back. Its plants: 90 on a throwaway stack, run 42336648958 (and 42337173892 for the one rerun): 62 caught by a failed probe, 18 refused by a rule, 10 green and named in the hand-off.** **Its second look (one agent, Opus, 345,568 tokens).** Took: a row already listed under a device name would have refused every write once the locator refused the name, so the pre-flight refuses first; the per-entry scan; offering what could only be refused; a frame folder's own row frozen; and every body this file adds is pinned by md5 in the post-conditions, and the pre-flight pins 0055's usage, in place of word tests a change beside the word would pass.

*Limits.* Rows under a frame folder cannot be called missing, because the walk does not go into one: a real `Selects/` emptied on the server and refilled with numbered frames still reads its old rows as there, and the frames are not listed, until the folder stops reading as a series. An operation the purge closes with an adopt's old picture leaves that picture until the bucket's own sweep. A former member's file in their folder on the office server is not moved: the desktop's routes open that folder to its owner's token only, so it reads `in_their_server_folder`. There is no undo of such a move, because the way back would be into a space the admin cannot write. Nearly full is nine parts in ten of what the meter counts, uploads in progress included. A member could still learn the total by probing uploads one size at a time: the notice stops telling them, and refusing an upload has to say it did not fit.

### 1.5 Trusted writers of the guarded columns

- **files.project_id, folder_id (to a non-NULL value), storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url, is_financial, share_missing_since, share_bin_path; workspace_id never (fn_files_body_guard, 0114)**: (1) fn_moth_apply_moves, the core of moth_move_items, moth_undo_move and settle's 'not_moved' replay, under wilson.file_move = the file_moves row written in this txid and admitted per item by fn_checked_move_admits(file, to_project, to_folder, to_provider, to_path): project and folder changes, cleared links, and re-home, copy-up and share-move locators. (2) rekey_file_body under wilson.body_rekey = 'file:<id>': same project prefix, provider unchanged, never s3, never a moved row. Its callers are the shot re-file (supabaseAdapter.rewriteFileRow ← refileOneObject ← refileShotFolders) and the migration runner's receipt move (runMigration.moveReceipt, is_financial false→true only). (3) The office-server catalogue writer and its file-manager RPCs (network_share_catalogue_apply, network_share_record_added, network_share_trash, network_share_restore, network_share_settle, and the adopt and share-move cores inside fn_moth_apply_moves) under wilson.network_share_writer = 'check:<project>' | 'op:<file>', for network_share rows only and never project_id. (4) RI ON DELETE SET NULL of folder_id. (5) Unbound sessions: migrations, pg_cron purges, service_role Edge Functions (storage-gc, teardown), pgTAP setup as postgres. Existing writers still working: uploadFile INSERT (admissible key), runMigration insertOrSkip (same id excluded; 23505 counted as skipped), updateFile and patchFile (guarded columns stripped; identical resends pass), Legal tag edits, soft_delete_row, restore_soft_deleted and purge_soft_deleted for non-office-server rows, and workspace teardown. A pgTAP text probe pins which functions name each marker literal.
- **files rows of a personal project: storage_provider never local_server or google_drive (folded into fn_files_body_guard)**: Every bound caller is refused. A private project's local media reaches a personal space only through a copy_up in fn_moth_apply_moves, where the provider switches in the same UPDATE.
- **projects.created_by, topic_epoch, is_private, producer_id, personal_owner_id, and the folder_slug/folder_root/deleted_at of a personal row; a reused project id (fn_projects_owner_guard, 0107 restated in 0110, 0114 and 0117)**: created_by and topic_epoch: the guard only. is_private: the creator or an admin, and never on a personal row. producer_id: those with can_manage_project_roster. personal_owner_id: ensure_personal_space at INSERT only. folder_root: admins and managers (0049, unchanged) within the claim rules. Unbound sessions and service_role pass except the epoch bump, which applies to everyone.
- **project_members on a personal project (fn_project_members_personal_guard)**: Only (personal_owner_id, 'manager'), seated by fn_projects_auto_staff through ensure_personal_space. DELETE is unguarded so account-deletion cascades still run.
- **folders.path, slug, parent_id locked status, and a folder's project_id and id (fn_folders_lock_guard, 0111)**: No writer may change a folder's money/Legal status, its project or its id, or detach a folder's first path segment from its parent's. Unbound sessions pass.
- **file_moves, network_share_ops, moth_history (no client grants)**: file_moves: fn_moth_apply_moves, moth_undo_move, purge_file_moves. network_share_ops: the network_share_* RPCs, fn_moth_apply_moves, purge_network_share_ops. moth_history: fn_moth_history_capture, the 0119 baseline, purge_moth_history, and suspension marks inserted by any later migration that disables the triggers.
- **network_share_checks**: network_share_catalogue_apply only. Clients have SELECT only, through the project hop.

### 1.6 Server functions

- **public.moth_move_items(p_file_ids uuid[], p_dest_project uuid, p_dest_folder uuid DEFAULT NULL, p_plan_only boolean DEFAULT false, p_bodies jsonb DEFAULT NULL) RETURNS jsonb** (RPC, SECURITY DEFINER, GRANT authenticated): Checks, in order: membership guard; 1 to 500 distinct ids; destination project and folder eligible (_moth_target_eligible; a folder in another project or locked gives not_found); the projects' advisory locks first (a walk holds its project's while it takes file rows), then project and folder rows FOR SHARE and files FOR UPDATE, each in id order, every source and the destination together; each file eligible (else not_found, the same text as a random uuid); moth_can_write_target on source and destination; cross-project rules (asset_versions gives is_an_asset_version, ingestion_chunks has_extracted_text, files_body_follow's refusals body_missing, body_shape, s3_moves_off, body_elsewhere, then only_on_a_computer, needs_desktop, no_folder, name_taken). Plan returns {items:[{id, how:'metadata'|'rehome'|'copy_up'|'share_move'|'none', to_provider, to_path, to_thumb, from}]} and writes nothing (as built: the to_ fields only for rehome, copy_up and share_move, from only for share_move). Commit: p_bodies [{file_id, path, thumbnail}] is required for rehome and copy_up into company storage and checked against the minted pattern, file_key_admissible and that the object exists (copy_refused otherwise). It writes the file_moves journal, sets wilson.file_move, does one UPDATE per file (project, folder, the seven links cleared, is_core_definer false, locator from follow's set, a legacy picture detached), calls files_body_retire, writes pending network_share_ops for share_move and office-server copy_up items, then clears the marker. Returns {move_id, moved, links_cleared, ops:[{op_id,id,from,to}]}.
- **public.moth_undo_move(p_move_id uuid, p_plan_only boolean DEFAULT false) RETURNS jsonb** (RPC, SECURITY DEFINER, GRANT authenticated): Membership guard. The journal row must be in this workspace, by this actor, and not undone (already_undone). Read gate on every from and to project and every file (not_found). Every file must still be at its to-position and live (changed_since). Replays the reverse through fn_moth_apply_moves; links are restored only where the target still exists in the origin; refuses needs_copy ('move it back with Move to') when the way back needs a fresh copy; office-server items return new ops for the desktop. Redo is moth_undo_move(<the undo's id>). As built it takes the locks, for where each file is now as well, then its journal row, then the gate. Returns {move_id, moved, links_cleared, ops, links_not_restored, pictures_not_restored}; a plan returns {items, move_id}.
- **public.moth_trash_files(uuid[]) / public.moth_restore_files(uuid[]) RETURNS jsonb {done, ops}** (RPC, SECURITY DEFINER, GRANT authenticated): Membership guard; 1 to 500 ids, in id order. _moth_target_eligible(file, id, true), else moth_trash:not_found. Non-office-server rows go through soft_delete_row or restore_soft_deleted (fn_trash_authz unchanged). network_share rows go to the network_share_trash and restore cores and return ops for the desktop. Any raise rolls back the whole batch.
- **public.moth_trash_index(p_limit int DEFAULT 200, p_before timestamptz DEFAULT NULL) RETURNS TABLE(id, project_id, project_title, folder_id, name, mime_type, size_bytes, kind, deleted_at, deleted_by_label, purges_at, can_restore, needs_desktop)** (RPC, SECURITY DEFINER STABLE, GRANT authenticated): Trashed files passing _moth_target_eligible(file, id, true), deleted within 30 days, newest first, capped at 500. No locator columns, no counts. A trashed project's files, money, Legal and others' personal rows are hidden.
- **public.ensure_personal_space() RETURNS uuid** (RPC, SECURITY DEFINER, GRANT authenticated): Refuses (42501) without auth, a workspace or an active membership. Returns the caller's existing personal row, or creates one (title 'Personal space', is_private, personal_owner_id = created_by = auth.uid()) with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING and a re-select. Exactly one per person per workspace.
- **public.record_personal_space_opened(p_project uuid) RETURNS void** (RPC, SECURITY DEFINER, GRANT authenticated): Admin only; the project must be someone else's personal space in this workspace; one 42501 message otherwise. Inserts app_events('admin', 'WIL-4114', 'An admin opened someone else''s personal space in Files', context {project_id, owner_user_id}), which other admins see in Logs.
- **public.moth_islands() / moth_project_folders(p_project uuid) / moth_project_files(p_project uuid, p_after uuid DEFAULT NULL, p_limit int DEFAULT 500)** (RPC, SECURITY INVOKER, GRANT authenticated): Filtered by moth_can_read_target. islands: (id, title, is_personal, is_mine, is_private, my_project_role, is_staffed, can_write, updated_at), never another person's space, admins included. files: keyset on id, clamped 1 to 1000, folder_id NULL when it is not this project's folder, no is_financial, no counts. A hidden or missing project gives an empty result.
- **public.moth_can_read_target / moth_can_write_target(p_kind text, p_id uuid) RETURNS boolean; public._moth_target_eligible(text, uuid, boolean); public.moth_file_row_locked(uuid, uuid, uuid, boolean, text, text[], uuid); public.moth_folder_path_locked(text)** (helpers (DEFINER; _eligible and _locked revoked from clients; moth_folder_path_locked IMMUTABLE)): Kinds in Session 1: project, folder, file. Missing and hidden both answer false. The personal arm has no admin leg. Money and Legal are excluded for every role through the classifiers, never the access gates. The write twin adds can_write_project, which is owner-only on personal rows.
- **public.rekey_file_body(p_file uuid, p_from_path text, p_to_path text, p_from_thumb text, p_to_thumb text, p_financial boolean DEFAULT false) RETURNS boolean** (RPC, SECURITY DEFINER, GRANT authenticated): false on a compare-and-set miss; 42501 'file not found or not writable' for every authority failure. Same project prefix only, never s3, never a moved row; money only false→true with money access; the picture must be NULL or path||'.jpg'. One UPDATE under the rekey marker.
- **public.can_presign_object(p_path text, p_op text) RETURNS boolean** (helper, SECURITY INVOKER, GRANT authenticated, service_role): A claimed byo-s3 key: get and head need a visible current owner row; put and del are false. An unclaimed key uses 0051's prefix rule unchanged. storage-presign calls it as the caller after checkRowShapedPath.
- **rabbit_private.storage_object_claimed / storage_object_current_project / file_key_admissible / files_body_follow / files_body_retire / s3_body_moves_on / project_id_tied_elsewhere** (helpers in the unexposed schema): As in the bodies design SB-4, SB-5, SB-9, SB-11, SB-13 and SB-18. follow and retire are callable only by fn_moth_apply_moves. Every definer has row_security off, search_path pinned, and is REVOKEd from PUBLIC and anon.
- **public.network_share_catalogue_apply(p_project_id uuid, p_root text, p_folder text, p_dirs jsonb, p_bin jsonb, p_final boolean, p_stopped_at text) RETURNS jsonb** (RPC, SECURITY DEFINER, GRANT authenticated): Check seat (roster managers plus privacy, or the owner of a personal space). byos/network/unc drive equal to p_root, else drive_changed. The space folder must match (no_folder); folders_unplaced; folder_shared. Limits: 2,000 directories, 5,000 files, 500 bin entries, 60 calls per 10 minutes (too_soon), 50,000 rows (too_many). Advisory lock, 'check:' marker, CTE parse, updates, renames, inserts, missing marks per complete directory, check row upsert, then marker reset. Returns {added, updated, renamed, found_again, newly_missing, purge_ok, restore_back, stale, checked_at}.
- **public.network_share_record_added / network_share_trash / network_share_restore / network_share_settle / network_share_pending_mine** (RPC, SECURITY DEFINER, GRANT authenticated): Server-first file-manager operations, journalled in network_share_ops ('pending'), each row write under 'op:<id>'. settle(p_op_ids, 'done'|'not_moved', p_facts) is for the creator (or check-seat holders for stale in-folder ops after 24 h; as built, after 24 h a move or adopt between projects may be settled not_moved alone by whoever may check both ends, and network_share_stale_moves() lists them). 'not_moved' replays the origin through a journalled reverse. restore refuses 'occupied'. pending_mine returns the caller's own pending ops as {op_id, op, from, to}. Return values carry ids and the caller's own locators only.
- **public.moth_history_read(p_scope_project uuid, p_since timestamptz, p_after uuid, p_limit int) / moth_history_window(p_scope_project uuid)** (RPC, SECURITY DEFINER STABLE, GRANT authenticated): As tables H7, H8 and the window spec. One refusal, 'that history is not available'. No bigint, no counts. Not called by any Session 1 client (the scrub is Session 2).
- **public.purge_file_moves / purge_network_share_ops / purge_moth_history** (cron purges, service_role only): At 04:41, 04:50 and 04:45. Retentions of 30, 90 (settled rows only) and 90 days. Each refuses a retention under 1 day.

### 1.7 Edge Function changes

- **supabase/functions/storage-presign/index.ts (l.135-138; header refusal item 5)**: Calls rpc('can_presign_object', {p_path, p_op}) as the caller instead of picking can_presign_project_read or _write on the key prefix. checkRowShapedPath stays first. Deployed after 0115 on each environment, and it must be verified deployed before 0120 is applied there.
- **supabase/functions/_shared/readUrls.ts (classifyOwned, handleReadUrls) and storage-read-urls deps (canReadProject, rowPage)**: classifyOwned drops the key-prefix = row.project_id equality. The canReadProject pre-check goes. rowPage drops .eq('project_id') and keeps the folder LIKE read as the caller. A key no visible row owns is refused as 'row'; 'project' is no longer emitted.
- **supabase/functions/storage-gc/index.ts (rowNamesTwin, referencedPaths)**: rowNamesTwin drops .eq('project_id') and adds .in('storage_provider', ['supabase','s3','local_server']); referencedPaths gets the same provider filter. The drain is unchanged.
- **supabase/functions/_shared/teardownCollect.ts (owned, take, takeThumb), deployed through operator-workspaces**: A path is owned when its prefix project is in this workspace, or when no projects row with that id exists and file_events ties the id to this workspace. Another workspace's prefix is still rejected.
- **supabase/functions/_shared/reservedObjects.ts**: No code change. A vitest pins it in lockstep with rabbit_private.storage_object_claimed's row-less shapes.
- **.github/workflows/rls.yml deno-check KNOWN block**: Re-measured in the same commit as the function edits (baseline 28 today). Lines fixed are lowered, and the merge commit's own run is read.

### 1.8 Desktop changes

- **electron/fileTrash.cjs (new), mounted from main.cjs in place of the inline files DELETE route**: Local Server 30-day trash. DELETE …/files/:id moves the row into bundle.trashedFiles {row, trashed_at, trash_name, base_kind} in one writeJsonAtomic and then renames the body into <base>/.trash/<id>-<safe name>. Routes: POST …/files/:id/restore, POST …/files/restore {ids}, POST …/files/trash {ids} (isMothExcluded gives 404), POST …/managed-files/:id/restore (rows of deleted assets excluded), GET …/trash (read-only), POST …/trash/purge-expired. Every path goes through resolveContainedFilePath before rename or unlink. The pathContainment pin moves here.
- **electron/fileMoves.cjs (new)**: POST …/projects/:projectId/files/move (within one project, folder_id only, one atomic write). POST /api/rabbit/files/move-between-projects (answer 2): validates both bundles, excluded rows, destination folders and name collisions; writes the durable journal <rabbit-data>/moves/<op>.json; renames on the same volume, otherwise copy, fsync, size and sha1 check, then unlink; writes the destination bundle, then the source bundle, reads both back, deletes the journal, and only then answers done. Recovery runs at start-up and before each new operation. The same journal records 'retire the local original after a cloud copy-up'.
- **electron/networkShare.cjs (new) and electron/rootReachable.cjs (extracted question machinery only; bins keeps its location mapping)**: Every route needs isSameOriginRequest plus the launch token, a UNC workspace root, and the root reachable first. File work runs in a Worker or with async, bounded fs, never sync on the main thread. Routes: POST /walk (lstat, no links or junctions, the exclusion lists, frame sequences collapsed, per-directory completeness, limits and deadline); GET/HEAD /file/* (ranges, safeMediaContentType, nosniff); POST /op {pairs} (shape-checked, contained, refuses an existing target, renames and verifies); POST /put (COPYFILE_EXCL, then the size is verified; source must be in the memory-only userAuthorizedFiles set from rabbit:pick-files, or a local media key for a copy-up); POST /purge (bin-shaped paths only); POST /api/network-share/personal-space/files (the owner's '_WILSON_PERSONAL/<uuid>' folder only, no overwrite, ' (2)' naming). Async shareContain uses realpath with STAT_MAX slots.
- **electron/openPath.cjs**: An async 'share' arm resolved by shareContain before openOrReveal. rabbit:open-path awaits reachability for share sources.
- **electron/localMedia.cjs**: A retire route: after a copy-up commits, the holding desktop moves its local copy into <local media root>/.trash for 30 days, recorded in the move journal.
- **electron/main.cjs (signed-out personal space, bundles, relink)**: GET/POST /api/rabbit/personal-space (bundle at {userData}/personal-space/project.json, bodies in {userData}/personal-space/files, one per computer and OS user, outside rabbit-data). getRabbitProjectDir and resolveProjectFilesDir resolve the personal id; resolveProjectFolder returns null for it. DELETE and PATCH /api/rabbit/projects/:id answer 409 for the personal id. POST and PATCH strip personal, personal_owner_id, folder_slug, folder_root and files_dir. reconcileRabbitBundle and emptyBundle add trashedFiles: []. The project GET stays free of side effects. relink-apply answers 409 while trash bodies would be stranded. mountNetworkShare shares one reachability instance with mountCloudBins.
- **electron/projectFilePatch.cjs**: 409 when the body's project_id differs from the route's; 400 on any folder_id change (use the move route). Also strips workspace_id, thumbnail_url and is_financial, matching the cloud guard.

### 1.9 Client changes

- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js updateFile and rewriteFileRow**: sanitize() strips project_id, workspace_id, storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url, is_financial, folder_id, share_missing_since and share_bin_path. rewriteFileRow uses two-phase rpc('rekey_file_body'); a false result goes down the existing re-read-and-put-back branch. *(production caller: RabbitProvider.patchFile and setFileCore; ProjectsPage.jsx; refileOneObject ← refileOneShot ← refileShotFolders)*
- **src/cloud/migrate/runMigration.js (moveReceipt, the ordinary-files loop, the personal-space report line)**: moveReceipt makes one rpc('rekey_file_body', {p_financial: true}). The local personal space is reported as not carried. *(production caller: MigrationPanel → runMigration)*

'moth_move:<code>' and 'moth_trash:<code>' errors map to sentences. copyBodyForMove puts the body at the server-minted key: storage copy (supabase), presigned GET then PUT (s3), or the holding desktop reads the local body and uploads it, with NOT_HERE elsewhere ('this file is on the computer that added it; move it from there'). The fixtures adapter twins each method. *(production caller: src/tools/moth_v0.1.0/state/mothFileOps.js and mothHistory.js; ProjectFilesExplorer.jsx (personal entries, record call, Move to))*
- **supabaseAdapter projectIsPrivate, listProjects; src/cloud/personalSpace.js (personalSpacesAvailable, isPersonalSpace)**: projectIsPrivate means is_private AND NOT personal, so personal uploads go to the company's storage. listProjects adds .is('personal_owner_id', null) and selects topic_epoch behind cached column probes. *(production caller: uploadFile, RabbitProvider.refreshProjectsIndex, TeamMembersPage, WorkspaceTakeout, ProjectFilesExplorer)*
- **supabaseAdapter subscribeProjectChanges and subscribeWorkspaceChanges; realtimeMerge.applyEventCore; RabbitProvider (revalidateRef, sequence-guarded refreshProjectsIndex, AUDIENCE handling, no merge of private or personal rows at either site, epoch re-join, deleteFile and deleteManagedFile undo steps, restoreFile and restoreManagedFile mutators, network_share routing, noteFilesChangedElsewhere, refreshOnOpen, settlePending)**: Repairs R11, moves MV-16 and NAS client wiring. restoreFile gets its first production caller. *(production caller: Existing channel callbacks; FileManager.jsx confirmDelete; mothFileOps.js; useRabbit().showUndoToast)*
- **src/permissions/projectRoleMatrix.js canOnProject and projectActionDeniedReason**: ctx.isPersonal and isPersonalOwner, mirroring the restated SQL helpers. Office-server banner (last checked by whom, Refresh as a GatedAction or 'Refresh from the desktop app'). Delete confirmation copy for local trash and for the office-server bin. The personal-space office-server sentence (answer 3). Labels for moved_out, moved_in and refiled. *(production caller: The Files page (existing route))*
