### 1.10 The Moth page for Session 1

REGISTRY: src/layout/pages.js PAGE_LIST gains {id:'moth', title:'M.O.T.H.', subtitle: the expansion from PLAN_MOTH §1, bars: bars(top, bottom) sized like the other tools, surface:'dark', chrome:'tool', nav:'primary'}. App.jsx: const MothPage = React.lazy(() => import('./tools/moth_v0.1.0')); a flag mothEntered is set once when currentPage === 'moth' (an effect, never reset); renderAllPages gains a 13th <PageSurface id="moth">{mothEntered && <Suspense fallback={<plain 'Opening M.O.T.H.…' line>}><MothPage/></Suspense>}</PageSurface>. Home.jsx MAIN_ITEMS gains a Moth entry, re-measured against the stack budget. PINS TO UPDATE in the same commit: `src/ui/typeScale.test.js` (her ruling of 2026-11-04, *"MO1 or MO2 adds it (Recommended)"*: one regex line beside T1_LANE and T2_LANE for `src/tools/moth_v0.1.0/`; the test fails with "a tool directory belongs to no lane" as soon as the folder exists without one; `po/moth2a-engine`, the same folder, will not touch that file); pages.test.js (PAGES.length 12→13, the primary list, the tool-chrome list dog/otter/rabbit/moth, the subtitle list, the dark list, 13 '<PageSurface id="'); PageSurface.test.jsx (ORDER of 13; the ownMain and only-DOG/OTTER-draw-<main> scan, since Moth draws no <main>); pageBars.test.js (RESTING gains moth; the Home stack budget for seven items); enterGenerates.test.js; rabbitCssGuards classReach (it already parses lazy(() => import())). New lazy-chunk vitest: no file outside src/tools/moth_v0.1.0 statically imports from it, and App.jsx reaches it only through lazy(import()). A planted static import turns it red. The bundle is measured before and after, and the main chunk must not grow by the Moth code.
WHAT IT SHOWS (src/tools/moth_v0.1.0/: MothPage.jsx, state/mothFileOps.js, state/mothHistory.js, state/mothEligibility.js (mothFileHidden), components/MothIslands.jsx, MothPersonalPanel.jsx, MothMoveTo.jsx, MothTrashView.jsx). On first entry, signed in: adapter.ensurePersonalSpace() (acceptance 1: exactly one personal space; a second entry returns the same id), then listMothIslands(). The island list shows projects the caller can see, with no counts. The caller's own space is labelled 'My personal space' (is_mine), and another person's space never appears, admins included (acceptance 2; the admin reaches it only from Files, which writes WIL-4114). Desktop-only projects are labelled 'On this computer only', read-only, with no live features. Opening an island lists its files from listMothFiles, followed in pages with no totals, and every row passes mothFileHidden. A name search filters only the rows already loaded, which the server has already stripped of money and Legal, so search, selection, trash and the list cannot show finance or Legal (acceptance 5).
WHAT IT DOES. Add files in the personal panel: Petal and s3 companies through uploadFile; office-server companies from the desktop through the personal-space route plus record_added, with the answer-3 sentence in the dialog; the browser shows 'Add files to your personal space from the desktop app'. Move to: the picker reads moth_islands rows with can_write. It runs planFileMove; does the physical step for rehome, copy_up or share_move (copyBodyForMove, a verified share rename via the op route); commits with moveFiles; rolls the physical step back on a refused commit; and calls noteFilesChangedElsewhere, so the Files page agrees and the file still opens (acceptance 3). Between two desktop-only projects it uses moveFilesBetweenProjects. Delete: always asks first, then trashFiles (cloud), the local trash route, or the office-server trash plus bin rename. Undo (Ctrl+Z/Ctrl+Y while on Moth, and the toast) and Restore from MothTrashView (cloud rows from moth_trash_index plus local rows per local project, a purge countdown, 'Could not look' kept apart from 'Nothing here') recover the exact file. Local and cloud are checked by sha1 in tests (acceptance 4). Every control is a GatedAction driven by canOnProject('project.entity.write', ctx from the island row, ready). A reviewer sees Move to, Delete and Restore greyed with the reason from projectActionDeniedReason, and no adapter write is made (acceptance 6). A Moth source scan forbids .from('projects'|'files'|'folders'), listPersonalSpaces, listFiles, listFolders, listManagedFiles, loadProject, listProjects, listProjectMembers and projectsIndex under the Moth folder. The undo stack is in memory, capped at 10 each way, cleared on account, workspace or adapter change, and survives R.A.B.B.I.T. project switches.

*As built, §1.8 to §1.10 (2026-11-06, `ec2ee469`, `c01a4917`, `fe04c989`, `5a9cb697`, `acde241b`; not yet read by a review round):* **Beyond the text.** (a) The add route takes who is calling from the call's bearer token, asked of the auth server, and the page's push must agree; the build writes the public URL and anon key to `dist/auth-public.json`, because the main process cannot read the renderer's compile-time constants and a value from the page would not do. (b) `op` reserves a target with an exclusive create and renames over only that empty reservation, as the add route does, so two movers cannot both win; `put` copies to a dot-led temp file first, so a walk never lists a part. (c) A folder moves only if it is one frame series, because a catalogue row can be one and nothing else that is a folder is what a row asked about; `purge` holds to the same. (d) `shareContain` refuses a path whose real path differs from the written one, so a link inside the drive is refused as well as one leading out. (e) A retire's journal is `moves/retire-<op>.json` beside the move journals, and `recoverMoves` leaves those to `localMedia.cjs`. (f) `folderRootRefusal` refuses `_WILSON_PERSONAL` anywhere in a project's folder. (g) A frame folder over 5,000 frames has its size scaled from the first 5,000: told, not certified. (h) The office-server provider's `put` takes `opts.from`, and its `del` empties only a bin entry; a live row is deleted through the trash. (i) The three exact-name storage checks ask one HEAD, as §4 owed since 0115's measurement. (j) A move's copy carries the file's picture to the key the plan minted; one that will not copy leaves the row without a picture and the move goes on. (k) The local adapter's `mothHiddenFacts` tells the page what a row cannot say on the signed-out desktop (a receipt by its expense, a folder's chain, the old money folders), and a failure to read it shows nothing; a desktop key whose first segment is a money name never leaves `listMothFiles`.

**Not built, and owed by the whole-session rounds or the walkthrough:** the `?storage=network` dev fixture; the Storage page's list of projects whose folder is outside the drive or overlaps another (it has the count and the sentence); Move to from Files (Moth has it); the personal-space office-server sentence in Files (Moth's panel has it); managed rows in the desktop trash view (`POST …/managed-files/:id/restore` and the provider's mutator are there, and Undo calls them). `thumbnails.js`'s refusal 'row' needed nothing: no client code tells 'row' from 'project'. **`runMigration`'s sentence on *This folder is already used...*:** the server's own sentence reaches the report as written, so nothing was added.

*As corrected before the second whole-session round (2026-11-06).* **A desktop project.** The trash asks the disk where a body is, so a crash or a file held open cannot strand one. `isMothExcluded` is the page's own rule: a money first segment, a money slug directly under the root, the three names and the old folders beside the project's slug, with a test holding it to `moneyFolderChainLocked`. A file's own restore does not ask it, because it is Undo from Files, which deleted the file unasked; the batch routes and the trash list do. A purge writes only when something went, without touching the project's date. **A move between projects** puts the bodies back on a failure it sees, and after a crash rolls back when the destination holds no row, as §1.11 asks, and forward when it does, always from the row as it is then. It takes turns, copies and hashes in a worker (`copyInWorker.cjs`), refuses a folder that is away, and its journal is durable. `GET /api/rabbit/files/pending-moves` is what the page reads to say one did not finish. A retire's journal cannot name a path. Archive and clear keeps each project's `.trash` beside the archive. **The office server.** `isShareRoot` is one question, asked by every gate, and refuses a device path and a money folder as the root. A write in a personal folder and a walk of one ask the bearer token. A rename that threw is looked at before anything is removed, and an empty file at the target with the source still there is a leftover reservation. A copy runs in a worker, and every other call takes the contained slots. `GET /api/network-share/reachable` is asked before anything is written. **The client.** A copy-up settles with what it read off the server. A rename nobody can say happened stays pending, and `settlePending` runs on opening Moth and before a check. A long walk stops short of the server's limit, and its last call is final and says where it stopped. Calls are 500 entries, and smaller again when the database runs out of time. `folder_root` is in the project list. **The page.** Delete asks first and counts what the search has hidden. A failed read offers Try again, and coming back reads again. Ctrl+Z is taken before any other listener, so a hidden page's own undo does not also run. A toast's Undo is for its own step. A step that failed for a reason that may pass stays. `LazyPageBoundary` holds a page that will not load or throws to its own surface.

*As corrected after the second whole-session round (2026-11-07).* **A desktop project.** A copy between drives goes to `.wilson-moving-<leaf>` beside its target and is renamed when proved, so a file at the real name is whole whenever a crash came; recovery sweeps those, and with a body at both ends compares: the same bytes lose one copy, and two different files keep both and leave the journal, because neither is WILSON's to throw away. **The office server.** Renames of one locator take turns on a computer, and only an empty target over ten minutes old is removed: a young one may be someone's rename landing, so that operation stays pending. A far end that cannot be read is not called empty. A copy-up with nothing at its target is failed only after a day. Calling off a move looks first on the desktop, through a new look route that answers for bin paths too. A personal folder's files are read by their owner alone. The drive's root refuses this computer's loopback names, a Linux or remote-session disk, an administrative share and the personal parent; **this computer's own name is not refused**, because a studio's one shared folder may be on the desk it runs on. **The page.** Only a refusal that will say the same again drops an Undo step. Moth shows the office-server banner.

**Not taken from round 2, and why.** A file in the trash of a desktop project that is money or Legal has only the toast's Undo, then goes at 30 days: it used to be unlinked at once, so this is no loss, and a money trash is a screen of its own. A locked name at a second segment passes the page and the routes alike, and the office server's stricter list stays stricter. The move route's name_taken counts hidden rows: the file is on disk under that name either way. `fileMoves.cjs` and `fileTrash.cjs` make synchronous calls that a desktop project's folder on a share could hold for the OS timeout, as every desktop route in `main.cjs` does. Links a server resolves itself are invisible to the checks (needs a real NAS to measure, and to decide). `op` then `purge` from script on the page can remove any file a member could rename: closing it needs main to ask the database, as round 1's `rabbit:copy-file` does. A demoted admin's claim lasts up to an hour, as every claim in the app does. A pending move whose origin project has been purged closes with its row live at the far end: the next check says it was not found, which is the honest answer, and the certificate holds the rest. **A re-home is not weighed against the plan:** the old copy counts until drained, so a nearly full company can overshoot during a move, which adds nothing once drained; refusing a move for that would be worse, so §1.15's "a plan-limit refusal is said plainly" does not hold for a move.

**Still not built, as of this note:** a way to empty a project's desktop trash before its 30 days (the panel says so); a row-level way to move within a project on the office server (it is refused with a sentence: the next check follows a file moved on the server); an admin moving a former member's file out of their folder on the office server. **To measure on a real server:** `shareContain`'s and the walk's real-path comparison on a DFS path, where a child on another target may not share the root's prefix.

**Measured.** Moth's chunk 16,657 bytes. The main chunk 5,031,797 bytes at `ec2ee469` and 5,045,514 with this, 13,717 more, which is the adapter and the provider's client; no Moth page code is in it (a scan holds that). Home's seven selections 408 px, inside the 444 budget. The page was opened on the dev fixtures in a browser: the list, a project's files and Move to's dialog drew, with no errors of its own. **Nothing here has run against a real office server, or against wilson-dev signed in:** the routes ran on real files and junctions, the client on fakes. The whole vitest set: 11,527 passed and 9 skipped, the failures under load (the same files green alone).

### 1.11 Tests and planted controls

- **supabase/tests/rls/110_private_project_write_hops.sql, 111_private_project_money_and_history.sql, 112_private_project_channels.sql**: As the repairs design. Per-table move-in, move-out and delete probes through filter-less statements. Guard pins, the flip rule, producer seating and the epoch bump. Same-project refusals answer like a random uuid. Money gate and storage money paths are closed to a non-creator manager seat. Per-type capture probes. Cross-project rows are admin-only, and private-era rows are creator-and-admin only. Workspace and project topic shape and AUDIENCE probes, plus project_topic_matches, which bites on CI. PLANTED: each hop removed; the owner trigger removed; a broad second SELECT policy on projects; one dependency endpoint dropped; a temp moth_zz table with a bare hop (S6); a map entry dropped; the privacy arm in the money gate dropped; the captured_private arm dropped; the is_private side test dropped; the side comparison in fn_realtime_broadcast dropped.
- **supabase/tests/rls/80_private_projects, 01_projects, 18_project_members, 25_app_events, 84 (extended); 113_moth_read_path.sql (new)**: One personal space per caller; idempotent; the ON CONFLICT path; owner and admin read it; another member, a manager and another workspace's admin read 0 rows; direct INSERT, owner change, bin, flip and slug changes refused; seat guard (including via producer auto-staff); an admin's writes, trash and upload signing refused, while the owner's succeed; a local_server row in a personal project refused; WIL-4114 written for an admin and refused for everyone else. Moth read path: admin false on another's space, folder and file; money, INVOICES, FINANCE, LEGAL keys, the legal tag, own-project receipts and locked folders false for every role; an expense in project B does not hide project A's file; a deep 'Legal' working folder stays visible; a foreign folder_id comes back NULL; MATRIX rows. PLANTED: unique index dropped; personal arm removed from can_write_project; provider rule removed from the guard; seat guard removed; the admin leg added to the personal arm; can_access_project_money swapped in; the expense project equality dropped; the folder walk dropped.
- **supabase/tests/rls/114_file_moves.sql (new), 05_files, 52_folders (extended)**: FORCE on, zero rows and every write refused for authenticated, no privilege for anon, created_by equals the caller. A cross-project folder_id is refused by the composite FK; a folder delete still nulls folder_id; a patch resending the same project_id passes. A planted definer that updates project_id without a journal row is refused. PLANTED: the single-column FK restored; the txid binding dropped from fn_checked_move_admits.
- **supabase/tests/rls/115_stable_file_bodies.sql (new), 61_workspace_storage_s3, 63, 66, 33, 78, 79, 80, 81, 83, 90, 95 (re-pointed and extended), 35_platform_audit**: As the bodies design. A moved file is readable by the destination only; foreign workspace and reused-id refusals; trashed and purged copies unreadable; delete_own is false on a claimed key; client changes of each guarded column give 42501 while identical resends pass; INSERT refusals, one each, with the same sentence for a money key whether or not the object exists; rekey refusals and its compare-and-set; the s3 namespace index; can_presign_object; meter arms. The role-GUC probe: inside a client-called definer the guard treats the call as bound. A text probe pins the functions naming each marker literal. PLANTED: 0042's prefix hop restored; claimed re-scoped to the caller's workspace; arm 3 dropped; current_project made a definer; NOT claimed dropped; the file_id filter dropped; the trigger dropped; admissible made true; the s3 namespace index dropped; the guard switched to a session_user test (the definer probe goes red).
- **supabase/tests/rls/116_network_share_checks.sql, 117_network_share_ops.sql, 118_network_share_catalogue.sql (new); 96, 60, 33, 20, 95, 99, 100, 101, 104 (extended)**: As the NAS design. The 13-term vocabulary; 'moved' not written on a project change; the broadcast check early return and its thin trigger; md5 sets updated. Generic soft_delete_row on an office-server row refused, while network_share_trash succeeds and sets the bin path; settle 'not_moved' restores the row; restore when occupied refused; a purge certificate says 'company server bin' and the id then appears in purge_ok; storage_gc_queue unchanged; the personal folder claim cannot be nested into. PLANTED: as in the NAS design, plus moving the trash core so it bypasses the op marker.
- **supabase/tests/rls/119_moth_moves.sql, 120_moth_trash.sql (new)**: Metadata move from shared A to B clears the seven links and the core flag, writes one journal row, exactly one moved_out and one moved_in with NULL paths, no B-readable row naming A's private id, one edit_history row with NULL project and one moth_history 'move' (once 0119 lands). A move from a personal space re-homes: fresh key under B, old copy queued (file-purged), and no B-readable row or event names the personal id. local_server gives copy_up and is refused into another private project (only_on_a_computer). An office-server item gives a pending op, and 'not_moved' reverses it. Each hidden cause returns not_found with the text of a random uuid (invoice, receipt, expense-linked file, file under INVOICES/x, Legal file, another person's personal space for an admin, a private project for a manager, another project's folder, a trashed source). A reviewer gets not_allowed_source. Three good files plus one money file leaves all four unchanged. Undo is exact; redo works; another user gets not_found; a second undo gives already_undone; after a re-move, changed_since; an undo that would need a copy gives needs_copy. Trash: a batch with one invoice trashes nothing, even for an admin; the index hides money, Legal, others' personal and trashed-project rows, carries no locator columns and sets needs_desktop for office-server rows. PLANTED: the personal arm removed from the re-home rule (the id-leak probe goes red); 'moved' allowed to fire on a project change; the membership guard removed; the eligibility call removed from the trash index.
- **supabase/tests/rls/121_moth_history.sql (new)**: As the tables design, including the 12 planted controls. The preflight names fn_projects_owner_guard. A move made through moth_move_items writes one 'move' row. An office-server catalogue insert is captured with missing=true.
- **electron/fileTrash.test.js, fileMoves.test.js, networkShare.test.js, rootReachable.test.js, openPath.test.js, projectFilePatch.test.js, personal-space route tests, runMigrationBins.test.js**: Trash then restore gives a deep-equal row and the same sha1 (today's unlink turns it red). Both crash windows recover. Containment holds against '..\\evil'. The 30-day purge runs only through POST. Without the token: 401. A move between local projects lands the same sha1 in the destination and leaves nothing in the source; a crash after the destination write rolls forward, and before it rolls back; a cross-volume copy is verified; a managed file is refused; an excluded row gives 404. Share routes refuse cross-origin and same-site requests, drive-letter roots, money and dot locators, and overwrites. No sync fs runs on the main thread during a walk or GET. The personal space is one per OS user, a copied data root carries none, DELETE and PATCH give 409, and archive-and-clear leaves it.
- **vitest: storagePresignBoundary, readUrls, teardownCollect, storageGcShotTwins, supabaseAdapter (updateFile, rewriteFileRow, copyBodyForMove, moveFiles, listTrashedFiles, personal routing, missingFunction), runMigration, config.toml pin (rabbit_private never exposed), reservedObjects and segment-allowlist lockstep, realtimeMerge, RabbitProvider audience and undo (fileVerbsProvider.test.jsx), mountRabbit and projectAudience, TeamMembersPage, WorkspaceTakeout, ProjectFilesExplorer (mounted fixtures), ProjectDetailPanel, projectRoleMatrix (MATRIX parity), writeGate (Moth folder), Moth tests (mothHistory, mothFileOps, MothMoveTo, MothTrashView, the Moth source scan, the lazy-chunk scan, the mothFileHidden migration-text parity), pages, PageSurface, pageBars**: Each new test is shown red on the pre-change source first, with a planted control. The reviewer's Delete is greyed with a reason and no adapter call happens. The admin fixture never sees another person's space in Moth, and sees it read-only in Files with one record call. Full run with --maxWorkers=4 and no .env.local.

- **docs/PRODUCTION_RUNBOOK.md (deployment inventory)**: Migrations 0107 to 0120 with their order and preflight queries; the enum migration (0112) committed before 0114 to 0117 are rehearsed; Edge Functions (storage-presign, storage-read-urls with _shared/readUrls.ts, storage-gc, operator-workspaces with teardownCollect) deployed after 0115, live state read from the functions list plus a source comparison; 0120 only after storage-presign is verified, never rolled back below it once any s3 row's prefix differs from its project_id (check query recorded); never expose rabbit_private in api.schemas; the three cron jobs; the off-hours apply of 0119; rollback in reverse.
- **docs/sessions/PLAN_MOTH.md §2, §4, §5 (controller's record)**: Answer 6 applied. Session 1 creates file_moves, network_share_checks, network_share_ops and moth_history. Section 4 suite numbers are assigned at creation. RLS_TABLES target 65. moth_local_scopes and file_body_locations dropped. Session 2's database line: views and layouts, with history from Session 1.
- **docs/OUTSTANDING.md**: Closed and opened per outstanding_entries, with a session-log row at the top. HTML comment markers counted after the commit: 4 and 4.
- **WILSON/src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/db/README.md, docs/walkthroughs (the Moth Session 1 walkthrough in plain words), the hand-off po-moth1-<date>.md**: The rules recorded above. The walkthrough states: signed URLs minted before a move stay valid until they expire; the office-server list shows what the checker could open; a re-homed copy counts toward storage until the old copy is drained.

No uuid-shaped values allowed in config; every hop through moth_can_read_target and moth_can_write_target. Local Moth state waits on her answer about one-computer layouts.

### 1.14 OUTSTANDING entries this session opens or closes

- **Closed: private-project write hops and workspace-channel suppression** (A-R1-04, A-R2-03, C-R2-01 (remaining bullet), S4d-01, S4d-02): Fixed by 0107 to 0109. Write hops on every clause, the owner guard, same-project references, the money gate's privacy arm, the history audience, workspace-channel suppression, the per-project split and the topic epoch. Suites 110 to 112.
- **Closed for claimed keys: storage reads by key prefix and the client-writable locator** (OSP-01, OSP-07, S4c-12): 0114 and 0115 plus the storage-presign and read-urls changes. Authority follows files.project_id for every key a row claims. Unclaimed keys keep the legacy rule. Clients cannot change a file's project, folder or locator.
- **Join-cache narrowing by role demotion or membership deactivation** (MO1-01): INFERRED and pre-existing. A subscriber who joined a project topic before being demoted or deactivated may keep hearing it until their token expires. The epoch handles privacy flips only. Settle it with a wilson-dev measurement: join, demote, write, observe.
- **Storage totals include personal-space bytes** (S4b-06 (added line)): Closed by 0121, her integration answer 3: members see only full or nearly full, and the figures are an admin's.
- **Admin reads of personal spaces through raw REST are not recorded** (MO1-02): WIL-4114 records every open in WILSON's Files page. RLS cannot log SELECTs, so an admin reading through the REST API directly leaves no record. Recorded in the handbook's deviations table.
- **Stale admin desktop builds list others' personal spaces in R.A.B.B.I.T.** (MO1-03): No minimum-version mechanism exists (grep). The exposure is read-only and no wider than Files: writes are owner-only at the database and local bodies are refused. It ends when the build updates.
- **Purged moved copy and the double-swallow window** (MO1-04): If both the queue enqueue and the purge certificate fail on the same purge, a purged moved copy falls back to its origin's readers until the next orphan scan removes the object. Two independent arms, pinned separately.
- **Same-project receipt oracle in Moth** (MO1-05): Moth hides a file that its own project's expense names, which tells a project member that the file is a receipt. They can already open it. There is no indicator or count. Recorded beside 0076 TRAP 1.
- **Office-server leftovers** (MO1-06): (a) Bin items of a project purged whole are never walked again and stay on the server; the certificate says 'company server bin' and the Admin Storage page notes it. (b) A rename made on the server and split across walk calls gets a new id. (c) Pre-C4 receipts in <slug>_FILES on the share carry no flag (handbook §12.9 residual 3). (d) Cross-project pending operations whose creator's laptop never returns are settled by an operator through service_role.
- **Local trash purge runs only on open** (MO1-07): Local Server trash bodies of a project nobody opens again linger past 30 days. Purge-expired runs on project open and when the trash view opens.
- **ingestion_chunks select policy hops through the run's project only** (MO1-08): Measured while designing moves. A file with extracted text is refused cross-project (has_extracted_text), so moves add no leak. The policy gap itself is recorded for a later session.
- **Asset-trash bodies on the desktop** (MO1-09): Managed files of a deleted asset are excluded from local restore. The asset's own .trash bodies are not yet listed in a trash view.

### 1.15 Residual risks

- The bound-caller test assumes the role GUC still reads 'authenticated' inside a client-called SECURITY DEFINER function (soft_delete_row, ensure_personal_space, the move core).
  - *Mitigation:* 0049 already reads the role GUC in a trigger. Suite 115 has a probe where a definer called as a persona is treated as bound. If the rehearsal shows otherwise, stop and report before 0110: the personal no-binning arm and the marker arm need another binding.
- Four restatements of fn_projects_owner_guard, three of fn_realtime_broadcast (0098→0109→0116) and two of fn_file_events_capture across one session.
  - *Mitigation:* Each restates the latest with an md5 or comment-stripped preflight. Post-conditions name every earlier arm. The md5 pin suites (100, 101, 95) are updated in the same commit.
- Per-object policy cost: every listed, signed or read object now runs a claimed check, and an invoker files lookup when claimed.
  - *Mitigation:* Every lookup is indexed. EXPLAIN ANALYZE before and after on dev is recorded in the hand-off.
- Physical steps (copy-up upload, share rename, local move) cannot share the cloud transaction.
  - *Mitigation:* Office-server operations are server-first with pending ops and pending_mine. Supabase and s3 copies are uploaded before the commit, and an orphan upload is removed by delete_own or the orphan scan. Local moves use the durable journal with roll-forward or roll-back at boot. A refused commit rolls the physical step back.
- Re-homing copies bytes (large videos leaving a personal space or a private project's computer), and on Petal the copy counts toward quota until the old copy is drained.
  - *Mitigation:* Only re-home and copy-up items copy. Progress is shown, and a plan-limit refusal is said plainly. Whether a storage copy passes reserve_upload_bytes is measured at build.
- Client writes in suites 05, 33, 78, 79, 80, 81, 83, 90 and 95 are now refused, and old clients (refile, moveReceipt) fail during the deploy window.
  - *Mitigation:* Re-point them in the 0114 commit after measuring every suite that writes files as a client role. Old-client failures are graceful and named.
- Composite FK with ON DELETE SET NULL (folder_id) needs PostgreSQL 15 or later.
  - *Mitigation:* server_version is measured on every environment before 0113. If it is older, stop and report (the repairs guard still covers folder_id).
- Signed URLs minted before a move or trash stay valid until they expire (up to an hour).
  - *Mitigation:* Stated in the walkthrough and handbook. Live audience channels for Moth come in later sessions.
- The office-server list reflects the checker's server permissions.
  - *Mitigation:* Team managers only (or the space owner), as her answer 1 of the integration's four settled. Directories the checker cannot open are counted as incomplete. Plain sentences on Storage and Files.
- Several channel and realtime probes are vacuous on CI.
  - *Mitigation:* Body-structure and project_topic_matches probes bite on CI. The rolled-back wilson-dev rehearsal, with its planted-control results, is mandatory and recorded.

### 1.16 Questions the integration put to her

All four were asked and answered (section 0).

## 2. The six areas as resolved (the design review's record)

Each area's design after both reviews: every finding with its verdict and the evidence from the code, then the revised design that section 1 integrates. Where section 1 and an area differ, section 1 wins.

### 2.1 Stable file bodies

Findings: 2 partly, 20 taken.

**Scope as resolved:** STABLE FILE BODIES (revised after both reviews and her six answers). A file changes project by association; its stored copy stays at the key it was made under, and authority over that copy follows the file's current project. Exceptions, from her answers and standing ruling: a body that lives on one computer, or whose key names a private project or personal space, is copied to a fresh key under the destination as part of the move (re-homed); a desktop-only project's body moves with the file on the same disk; an office-server file is moved by the office-server writer.

**Summary:** I read every finding against the code. All 22 findings change the design: 19 taken, 3 partly. Six things changed. (1) Any project can now see that a stored copy is claimed, whichever company claims it, but only the file's own readers can open it. Clients can no longer re-use a gone project's id when that id is tied to another company (SEC-1). (2) In a company's own s3 bucket, bodies and pictures share one set of names. A unique expression index now gives each name at most one owner (SEC-2). (3) The two claim records that outlive a deleted row are narrowed. One is a purge-queue row that names a file and has not been drained yet. The other is a purge certificate of a moved body. This fixes repeat desktop-to-cloud carries, bin posters and reserved objects (SFB-01, SFB-02). (4) Her standing ruling (no hidden ids) and her answer 1 (copy up from the holding computer) lead to one mechanism, re-homing. A file whose stored copy is on one computer, or whose key names a private project or a personal space other than the destination, is copied by the client to a fresh key under the destination. The checked move then switches the row and queues the old copy (SEC-6). (5) The guard refuses client changes to seven columns. It also requires a transaction marker for any server-role change made through the API, so a future definer fails closed (SEC-11, SFB-11). (6) Moving s3 files between projects stays switched off until the new storage-presign is confirmed deployed (SEC-4). There is still no new table. I align with the moves area: I adopt its move RPC (moth_move_items with a p_rehome argument), its 'refiled' event, its realtime split and its GUC names, and drop my draft's move_files and moved_out/moved_in. Helper functions move to a schema the API does not expose. Process note: to read the JSON I wrote one scratch file, bodies_dump.txt, in the session scratchpad. No repository file was touched.

#### Findings and their dispositions

- **SEC-1 [blocker] — taken.** storage_object_claimed decides across ALL workspaces. storage_object_current_project still runs under the caller's files_select, so another company's member gets NULL and is refused. New trg_projects_id_reuse_guard (BEFORE INSERT, client roles) refuses an id that has no projects row and that file_events or a files key ties to ANOTHER workspace. Same-workspace re-carries still pass, so runMigration keeps working; forcing gen_random_uuid() would have broken it. Probe and planted control 3 inverted; new probe: a W2 project re-taking a purged W1 id reads 0 rows of W1's moved body.
  - *Evidence:* 0013_project_members.sql projects_insert checks workspace, membership and app role, not id. supabaseAdapter.createProject sanitizes 'id' out, but runMigration.js l.2962 insertOrSkip('projects', row) sends the desktop id, and cloudProjectThere (l.973) already treats 'an id another workspace holds' as a real case. 0042 rabbit_files_select falls back to EXISTS projects p WHERE p.id = seg2 under projects_select. 0027 file_events has no FK on project_id ('purged rows are deletion certificates and must survive their subject'), so the tie survives.
- **SEC-2 [blocker] — taken.** New UNIQUE INDEX files_s3_namespace_uidx ON files ((regexp_replace(storage_path,'(\.jpg)+$',''))) WHERE storage_provider='s3', so X, X.jpg and X.jpg.jpg cannot belong to two rows (race-free). Admissibility and rekey check the queue and certificates for both p_path and p_path||'.jpg'. storage_object_current_project returns NULL unless exactly one row matches. Preflight counts collisions. Suite 61 gets probes for both directions, with a planted control that drops the index.
  - *Evidence:* storage/thumbnails.js thumbnailKeyFor = storagePath + '.jpg', and an s3 picture is put beside its body in the same bucket. _shared/readUrls.ts classifyOwned adds bodies[storage_path] and thumbs[thumbnail_url when it equals path+'.jpg'] with no cross-row check. The draft's admissibility checked uniqueness on storage_path alone.
- **SEC-3 [blocker] — taken.** The contract is now exact, and both owners agree. The moves area restates the files arm: a project change sends DELETE with OLD on the old topic and INSERT with NEW and NULL old_record on the new one, after the money return. The history/repairs area makes edit_history show a cross-project files diff only to a reader in both sides' audience, or strips project_id, folder_id and storage_path from such diffs. moth_history never stores locators. The moves migration's post-condition asserts both realtime arms and the capture shape, comments stripped, so the RPC cannot exist without them.
  - *Evidence:* 0098_table_views.sql fn_realtime_broadcast files arm: v_project from the row's project_id, one broadcast_changes on that topic carrying NEW and OLD. 0092 fn_edit_history_money_snapshot stores one NEW.project_id per diff, and edit_history_select has no privacy arm (measurement fact 7).
- **SEC-4 [major] — taken.** rabbit_private.s3_body_moves_on() is created returning false. The body-follow step refuses a cross-project move of an s3 row while it is false ('s3_moves_off', sentence: 'moving files between projects on your company's bucket switches on with the next update'). A one-function migration restates it as true only after the new storage-presign is verified deployed on that environment (functions list plus a source comparison). The runbook says storage-presign is never rolled back below that version once any s3 row has project_id other than its key prefix, and gives the query that checks it.
  - *Evidence:* storage-presign/index.ts l.135-137 picks can_presign_project_read/_write and calls it with shape.projectId (the key prefix). 0051 can_presign_project_read is workspace plus membership on the prefix project only.
- **SEC-5 [major] — partly.** Every project check in rabbit_private.file_key_admissible, rekey_file_body and files_body_follow is now: workspace = current_workspace_id() AND p.deleted_at IS NULL AND passes_project_privacy AND can_write_project. Probes: a non-owner member is refused rekey on a personal-space file; anyone is refused on a trashed project's file. Dependency recorded: personal ⇒ is_private CHECK.
  - *Evidence:* 0082 passes_project_privacy checks only NOT is_private OR created_by OR admin, with no deleted_at; 0072 projects_select has deleted_at IS NULL. The personal-space area keeps is_private = true on every personal space (projects_personal_shape_chk), so the privacy arm already covers personal spaces. The 'unstaffed personal space open to all' scenario does not arise.
- **SEC-6 [major] — taken.** Decided under her ruling; no question needed. Re-home (SB-12): a cross-project move whose key's second segment names a private project or personal space other than the destination, or whose provider is local_server, needs a fresh copy under the destination's prefix, made by the client. The move switches the row in the same UPDATE and queues the old copy. A shared origin's id stays in the key: everyone in the workspace can already see that project. Recorded: a shared origin later trashed still shows its id.
  - *Evidence:* 0092 files_select returns the whole row, storage_path included. uploadFile keys are projects/<pid>/<seg>/<entity>/<ts>-<name> (uploadContainerFor). Private-project non-money bodies are all local_server (supabaseAdapter l.3481). Personal-space bodies go to company storage (personal area, projectIsPrivate change). A project made private later keeps earlier supabase keys under its id. Her standing ruling: no ids that reveal existence.
- **SEC-7 [minor] — taken.** The money/Legal arm (can_access_project_money, or Legal plus can_access_project_legal) is checked first, right after caller rights and before any existence probe. The guard therefore refuses with one sentence whether or not an object exists. The function is also moved out of the API (SFB-10).
  - *Evidence:* Draft order in rabbit_file_key_admissible: rights, shape, allowlist (with invoices/legal), uniqueness, queue, object existence, all before any money gate. 0092 files_insert's money arm runs after BEFORE triggers.
- **SEC-8 [minor] — taken.** Every new definer that reads files, storage_gc_queue or file_events is declared SET row_security = off, so a role subject to RLS gets an error instead of an empty read. A post-condition checks that each function's owner has rolbypassrls.
  - *Evidence:* 0027 storage_gc_queue has RLS on with zero policies. A definer whose owner lacked BYPASSRLS would read zero rows and answer 'unclaimed' (fail open).
- **SEC-9 [minor] — taken.** Move events never write NULL details. Adopting the moves area's 'refiled' capture, details are always a jsonb object. Suite 33 counts exactly two events per cross-project move against the real constraint.
  - *Evidence:* 0027 file_events.details JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'. fn_file_events_capture swallows every exception (0074 handler), so a NULL details would silently drop the whole trail.
- **SEC-10 [minor] — taken.** files_body_follow refuses ('body_shape') any source whose key fails the shape and segment allowlist: bin_files, connection-check, finance, PROJECT.json, too few segments. The preflight reports how many such rows there are.
  - *Evidence:* 0091 petal_bin_posters_insert/_update are PERMISSIVE on projects/<seg2>/bin_files/* through can_edit_shot_lists(seg2) and OR with the base policies. The draft guard checked shape on INSERT only.
- **SEC-11 [minor] — taken.** The guard now requires a transaction-local marker for any server-role change through the API (session_user not postgres/supabase_admin) to project_id, folder_id (except to NULL), workspace_id (never) or locator columns. An unknown definer therefore fails closed. Probes: a planted definer called as authenticated that UPDATEs project_id is refused. A text probe lists the functions whose body names each marker literal and pins the set.
  - *Evidence:* 0014 soft_delete_row/restore_soft_deleted use EXECUTE format('UPDATE public.%I …'). A text scan for 'project_id =' cannot see dynamic SQL.
- **SFB-01 [major] — taken.** Arm 2 = queue rows with file_id IS NOT NULL AND status IN ('pending','failed'): a purged row's copy not yet removed. Arm 3 = 'purged' certificates whose project_id differs from the key's second segment (moved bodies only). After the drain, or for an unmoved purge, the key is free again, so a deterministic re-carry works as it does today. New pgTAP and vitest: orphan sweep, then re-carry succeeds, with a planted control (arm 2 without status/file_id) that bites.
  - *Evidence:* runMigration cloudObjectPathFor builds projects/${pid}/${segment}/${f.id}/${safeName} with no timestamp. storage-gc's orphan scan removes the object, then certify() inserts a queue row (reason orphan-scan, status deleted, no file_id). In the draft, arms 2 and 3 claimed a key forever.
- **SFB-02 [major] — taken.** Arm 2's file_id IS NOT NULL plus its status filter exclude poster and orphan-scan rows and skipped reserved rows. storage_object_claimed also returns false outright for row-less shapes: third segment bin_files or connection-check, projects/<id>/PROJECT.json, projects/<id>/FINANCE/RATES.json. These are kept in lockstep with _shared/reservedObjects.ts by vitest. Probe: replace a poster, restore the older key, and the poster still reads; a planted control drops the file_id filter.
  - *Evidence:* 0091 fn_bin_files_gc_enqueue queues the old poster with file_id NULL, also on poster_path change ('a restore brings an older key back'). Poster reads go through rabbit_thumbnails_select. The storage-gc drain marks reserved rows 'skipped' and keeps them.
- **SFB-03 [major] — taken.** files_body_follow refuses ('body_missing') a supabase source with no storage.objects row in rabbit-files at storage_path. Sentence: 'this file's stored copy is still being tidied — open its project once, then move it'. Because no row with a missing body can move, storage-gc's twin change becomes defence in depth. Probe plus a planted control.
  - *Evidence:* supabaseAdapter rewriteFileRow/moveObjectVerified: an interrupted re-file leaves the object at the nested key while the row still names the old key. filesUnderPrefix filters .eq('project_id'), and moved rows are never re-keyed.
- **SFB-04 [major] — taken.** The re-point list is 05, 33, 78, 79, 80, 81, 83, 90 and 95. 87 is checked; it touches tags only, which the guard allows. The build re-measures every suite that writes public.files as a client role (all 14 that write files), seeds storage.objects or inserts as postgres, keeps CHECK-code probes as postgres twins, and recounts plans. The list goes in the hand-off.
  - *Evidence:* Role-tracking scan of supabase/tests/rls: files INSERT/UPDATE as authenticated in 05, 33, 78, 79 (3 ins, 6 upd), 80 (local_server insert), 81 (2 ins), 83, 87 (tags only), 90, 95. The scan is a heuristic: role resets were matched by pattern.
- **SFB-05 [minor] — partly.** With re-home, a destination-visible path never names a hidden project. Either the key's origin is a shared project or it is a fresh destination key. The source's 'refiled' row carries old_path and the destination's carries new_path only, which the destination already sees on the files row. A re-homed UPDATE must NOT also fire the plain 'moved' arm. The probe is restated as 'no B-readable file_events or files row names a private or personal project's id'. The argument against design B is withdrawn.
  - *Evidence:* 0074 'moved' arm writes OLD and NEW storage_path. The draft wrote old_path = new_path = NEW.storage_path into the destination's event.
- **SFB-06 [minor] — taken.** delete_own is pinned by its qual text, and the policy expression is evaluated in a SELECT as the uploader. The real refusal is covered in vitest through the Storage API. The purge probes run while A still exists, as an A-only reader, with and without the queue row. Hard-deleting A is a separate positive case.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/tests/rls/33_file_lifecycle.sql probe 44: storage.protect_delete() blocks direct deletes, so delete_own is pinned by text. With A hard-deleted, the prefix fallback already denies, so plant 2 would stay green.
- **SFB-07 [minor] — taken.** runMigration files a non-money row whose desktop folder is under INVOICES, FINANCE or LEGAL at the project's top level and says so in one sentence. Step 0 measures it on the desktop side. The guard's 42501 is then never the first refusal. Migration vitest added.
  - *Evidence:* migrationRows.js desktopFolderOfFile and cloudFolderOfFile; runMigration isMoney = f.is_financial OR receipts.has(id), not folder-based. The draft only reported such rows (NOTICE).
- **SFB-08 [minor] — taken.** Every queue row this area writes (legacy-picture detach, re-home retirement) uses reason 'file-purged', file_id = the file, kind body|thumbnail, and bucket/provider by 0054's CASE ('byo-s3'/'s3' for s3; otherwise 'rabbit-files' or 'rabbit-thumbnails'/'supabase'). Status is the default pending. No CHECK change.
  - *Evidence:* 0027 reason CHECK ('file-purged','orphan-scan','avatar-orphan'); 0054 kind/provider; 0091 comment ''file-purged' is the word'.
- **SFB-09 [minor] — taken.** When runMigration's upload is refused by row security, it still tries insertOrSkip. 23505 counts as skipped ('already in the cloud, perhaps in its bin'). Any other result counts as failed with its sentence. The list() effect is recorded in the trash behaviour change and in handbook §12.4/§17.
  - *Evidence:* runMigration ordinary-files loop: list(objectDir), then upload upsert:false, then insertOrSkip. Under SB-20 a trashed row's object is unlisted and its key claimed.
- **SFB-10 [minor] — taken.** New schema rabbit_private holds storage_object_claimed, storage_object_current_project, file_key_admissible, files_body_follow, files_body_retire, project_id_tied_elsewhere and s3_body_moves_on. It is not exposed. USAGE goes to authenticated and service_role; each function is REVOKEd from PUBLIC and anon, with EXECUTE granted to authenticated where policies or triggers call it. Suite 35 audits definers in public and rabbit_private. A vitest pins that config.toml's api schemas do not include rabbit_private; the runbook says never expose it.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/config.toml api schemas = public, storage, graphql_public. The only other schema is 'tests' (0005). 35_platform_audit only audits public definers for anon.
- **SFB-11 [minor] — taken.** Same as SEC-11: a behavioural marker gate plus a pinned list of the definers that name each marker literal.
  - *Evidence:* Same as SEC-11. The draft probe did not define what 'assigns' means.

#### Decisions

- **SB-1** No new table. files.storage_path (with its derived picture key storage_path||'.jpg') is the physical locator, minted once under projects/{creating project}/. Clients can never change it. files.project_id is the current association, and authority over the stored copy follows it.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: every body consumer resolves from the row (resolveFileProvider(row).getUrl, fn_files_gc_enqueue, storage-gc, teardown, runMigration). Her answer 6 has Session 1 create only what it needs, and this area needs no table.
- **SB-2** Indexes. files_storage_path_key_uidx: UNIQUE ON files (storage_path text_pattern_ops) WHERE storage_provider IN ('supabase','s3','local_server'). files_s3_namespace_uidx: UNIQUE ON files ((regexp_replace(storage_path,'(\.jpg)+$',''))) WHERE storage_provider='s3'. storage_gc_queue_claim_idx ON storage_gc_queue (bucket_id, object_path) WHERE file_id IS NOT NULL AND status IN ('pending','failed'). file_events_purged_path_idx ON file_events (old_path) WHERE event='purged'. file_events_project_any_idx ON file_events (project_id). files_moved_body_ws_idx ON files (workspace_id) WHERE storage_provider='supabase' AND lower(split_part(storage_path,'/',2)) <> project_id::text.
  - *Why:* storage_path has no index today (storage-gc comment). The s3 index closes SEC-2 with no race. Every lookup carries a matching provider predicate so the partial indexes serve it. network_share (NAS area) has its own per-workspace locator index.
- **SB-3** Schema rabbit_private, not exposed by the API, holds the body helpers (SFB-10). Each is SECURITY DEFINER unless stated, SET search_path = public, and SET row_security = off when it reads files, storage_gc_queue or file_events (SEC-8). Each is REVOKEd from PUBLIC and anon.
  - *Why:* The helpers must be callable from storage policies and the guard as authenticated, but never as an RPC oracle.
- **SB-4** (0115 adds an arm: a row's `thumbnail_url` under its own project claims that picture; 0115's note.) rabbit_private.storage_object_claimed(p_bucket text, p_name text) RETURNS boolean, STABLE, across ALL workspaces. False if p_name is NULL or a row-less shape (third segment bin_files or connection-check, ^projects/[^/]+/PROJECT\.json$, ^projects/[^/]+/FINANCE/RATES\.json$). Otherwise true at the first hit of: (1) a files row in any trash state that names it: rabbit-files: provider IN (supabase, local_server) AND storage_path = name; rabbit-thumbnails: name ends '.jpg' AND provider IN (supabase, local_server) AND storage_path = left(name,-4); byo-s3: provider s3 AND regexp_replace(storage_path,'(\.jpg)+$','') = regexp_replace(name,'(\.jpg)+$',''); (2) a storage_gc_queue row with bucket_id = p_bucket, object_path = name, file_id IS NOT NULL, status IN ('pending','failed'); (3) a file_events row with event 'purged', old_path = name (or name without '.jpg' for a picture bucket, both forms for byo-s3), a storage_provider that fits the bucket, and project_id IS DISTINCT FROM fn_try_uuid(split_part(old_path,'/',2)).
  - *Why:* SEC-1 (cross-tenant), SFB-01 and SFB-02 (narrowed claims). Two independent records still cover a purged moved copy until it is removed. Once removed, nothing remains to protect, and re-carries work.
- **SB-5** (0115 adds the same arm.) rabbit_private.storage_object_current_project(p_bucket text, p_name text) RETURNS uuid, SECURITY INVOKER, LANGUAGE sql STABLE. The exact bucket match of SB-4 arm 1 (for byo-s3: storage_path = name OR (storage_path||'.jpg' = name AND thumbnail_url = name)) is evaluated under the caller's files_select. It returns the project only when exactly one row matches (CASE WHEN count(*)=1 THEN (array_agg(project_id))[1] END), otherwise NULL.
  - *Why:* files_select decides as the caller (live, workspace, privacy, money/Legal), so a trashed file, a hidden project or a money row the caller may not see gives NULL. This also closes the pre-existing leak of an is_financial receipt under an ordinary key.
- **SB-6** Restate the 8 base storage policies by DROP + CREATE, names unchanged and every predicate word for word: rabbit_files_select/_insert/_update/_delete_own from 0042, rabbit_thumbnails_* from 0053. Each project argument becomes CASE WHEN rabbit_private.storage_object_claimed(bucket_id,name) THEN rabbit_private.storage_object_current_project(bucket_id,name) ELSE public.fn_try_uuid((storage.foldername(name))[2]) END, used in the EXISTS projects hop and in can_write_project. Both UPDATE arms are kept. rabbit_files_delete_own adds AND NOT rabbit_private.storage_object_claimed(bucket_id,name); rabbit_thumbnails_delete_own does not (moveReceipt removes a picture it could not move).
  - *Why:* Authority follows the file. Permissive policies OR together, so the policies are restated, never added beside. A purged moved copy has no current project, so nobody reads it.
- **SB-7** The 8 money storage policies (0092 §4), petal_bin_posters_* and its remote-viewing pair (0091), and petal_storage_quota_insert (0078) are NOT touched. A post-condition pins their text by md5.
  - *Why:* Money and Legal rows never move (Moth excludes them; the move refuses them) and money-ness only grows, so the second segment of a money key is its current project forever. Bin-poster keys are row-less (SB-4) and moved sources with those shapes are refused (SB-11).
- **SB-8** public.fn_files_body_guard(), plpgsql, NOT SECURITY DEFINER, trigger trg_files_body_guard BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON public.files FOR EACH ROW. (a) Client roles (current_user IN authenticated, anon). UPDATE raises 42501 'a file''s project, folder and storage change only through Move to' if any of project_id, workspace_id, folder_id, storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url, is_financial IS DISTINCT FROM OLD. INSERT requires storage_provider::text IN ('supabase','s3','local_server'), COALESCE(rabbit_private.file_key_admissible(NEW.storage_provider::text, NEW.storage_path, NEW.project_id, NEW.id, NEW.folder_id, NEW.is_financial), false), and thumbnail_url NULL or = storage_path||'.jpg'. (b) Server roles reached through the API (session_user NOT IN ('postgres','supabase_admin')). workspace_id never changes. A change of project_id, or of folder_id to a non-NULL value, needs current_setting('wilson.checked_move',true)='on'. A change of storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url or is_financial needs checked_move='on' (re-home or picture detach inside a move), or wilson.body_rekey='on' (provider unchanged), or wilson.network_share_writer='on' (OLD or NEW provider::text = 'network_share'). folder_id → NULL passes, because RI SET NULL runs as the owner. (c) Migrations, cron, the SQL editor and pgTAP (session postgres) pass. Provider literals compare as ::text, so the guard can exist before 'network_share' is added.
  - *Why:* Precedent: 0093 fn_gateways_client_guard. The client branch closes OSP-07 and the folder route (fact 12). The marker branch makes any unknown definer fail closed (SEC-11, SFB-11) without forcing pgTAP seeds or backfills to set a GUC. Marker names reuse the moves and NAS areas' GUCs.
- **SB-9** rabbit_private.file_key_admissible(p_provider text, p_path text, p_project uuid, p_file uuid, p_folder uuid, p_financial boolean) RETURNS boolean, never NULL. In order: (1) the project is in current_workspace_id(), deleted_at IS NULL, has_active_membership, passes_project_privacy, can_write_project; (2) if p_financial or the third segment is a money segment: can_access_project_money(p_project), or a Legal segment with can_access_project_legal(p_project); (3) shape: at most 1024 characters, first segment 'projects', lower(second segment) = p_project::text, at least 5 segments each '^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$' and not '.' or '..', lower(third segment) IN (scenes, shots, levels, experiences, assets, tasks, phases, project, files, invoices, legal); (4) no other files row (id <> p_file, any trash state) names p_path; for s3, the normalized form is not held by another row; (5) neither p_path nor p_path||'.jpg' is claimed by SB-4 arms 2 or 3; (6) for supabase, a storage.objects row exists in rabbit-files at p_path; (7) the folder is NULL, or a folder of p_project whose top path segment is not money-locked unless file_row_is_money(p_financial, p_path).
  - *Why:* A row can vouch only for a never-claimed object under its own creation project, which that project's readers could already read. Money is checked before any existence probe (SEC-7). Excluding the same id keeps runMigration's insertOrSkip reruns working (the BEFORE trigger fires before ON CONFLICT).
- **SB-10** public.rekey_file_body(p_file uuid, p_from_path text, p_to_path text, p_from_thumb text, p_to_thumb text, p_financial boolean DEFAULT false) RETURNS boolean, SECURITY DEFINER, row_security off. It selects the row FOR UPDATE. Every authority failure gives one refusal (42501 'file not found or not writable'): workspace, live project, membership, privacy, can_write_project, the 0092 money/Legal arm, a trashed row, a moved row (lower(second segment) ≠ project_id). It returns false on a compare-and-set miss of storage_path or thumbnail_url. A path change keeps the same first and second segments, must not be s3, keeps its Legal status, must pass file_key_admissible for that project, and for supabase needs an object at p_to_path. Money may change only false→true, with p_financial, an invoices third segment and can_access_project_money. The picture must be NULL or p_to_path||'.jpg'. It sets wilson.body_rekey='on', does one UPDATE, then resets the marker.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: the only client writers of the locator after insert are rewriteFileRow (S4c re-file) and runMigration.moveReceipt. One call keeps moveReceipt atomic. Moved rows never re-key, so every moved key keeps its creation prefix, which the meter, teardown and twin rules rely on.
- **SB-11** The body contract inside the checked move. fn_moth_apply_moves (moves area) calls rabbit_private.files_body_follow(p_file uuid, p_dest_project uuid, p_rehome jsonb) RETURNS jsonb for every cross-project file, after its own Moth exclusion and write checks and before its UPDATE. Refusals (RAISE, error code text in the message, plain sentence in the detail): 'body_elsewhere' (google_drive; network_share goes through the NAS writer only); 's3_moves_off' (s3 while s3_body_moves_on() is false); 'body_missing' (supabase with no object at storage_path); 'body_shape' (key fails the SB-9 shape or allowlist); 'needs_copy' (SB-12 says re-home and p_rehome is absent); 'copy_not_needed' (a re-home given when none is needed); 'copy_refused' (the re-home fails its checks). It returns {set:{storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url}, retire:[{bucket, path, provider, kind}]}. The apply core folds 'set' into its single UPDATE (with project_id, folder_id and the cleared links) under wilson.checked_move='on', then calls rabbit_private.files_body_retire(p_file uuid, p_retire jsonb), which inserts the queue rows (SFB-08). Association moves (no re-home) return an empty set. For a non-NULL legacy picture other than storage_path||'.jpg', 'set' gives thumbnail_url NULL and 'retire' gives that picture.
  - *Why:* The moves area's design says the body follows through 'the body-location designer's same-transaction hook'; this is that hook. One UPDATE means the capture and realtime see the project and the locator change together.
- **SB-12** Re-home rule (her answer 1 plus her standing ruling). A cross-project move needs a fresh copy when: storage_provider = 'local_server' (a private project's media, on the computer that added it), OR the project named by lower(second segment of storage_path) exists, is not the destination, and is_private (this includes personal spaces). p_rehome is [{file_id, to_provider, to_path, to_thumb}]. to_provider must be what the company's storage gives a non-money file: the active provider (workspace_storage.provider when mode is 'byos', otherwise petal), where 'petal' gives supabase and 's3' gives s3. For 'network', the NAS writer must re-home the row to network_share in the destination's folder; that is a need from the NAS area, and until it lands the move is refused with a plain sentence. to_path must pass file_key_admissible for the destination (third segment 'files'). For supabase the object must exist at to_path. to_thumb must be NULL or to_path||'.jpg'. The retired old copy and picture are queued. A local_server original is removed by the holding desktop after the move commits. Undo of a re-homed move is by association only. If undo would itself need a re-home, it refuses with 'move it back with Move to'.
  - *Why:* Her answer 1: copy it up, only from the computer that holds it. The server cannot see which computer is calling, so it requires the fresh copy, and only the holding desktop can make one (NOT_HERE elsewhere). Her ruling forbids a hidden project's id (and the original name) reaching the destination through the key, which SEC-6 shows is otherwise permanent.
- **SB-13** rabbit_private.s3_body_moves_on() RETURNS boolean, LANGUAGE sql IMMUTABLE, returns false in Session 1's body migration. A later one-function migration restates it as true after the new storage-presign is confirmed deployed on that environment.
  - *Why:* SEC-4: the deployed storage-presign authorises by key prefix, and a database cannot see what has been deployed.
- **SB-14** public.can_presign_object(p_path text, p_op text) RETURNS boolean, SECURITY INVOKER, LANGUAGE sql STABLE, COALESCE false. op not in get/head/put/del: false. If rabbit_private.storage_object_claimed('byo-s3', p_path): get/head = has_active_membership AND rabbit_private.storage_object_current_project('byo-s3', p_path) IS NOT NULL; put/del = false. Otherwise the 0051 can_presign_project_read/_write(fn_try_uuid(second segment)), unchanged. storage-presign calls it as the caller; checkRowShapedPath stays first.
  - *Why:* Closes OSP-01 for every claimed key: trashed and purged keys are not signed, and nobody can PUT over or DELETE a referenced copy. Unclaimed keys keep the legacy rule (the connection check's HEAD, connectionStatus l.191). The single-claimant rule rests on SB-2.
- **SB-15** Restate workspace_petal_committed_bytes (0073 whole) UNION ALL a moved-copies arm: files f JOIN storage.objects o on (rabbit-files, f.storage_path) or (rabbit-thumbnails, f.thumbnail_url), WHERE f.workspace_id = ws AND provider supabase AND lower(second segment) <> f.project_id::text AND NOT EXISTS (projects p WHERE p.id = fn_try_uuid(second segment)). service_role only.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: the meter joins projects on the second segment, so purging an origin would free quota for its moved copies. Re-homed copies sit under the destination and are counted by arm 1. Id reuse is blocked (SB-18), so 'no projects row' stays meaningful.
- **SB-16** storage-gc: rowNamesTwin drops .eq('project_id') and adds .in('storage_provider',['supabase','s3','local_server']); referencedPaths gains the same provider filter. The drain is unchanged.
  - *Why:* Defence in depth now that body_missing stops the data-loss case, and it lets the partial index serve the lookup. The drain's restorability check already uses thumbnail_url for picture rows.
- **SB-17** _shared/teardownCollect.ts collectBlobPaths: for paths from this workspace's files and queue rows, owned(p) = projectIds.has(first segment after 'projects') OR (no projects row with that id exists AND file_events ties that id to this workspace). A path under another workspace's project is still rejected.
  - *Why:* A moved copy whose origin was purged must be collected by a certified teardown, not stranded.
- **SB-18** public.fn_projects_id_reuse_guard(), plpgsql, NOT SECURITY DEFINER, trigger trg_projects_id_reuse_guard BEFORE INSERT ON public.projects. For client roles: if NOT EXISTS a projects row with NEW.id AND rabbit_private.project_id_tied_elsewhere(NEW.id, NEW.workspace_id), raise 42501 'this project cannot be brought in under that id'. The helper is a definer: EXISTS file_events with project_id = id and workspace_id <> ws, OR EXISTS files with workspace_id <> ws and storage_path LIKE 'projects/'||id||'/%' and a key provider. It fires after trg_projects_populate_workspace, so it reads NEW.workspace_id; the trigger name must sort after it, or it uses current_workspace_id() instead.
  - *Why:* SEC-1 part 2. Same-workspace re-carries pass, so runMigration's insertOrSkip keeps working; the 'exists' arm lets ON CONFLICT handle reruns. The projects-guard owners may fold this into their BEFORE INSERT guard.
- **SB-19** file_events: adopt the moves area's 'refiled' term (the source row under OLD.project_id with old_path; the destination row under NEW.project_id with new_path; details an object, never NULL) and the NAS area's 'catalogued'. The CHECK is one DROP + ADD in order, restating the union: uploaded, downloaded, moved, relinked, trashed, restored, purged, upload_abandoned, viewed_remote, refiled, catalogued. On an UPDATE that changes project_id, the plain 'moved' (storage_path) arm must not fire. My draft's moved_out/moved_in are dropped.
  - *Why:* One vocabulary across areas. SFB-05 and SEC-9 are met: with SB-12, no destination-readable path names a hidden project.
- **SB-20** A trashed file's stored copy cannot be read through the storage API or the signer until it is restored; list() hides it too. Recorded as a behaviour change. (As built, 0115: except under a money or Legal key, whose policies do not read the row; its cleared audience can restore it anyway.) The trash views show names and icons, not previews (consistent with moves MV-11).
  - *Why:* Follows from SB-5 and closes OSP-01 for Petal. No client reads a trashed copy today: restoreFile has zero callers (measured).
- **SB-21** Desktop-only projects (Local Server bundles), her answer 2. The bundle locator stays the same string, because it is relative to the project's base folder (resolveFileBaseDir). The file itself moves from the source project's base folder to the destination's on the same computer: rename if on the same volume, otherwise copy, flush, compare size, then unlink. The move route says done only after the destination file exists with the right size and both bundles are written. A small durable pending-move record (rabbit-data, writeJsonAtomic durable:true) lets the next project open finish or undo an interrupted move. Money and Legal base folders (INVOICES, LEGAL) are never involved.
  - *Why:* This overrides moves MV-14's 'refused in Session 1'. The route belongs to the moves area; this is the body rule it must keep.
- **SB-22** Office-server (network_share) files, her answers 3 and 5. Only the NAS area's writer (wilson.network_share_writer) changes their locator, project or provider. Following her 'it acts like a file manager', a move between projects should move the real file into the destination project's folder on the server, from the desktop; in a browser the move says to do it from the desktop app. A delete moves the real file into the server's bin folder (her answer 5). The body guard allows both only under that marker.
  - *Why:* An association-only NAS move would keep the file under the source project's folder, where the next Refresh finds it again, and a personal-space folder name in the 'nas:' locator would reach the destination. Reconciliation point with the NAS area, which currently changes association only.
- **SB-23** Sticky-note publication fencing waits for Session 4 (her answer 6). Session 4 puts the lease and generation on sticky_locks and adds one lease-checked arm to rekey_file_body, by then the only writer of a locator after insert.
  - *Why:* A column with no writer cannot be pinned by a test that bites; the seam exists from Session 1.

#### Schema changes

- **SCHEMA rabbit_private** (restates from: new): CREATE SCHEMA rabbit_private; REVOKE ALL ON SCHEMA rabbit_private FROM PUBLIC; GRANT USAGE TO authenticated, service_role. Never listed in the API's exposed schemas (config.toml pin, runbook).
- **Preflight (top of the first body migration)** (restates from: new): Refuse when row security applies to current_user (0088/0092 idiom). LOCK TABLE public.files IN SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE MODE. Refusing counts, each RAISEd with its number: duplicate storage_path among key providers; s3 normalized-key collisions; key-provider rows whose lower(second segment) ≠ project_id; key-provider rows whose second-segment project is in another workspace; non-key-provider rows whose storage_path starts 'projects/'. NOTICE only: legacy pictures other than path||'.jpg'; rows whose third segment is outside the allowlist or a reserved shape; non-money rows in money-locked folders; queue rows with file_id NOT NULL in pending/failed naming reserved shapes.
- **public.files indexes files_storage_path_key_uidx, files_s3_namespace_uidx, files_moved_body_ws_idx** (restates from: new): As SB-2. Plain CREATE inside the transaction. The rehearsal EXPLAINs each lookup.
- **public.storage_gc_queue storage_gc_queue_claim_idx; public.file_events file_events_purged_path_idx, file_events_project_any_idx** (restates from: new): As SB-2.
- **rabbit_private.storage_object_claimed(text,text) RETURNS boolean** (restates from: new): SB-4. DEFINER, STABLE, plpgsql, search_path public, row_security off. Row-less shapes return false first, then the sequential IFs. REVOKE PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE authenticated, service_role.
- **rabbit_private.storage_object_current_project(text,text) RETURNS uuid** (restates from: new): SB-5. INVOKER, sql STABLE. Post-condition: NOT prosecdef. REVOKE PUBLIC, anon; GRANT authenticated, service_role.
- **rabbit_private.file_key_admissible(text,text,uuid,uuid,uuid,boolean) RETURNS boolean** (restates from: new): SB-9. DEFINER, STABLE, row_security off, COALESCE false. REVOKE PUBLIC, anon; GRANT authenticated (the guard runs as the caller), service_role. COMMENT names its callers.
- **rabbit_private.files_body_follow(uuid,uuid,jsonb) RETURNS jsonb; rabbit_private.files_body_retire(uuid,jsonb) RETURNS void; rabbit_private.s3_body_moves_on() RETURNS boolean; rabbit_private.project_id_tied_elsewhere(uuid,uuid) RETURNS boolean** (restates from: new): SB-11, SB-12, SB-13, SB-18. follow and retire are DEFINER and REVOKEd from every client role (only fn_moth_apply_moves calls them). s3_body_moves_on returns false. tied_elsewhere is DEFINER, row_security off, EXECUTE authenticated (the projects trigger calls it as the caller).
- **public.fn_files_body_guard() + trg_files_body_guard BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON public.files** (restates from: new (idiom of 0093 fn_gateways_client_guard)): SB-8. NOT prosecdef; REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated. COMMENT: it fires before trg_files_legal_fixed, trg_files_network_share_guard and trg_files_populate_workspace and reads none of their effects (it uses current_workspace_id()). Post-conditions (comments stripped): trigger attached; the body names all seven columns and the three marker literals 'wilson.checked_move', 'wilson.body_rekey', 'wilson.network_share_writer'.
- **public.rekey_file_body(uuid,text,text,text,text,boolean) RETURNS boolean** (restates from: new): SB-10. DEFINER, row_security off, sets and resets wilson.body_rekey. REVOKE PUBLIC, anon; GRANT authenticated.
- **public.fn_projects_id_reuse_guard() + trg_projects_id_reuse_guard BEFORE INSERT ON public.projects** (restates from: new): SB-18. NOT prosecdef; REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated.
- **storage.objects rabbit_files_select/_insert/_update/_delete_own** (restates from: 0042_money_segments_and_manifest_rewrite.sql): DROP + CREATE each whole from 0042 l.121-200 with the SB-6 CASE. delete_own adds the NOT-claimed arm. Post-conditions: exactly 8 rabbit_files% policies; all 16 rabbit policies name rabbit_money_segment; the 4 base ones name both rabbit_private helpers; both UPDATE arms carry WITH CHECK.
- **storage.objects rabbit_thumbnails_select/_insert/_update/_delete_own** (restates from: 0053_thumbnails_bucket.sql): DROP + CREATE each whole from 0053 with the CASE (bucket rabbit-thumbnails). delete_own unchanged in spirit: own object, one hour, no NOT-claimed arm.
- **rabbit_*_money_* (8), petal_bin_posters_* (and the remote-viewing pair), petal_storage_quota_insert** (restates from: 0092 / 0091 / 0078 (unchanged)): NO CHANGE. Post-condition compares the qual/with_check md5 to the 0092 / 0091 / 0078 text.
- **public.can_presign_object(text,text) RETURNS boolean** (restates from: new (calls 0051 can_presign_project_read/_write)): SB-14. INVOKER sql STABLE. REVOKE PUBLIC, anon; GRANT authenticated, service_role. 0051's predicates are unchanged.
- **public.workspace_petal_committed_bytes(uuid)** (restates from: 0073_upload_reservations.sql): SB-15. CREATE OR REPLACE: 0073's body whole, UNION ALL the moved-copies arm. REVOKE PUBLIC, anon, authenticated; GRANT service_role.
- **file_events_event_check, fn_file_events_capture, fn_realtime_broadcast (files arm)** (restates from: 0093 (CHECK), 0074 (capture), 0098 (broadcast)): Built by the moves area (and the NAS area for 'catalogued'), to the SB-19 and SEC-3 contract. Nothing here restates them separately.
- **Migration after deploy: rabbit_private.s3_body_moves_on()** (restates from: this area's first body migration): CREATE OR REPLACE returning true. Applied per environment only after storage-presign's deployed source is verified. Post-condition: it returns true.

#### Consumers to change

- **supabase/functions/storage-presign/index.ts (l.135-138, header refusal item 5)**: Call rpc('can_presign_object', {p_path, p_op}) as the caller instead of choosing can_presign_project_read/_write(shape.projectId). checkRowShapedPath stays first.
- **supabase/functions/_shared/readUrls.ts classifyOwned, handleReadUrls; storage-read-urls deps canReadProject and rowPage**: classifyOwned drops the keyProject === row.project_id equality. The canReadProject pre-check goes. rowPage drops .eq('project_id') and keeps the folder LIKE read as the caller, served by the unique index. 'project' is no longer emitted; an invisible key is 'row'. Deploy only after the body migrations are on that environment.
- **supabase/functions/storage-gc/index.ts rowNamesTwin, referencedPaths**: SB-16. Re-measure the deno KNOWN baseline in rls.yml on the same branch.
- **supabase/functions/_shared/teardownCollect.ts owned()/take/takeThumb**: SB-17. Deploy via operator-workspaces.
- **electron/projectFilePatch.cjs**: Strip or refuse project_id, workspace_id, thumbnail_url, is_financial and folder_id (it already strips storage_path, storage_provider and id), agreeing with moves MV-14 that the move route is the only folder writer.
- **Moves area: fn_moth_apply_moves / moth_move_items**: Gains p_rehome jsonb DEFAULT '[]'. Calls files_body_follow per cross-project file, folds 'set' into its UPDATE, then calls files_body_retire. The move guard and the body guard share wilson.checked_move. The provider list is replaced by follow's answer.
- **Moves area: Local Server move route and electron/fileTrash.cjs**: Cross-project moves between desktop-only projects follow SB-21 (real file moves, verified, pending-move record), not 'refused'.
- **NAS area: network_share_catalogue_apply and fn_files_network_share_guard**: Its writer marker is honoured by the body guard. It should provide the real-file move between project folders (SB-22) and a re-home target for SB-12 in network companies.
- **Personal-space area: projectIsPrivate and the personal CHECK**: Personal uploads are never local_server; personal ⇒ is_private stays a CHECK (SB-12 reads is_private).
- **History and repairs areas: edit_history_select / capture; moth_history**: The SEC-3 contract for cross-project files diffs. moth_history never stores storage_path, thumbnail_url or locators.
- **supabase/tests/rls 35_platform_audit.sql**: The anon and search_path audit covers prosecdef functions in public AND rabbit_private.
- **.github/workflows/rls.yml (replay list, deno KNOWN) and src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/storage/rlsSuiteLists.test.js tail pins**: The new cross-table suite covers no table: add it by hand to the replay list and move both tail pins. No RLS_TABLES line.
- **docs: SYSTEMS_HANDBOOK §4.4, §4.7 (a key's prefix is where a file was created), §4.9 chains, §5.4, §12.3, §12.4, §12.7, §17; OUTSTANDING OSP-01, OSP-07, S4c-12; PLAN_WEEK F7; PRODUCTION_RUNBOOK**: Same commit as the behaviour. Record: OSP-01 and OSP-07 closed for claimed keys; F7's storage_path index is provided here; the preflight queries; the never-roll-back-storage-presign rule; never expose rabbit_private.

#### Client changes

- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js updateFile (l.3809)**: sanitize() also strips project_id, workspace_id, storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url, is_financial and folder_id before .update(). localServerAdapter keeps project_id for its URL only. columnAllowlist test updated. No new export. *(production caller: RabbitProvider.patchFile / setFileCore; ProjectsPage.jsx l.357; FileEditor through patchFile)*
- **supabaseAdapter.js rewriteFileRow (l.1550)**: Two-phase update through rpc('rekey_file_body') (the body phase keeps the picture; the picture phase keeps the path). A false result goes down the existing no-row branch (re-read, put back). *(production caller: refileOneObject ← refileOneShot ← adapter.refileShotFolders)*
- **src/cloud/migrate/runMigration.js moveReceipt (l.2618) and the ordinary-files loop**: moveReceipt makes one rpc('rekey_file_body', {…, p_financial:true}), keeping 'changed by someone else' on false. In the files loop, an upload refused by row security still tries insertOrSkip (23505 = skipped 'already in the cloud, perhaps in its bin') (SFB-09). A non-money row whose desktop folder is under INVOICES, FINANCE or LEGAL goes to the project top level with a sentence (SFB-07). *(production caller: MigrationPanel run → runMigration)*
- **New adapter method copyBodyForMove(fileRow, destProjectId) → {to_provider, to_path, to_thumb} (supabaseAdapter, and the fixtures adapter, which refuses)**: Builds projects/{dest}/files/{fileId}/{Date.now()}-{safeName}. supabase: storage copy for the copy and its .jpg. s3: presigned GET, then presigned PUT. local_server: the holding desktop reads it through localServerProvider and puts it to the company provider (NOT_HERE elsewhere, with the sentence 'this file is on <the computer that added it>; move it from there'). After the move commits, the desktop removes the local original. Called only when the move answers needs_copy. *(production caller: Moth's Move to and the Files page's Move to (the moves area's adapter.moveItems) on the 'needs_copy' answer, then a retry with p_rehome)*
- **storage/thumbnails.js thumbnailUrlsAt / FileManager thumbnail refusal handling**: Treat refusal 'row' exactly as 'project' was treated (icon fallback). No new export. *(production caller: FileManager thumbUrls effect → adapter.thumbnailUrls)*
- **supabase/config.toml pin (vitest beside rlsSuiteLists.test.js)**: Asserts api.schemas never contains rabbit_private. *(production caller: n/a (test))*

#### Migration order

- **0, measurement (read-only)**: On wilson-dev and wilson-staging (each named by inet_server_addr plus a workspace count): every preflight count, s3 namespace collisions, third-segment values in use, legacy pictures, queue rows (file_id NOT NULL, pending/failed) naming reserved shapes, server_version, rolbypassrls of postgres. Desktop side: bundle files filed under INVOICES, FINANCE or LEGAL without a money flag. Every suite that writes public.files as a client role.
