#### Risks

- An admin's stale desktop build lists other people's personal spaces in R.A.B.B.I.T. until it updates (no minimum-version mechanism exists).
  - *Mitigation:* Writes are owner-only at the database and local bodies are refused, so the exposure is read-only and no wider than Files. The client ships first. Recorded in OUTSTANDING with the question of whether a minimum-version check belongs in this release.
- Admins read personal spaces through raw REST without an activity record; 'Files only' is a read-path rule.
  - *Mitigation:* Required by the Files ruling. The handbook's deviations table says so; WIL-4114 records every open in WILSON; OUTSTANDING records the REST gap.
- Settled by her integration answer 2 (section 0), built by 0121: once someone has left, an admin may move that person's files into a project.
  - *Mitigation:* Admins can open and download them from Files. Ask plainly: 'When someone leaves, should an admin be able to move their personal files into a project?' The recommended answer is yes, only once the person is no longer an active member. It needs one more arm in can_write_project and the move RPC.
- The restated can_write_project adds a primary-key lookup on projects to every write evaluation, presign check and trash call.
  - *Mitigation:* One indexed read. Timings are measured on the fixture seed and wilson-dev and go into the hand-off.
- A seated writer who renames both the slug and the path of a money category brings unflagged pre-0076 receipts into Moth.
  - *Mitigation:* Bounded by RLS (the writer already sees them). The moves area's reserved-folder guard is asked to pin money category rows. Recorded.
- The same-project receipt oracle: hiding a receipt from Moth tells a member it is attached to an expense.
  - *Mitigation:* They can already open the body. No indicator or count. Recorded.
- Office-server personal files are readable by anyone with share access outside WILSON.
  - *Mitigation:* Her answer 3: kept there and stated plainly in every add dialog.
- Workspace storage totals and the provider-switch count include personal bytes (admin-only views).
  - *Mitigation:* Same as private projects today. Admins only; no per-person figure is added.
- The NAS catalogue's ON CONFLICT on a locator already owned by a personal row could surface in another project's walk result.
  - *Mitigation:* Interface requirement on the NAS area: the apply answer never counts or names entries that conflict with a row the caller cannot see. Probe there.

#### Interfaces with the other areas

- **Personal-space classifier** (provides): SQL: projects.personal_owner_id IS NOT NULL (owner = personal_owner_id; CHECK makes is_private true and created_by = owner; never binned; folder_slug 'Personal_' || replace(owner::text,'-','')). JS: isPersonalSpace(row) in src/cloud/personalSpace.js (row.personal_owner_id != null || row.personal === true). Desktop: bundle.project.personal === true, only at {userData}/personal-space.
- **Owner-only write helper (for files column guard trusted writers, moves, NAS catalogue)** (provides): can_write_project(p_project uuid) boolean (restated): personal → personal_owner_id = auth.uid(); else the 0013 body. Same arm in can_comment_project, can_edit_shot_lists; can_manage_project_roster personal → false. Definer callers (moth_move_items, network_share_catalogue_apply, fn_trash_authz) use it unchanged.
- **Moth eligibility** (provides): moth_can_read_target(p_kind text, p_id uuid) boolean [authenticated]; moth_can_write_target(p_kind text, p_id uuid) boolean [authenticated]; _moth_target_eligible(p_kind text, p_id uuid, p_allow_trashed boolean) boolean [definers only; moth_trash_index passes true]. Kinds 'project'|'folder'|'file'; personal arm has no admin leg; money via file_row_is_money, legal tag, folder chain, own-project expenses.
- **Moth catalogue RPCs** (provides): moth_islands() → (id, title, is_personal, is_mine, is_private, my_project_role, is_staffed, can_write, updated_at); moth_project_folders(p_project) → (id, parent_id, kind, entity_type, slug, label, path, sort_order, updated_at); moth_project_files(p_project, p_after uuid DEFAULT NULL, p_limit int DEFAULT 500) → MR-5 columns. INVOKER, no counts.
- **Provisioning and admin record** (provides): ensure_personal_space() RETURNS uuid; record_personal_space_opened(p_project uuid) RETURNS void → app_events('admin','WIL-4114', context {project_id, owner_user_id}).
- **Body provider values for a personal project** (provides): Allowed storage_provider: 'supabase' (petal, and money everywhere), 's3', 'network_share' (NAS catalogue, Personal_<hex> folder). Refused by fn_files_personal_provider_guard: 'local_server', 'google_drive' (text compare; bodies area may fold into fn_files_body_guard). New uploads keyed projects/<personal id>/... per the bodies area's mint rule.
- **Move operation contract (moves area)** (needs): moth_move_items(file_ids uuid[], folder_ids uuid[], dest_project uuid, dest_folder uuid): read gate = moth_can_read_target on every source and dest_project; write = can_write_project (personal arm) on source and destination; into a personal project refuse local_server/google_drive bodies unless the answer-1 copy-up (desktop holding the body copies to company storage) has changed the provider in the same transaction; local moves (answer 2) resolve the personal dir through resolveProjectFilesDir(personal) → {userData}/personal-space/files; office-server deletes (answer 5) apply inside Personal_<hex> too. Name to reconcile with the bodies area's 'move_files'.
- **Files column guard and trusted writers (bodies area)** (needs): fn_files_body_guard keeps project_id/storage_path/provider changes to the trusted writers (move RPC, rekey_file_body); either absorbs the personal provider rule or leaves fn_files_personal_provider_guard firing on UPDATE OF project_id, storage_provider.
- **NAS catalogue (NAS area)** (needs): 'network_share' provider value (own migration); network_share_folder_of(folder_root, folder_slug, root) resolves the personal folder from folder_slug; network_share_catalogue_apply authority via can_write_project (owner-only on personal); the desktop's resolved drive root + pathContainment reused by POST /api/network-share/personal-space/files; Moth's personal panel triggers the walk for the personal project on open (owner, desktop) and after Add files; apply answers never count or name entries owned by rows the caller cannot see.
- **Workspace-channel suppression (repairs area)** (needs): fn_workspace_realtime_broadcast restated from 0018, each side fail-closed (projects side visible iff NOT is_private; child side iff EXISTS project NOT is_private), AUDIENCE signal with no row on visibility change. Personal rows never flip, so they never reach the topic. Client side provided here: no merge of is_private or personal records at handleWorkspaceEvent and project-patch.
- **Privacy repairs (repairs area)** (needs): D4 trg_projects_owner_guard (created_by pin, is_private flip creator|admin); D5 can_access_project_money + passes_project_privacy; D6 edit_history_project_of for all entity types + edit_history_select (admin OR can_read_project_topic(project_id)); D11 project_members_select hop; comments hop via fn_comment_project_id. All applied before P1.
- **file_events vocabulary** (needs): No new event from this area. Events written under a personal project ('catalogued', 'refiled' or 'moved_out'/'moved_in' per moves/bodies reconciliation) never name the other project; file_events_select (0092: can_read_project_topic OR admin) gives owner + admin, which is the Files reach. The admin-open record is app_events, not file_events.
- **History capture (tables area)** (needs): moth_history snapshots scope class 'personal' when projects.personal_owner_id IS NOT NULL with owner = personal_owner_id at capture; readers satisfy original and present audience with no admin bypass for personal; the provisioning INSERT is captured as structure only.
- **Moth tool folder name** (needs): This area assumes src/tools/moth_v0.1.0/ (the rabbit_v0.1.0 convention); the moves area wrote src/tools/moth/. The integrator picks one; the source scan and writeGate assertion follow it.

### 2.3 Moves, deletes, trash and undo

Findings: 4 partly, 1 refuted, 23 taken.

**Scope as resolved:** Moves, deletes, trash and undo (cloud and desktop), revised after the security and correctness reviews and her six answers

**Summary:** Session 1 moves FILES only. Folders are not movable or trashable from Moth this session: nothing in src/ or electron/ writes kind='custom' folders (grep), and board folders belong to Session 4 (answer 6). That one cut removes the folder-move oracle, the carried trashed subtree, entity folders nested under custom folders, cycles, and the need for a workspace advisory lock.

The cloud move is one SECURITY DEFINER RPC in two phases: public.moth_move_items(p_file_ids, p_dest_project, p_dest_folder, p_plan_only, p_bodies).
- The plan phase writes nothing. It tells the caller what each file needs: a metadata move, a copy-up of a body held on one computer (answer 1), or a real move on the office server (answers 2 and 5: "it acts like a file manager").
- The commit phase re-validates everything. It writes a definer-only journal row (file_moves) first, sets a transaction-local marker bound to that row, and then updates the rows. The files guards (bodies and NAS areas) admit a project, folder or locator change only for a row that is journalled in the current transaction.
- Every read and exclusion check goes through the personal area's _moth_target_eligible, so money, Legal, expense receipts, files under locked folders, others' personal spaces and unreadable private projects all answer not_found. Write rights go through moth_can_write_target.
- A cross-project move clears the seven entity links and is_core_definer. It refuses asset-version files and files with extracted text. Nothing it writes names the origin: the moved_out/moved_in events carry no paths, the realtime split puts only NEW on the new topic, and the edit-history old side is gated by the repairs area.

Undo and redo are both moth_undo_move(move_id), which replays the server's own journal.

Cloud delete and restore wrap soft_delete_row and restore_soft_deleted in atomic batches that add the Moth exclusions. moth_trash_index lists metadata only. Deleting an office-server file is desktop-only: the row is trashed, then the desktop moves the real file into <project folder>/.wilson-bin on the server. Undo and restore move it back. After 30 days it is removed for good. A browser says the delete must be done from the desktop app.

On a Local Server desktop:
- A delete becomes a 30-day trash. The body goes to <base dir>/.trash, and the base kind is recorded when the file is trashed.
- Moves between two local projects move the real file and are verified before WILSON answers. A local journal covers crashes.
- Managed (entity-bound) files do not move between projects.
- Local-to-cloud moves are refused.

Moth has its own in-memory undo stack. R.A.B.B.I.T. deletes gain undo, which gives restoreFile its first production caller.

#### Findings and their dispositions

- **SEC-MV-01 [blocker] — partly.** (1) The move migration's pre-flight refuses to apply unless the four base rabbit_files policies' text names storage_object_current_project. A function merely existing is no longer enough, and the runtime 'not_yet' switch is dropped. (2) The moved_out and moved_in rows carry old_path and new_path NULL. (3) The new topic gets INSERT with NEW only; the old topic gets DELETE with OLD. (4) A copy-up mints its key under the DESTINATION project. The residual, storage_path naming the origin on metadata moves, belongs to the bodies area and is recorded in risks; the move adds no other channel.
  - *Evidence:* 0042 rabbit_files_select authorises by fn_try_uuid(foldername[2]) with no files-row check. 0098 broadcasts COALESCE(NEW,OLD) and passes NEW and OLD to broadcast_changes. The bodies design (SB-5) restates the 8 base storage policies to follow the current project for claimed keys, and its own SEC-6 leaves the origin uuid in storage_path open as a question for her.
- **SEC-MV-02 [blocker] — partly.** Folder sources are removed from Session 1, so no visible folder can be refused because of hidden contents. Guard arm (b) is dropped (see MV-R1). The re-home repair is not taken: it would change R.A.B.B.I.T.'s filing of receipts with no ruling, and nothing now depends on it. Residual: Moth's own omission of an unflagged expense receipt shows a member that the file is excluded. This is inherent to her exclusion ruling and recorded beside 0076 TRAP 1.
  - *Evidence:* supabaseAdapter.uploadFile l.3375-3427: a financial upload takes the entity folderRow first, and INVOICES only as the fallback. 0041 has no writer of kind 'custom' (grep of src and electron). Exclusion helper: personal area MR-3 (expense membership, locked-folder descendants, the 'legal' tag).
- **SEC-MV-03 [blocker] — taken.** There are no folder sources, and a source must be live (_moth_target_eligible with allow_trashed=false), so a trashed file can never change project.
  - *Evidence:* Draft MV-2(4)/MV-6 carried trashed subtree files cross-project. 0092 fn_trash_authz judges only the current project.
- **SEC-MV-04 [major] — taken.** A cross-project move refuses with has_extracted_text when EXISTS ingestion_chunks c WHERE c.file_id = f.id. A planted chunk probe is added. The policy gap goes to OUTSTANDING.
  - *Evidence:* 0000 ingestion_chunks (raw_text, parsed_json, file_id SET NULL). 0004 ingestion_chunks_select hops through the run's project only.
- **SEC-MV-05 [major] — taken.** Every new definer (move, undo, trash, restore, index) opens with that guard. A post-condition checks for has_active_membership in each body. An inactive-member probe is added.
  - *Evidence:* 0082 milestones_trash_index l.193: IF v_ws IS NULL OR NOT has_active_membership(v_ws) THEN RAISE 'not_a_workspace_member'.
- **SEC-MV-06 [major] — partly.** fn_trash_authz is not restated. Under PS-1/PS-3 the generic RPCs admit only the owner and admins, and admins are her Files-view reach. This is now a stated interface requirement. moth_trash suite probes on the generic path: a manager and an unstaffed member are refused; an admin is allowed.
  - *Evidence:* 0092 fn_trash_authz gives privacy only through passes_project_privacy (0082: NOT is_private OR created_by=auth.uid() OR admin). Personal area PS-1 CHECK (is_private=true AND created_by=personal_owner_id) and PS-3 (created_by pinned, owner immutable).
- **SEC-MV-07 [major] — taken.** Requirement on the repairs area's edit_history_select: add AND (entity_type <> 'files' OR NOT (diff ? 'project_id') OR can_read_project_topic(fn_try_uuid(diff->'project_id'->>'old'))). The move migration's pre-flight checks that clause. Probe: a manager reads no history row naming a personal space they do not own.
  - *Evidence:* 0061 capture: an UPDATE diff is {col:{old,new}}, so project_id.old names the origin. 0092 edit_history_select has no privacy arm. Repairs D6 gates only the snapshot project_id, which is the destination.
- **SEC-MV-08 [major] — taken.** fileTrash.cjs and fileMoves.cjs get isMothExcluded(row, bundle): is_financial, isLegalRow, the 'legal' tag, a legacy money-folder name, a folder whose first path segment is a money segment, or the id in any expense's file_ids. The Moth batch routes answer 404 for an excluded id. The PATCH twin refuses any folder_id change, matching the cloud guard.
  - *Evidence:* migrationRows.cloudHomeOfFile keeps the desktop folder. main.cjs resolveFileBaseDir and isLegalRow are the local money tests.
- **SEC-MV-09 [major] — taken.** The Move to picker, the trash view and every Moth project chooser read only moth_islands rows, which already exclude others' personal spaces, and gate them with canOnProject('project.moth.write', {ready,…}). A fixture-admin test asserts another person's space is absent.
  - *Evidence:* projectRoleMatrix canOnProject: the admin/manager short-circuit. projectsIndex holds others' private and personal rows for admins.
- **SEC-MV-10 [minor] — taken.** Removed with folder sources.
  - *Evidence:* 0041 folders_kind_chk has no parent-kind rule; entity FKs CASCADE.
- **SEC-MV-11 [minor] — taken.** wilson.file_move = the journal row id. fn_checked_move_admits requires that row to have been written in this transaction (txid) with an item matching (file, to_project, to_folder, to_provider, to_path). The bodies and NAS guards call it. A planted test definer that moves an unjournalled row is refused.
  - *Evidence:* 0025/0064/0084 bind their bypass GUC to an id. Draft used 'on'.
- **SEC-MV-12 [minor] — taken.** A destination folder outside the destination project, or locked, answers not_found; a probe compares the message with a random uuid's. Undo runs the read gate on every from and to project before comparing positions.
  - *Evidence:* Draft MV-2(6) had no code. Draft MV-5 checked changed_since before the read gate.
- **SEC-MV-13 [minor] — taken.** Each trashedFiles entry records base_kind (files|invoices|legal_project|legal_internal|internal), chosen by the server at trash time. Restore and purge resolve from it.
  - *Evidence:* main.cjs resolveFileBaseDir l.2315-2330 and legalFiling.baseDirFor choose by existsSync.
- **SEC-MV-14 [minor] — taken.** The project GET has no side effects. Expiry runs only in POST …/trash/purge-expired, which the client calls on project open and when the trash view opens. GET …/trash is read-only.
  - *Evidence:* main.cjs l.5065 sameSite 'lax'. localToken.cjs l.198-200 accepts the cookie. The cookie lives in Electron's session, so exposure is lower than stated.
- **MV-R1 [blocker] — taken.** The money-folder arm is dropped. The client column guard (bodies area) compares IS DISTINCT FROM. Exclusion is only by the read helper.
  - *Evidence:* migrationRows.js l.496-500 (money keeps its desktop folder). RabbitProvider.patchFile l.5394-5404 always sends project_id, so UPDATE OF project_id fires on every patch.
- **MV-R2 [major] — taken.** The split goes after the IF v_project IS NULL return, uses to_jsonb(OLD/NEW)->>'project_id' and TG_TABLE_NAME, and names no 'files' literal. The same commit updates 100 #7 and 101 #18 with the new md5, re-counts 99 #14 and 101 #16, and records that 0098 cannot be replayed afterwards.
  - *Evidence:* 95 #170 matches the files arm text up to the v_project line. 99 #14 and 101 #16 count 'files' once. 100 #7 and 101 #18 pin the md5.
- **MV-R3 [major] — taken.** base_kind is recorded (SEC-MV-13). relink-apply with changingBase answers 409 while any trashedFiles entry of kind files has its body under the current dir. Tests cover a legacy invoice dir and a relink between trash and restore.
  - *Evidence:* main.cjs relink-apply: the stranded check reads bundle.files only.
- **MV-R4 [major] — taken.** The per-project ctx comes from moth_islands (my_project_role, is_staffed, is_personal, can_write), with ready kept false until it settles. writeGate.test.js scans src/tools/moth.
  - *Evidence:* moth1_core E14: the seat is held for the open project only.
- **MV-R5 [major] — taken.** One exclusion: _moth_target_eligible (it includes locked-folder descendants), used by the move, the trash, the restore and the index. Locally, isMothExcluded walks bundle.folders.
  - *Evidence:* PLAN_MOTH §4 shared rules; uploadFile honours scope.folderId.
- **MV-R6 [major] — partly.** Moth's Session 1 deletes and moves are files only. Folder trash and folder moves are specified as deferred to Session 4 with board folders, and recorded as a material plan difference.
  - *Evidence:* 0041: no deleted_at, parent CASCADE, no custom writer. Answer 6 puts boards in Session 4.
- **MV-R7 [major] — taken.** Interface requirement on personal PS-6, plus a probe that a personal-space upload is never local_server. A private project's local bodies follow answer 1 (copy-up).
  - *Evidence:* uploadFile l.3481 sends private uploads to LOCAL_SERVER. Personal area PS-6 narrows projectIsPrivate.
- **MV-R8 [minor] — taken.** The pin moves to fileTrash.cjs: the route string, and resolveContainedFilePath before every renameSync and unlinkSync.
  - *Evidence:* pathContainment.test.js l.168-173.
- **MV-R9 [minor] — taken.** The phantom revert change is dropped. 'trashedFiles' joins NOT_CARRIED and BUNDLE_COUNTED, with a binsMigration.test.js assertion.
  - *Evidence:* editHistoryRevert REVERTABLE_TABLES has no files. binsMigration.js NOT_CARRIED and BUNDLE_COUNTED are at l.410-479.
- **MV-R10 [minor] — taken.** trg_file_moves_audit runs BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE, with a created_by probe.
  - *Evidence:* 0001 fn_audit_touch has an INSERT arm; 0098 trg_item_groupings_audit runs BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE.
- **MV-R11 [minor] — taken.** Event paths are NULL on a project change, and the claim is corrected. The 'moved' arm is skipped when project_id changes, so a NAS or copy-up locator never reaches the other side.
  - *Evidence:* uploadFile key projects/<pid>/…; 0027 old_path/new_path.
- **MV-R12 [minor] — taken.** There are no subtree items, and 'not_yet' is replaced by a pre-flight ordering check.
  - *Evidence:* Draft MV-5 and the 'not_yet' post-condition contradiction.
- **MV-R13 [minor] — taken.** listLocalTrash and the managed restore leave out rows whose asset_id is not in bundle.assets. The asset-trash bodies stay an OUTSTANDING item.
  - *Evidence:* main.cjs assets DELETE l.3090-3125 sets mf.deleted_at and moves the directory into root/.trash.
- **MV-R14 [minor] — refuted.** No change. A file naming a folder created after a window loaded is today's upload behaviour too, and is not caused by moves.
  - *Evidence:* Only files move. The destination folder must already exist in the destination project (validated), and realtimeMerge INSERT upserts by id.

#### Decisions

- **MV-1** Session 1 scope.
- Movable: live files rows. Within one project, folder_id only, on any provider and any surface. Cloud cross-project, by the MV-3 matrix. Between two Local Server projects on one computer, by moving the real file (answer 2).
- Refused, each with a plain sentence: folders, local-to-cloud and cloud-to-local moves, managed (entity-bound) local files across projects, google_drive bodies.
- Deletes: files only.
  - *Why:* No production writer of custom folders exists, and board folders arrive in Session 4 (answer 6). Managed files take their directory from their entity, so a cross-project move has no home for the body without inventing one.
- **MV-2** public.moth_move_items(p_file_ids uuid[], p_dest_project uuid, p_dest_folder uuid DEFAULT NULL, p_plan_only boolean DEFAULT false, p_bodies jsonb DEFAULT NULL) RETURNS jsonb. It is SECURITY DEFINER, search_path=public, REVOKEd from PUBLIC and anon, and granted to authenticated. The work is done by an internal fn_moth_apply_moves (REVOKEd from every client role).

Checks, in order, all before any write:
(1) Membership guard (0082 shape): 'not_a_workspace_member'.
(2) 1 to 500 distinct ids, else empty or too_many.
(3) The destination project passes _moth_target_eligible('project', dest, false). A non-NULL destination folder must have project_id = dest and pass _moth_target_eligible('folder', …). Failure gives not_found.
(4) Take the advisory locks of the destination and every source, in id order, with no row held (0118 as built). Then lock the destination and every source project row FOR SHARE, ordered by id, the destination folder likewise. Then lock the files FOR UPDATE, ordered by id. Each file must pass _moth_target_eligible('file', id, false), else not_found, before anything else is said about it.
(5) moth_can_write_target on the source and the destination: not_allowed_source or not_allowed_destination.
(6) Cross-project rules: the MV-3 matrix; asset_versions.file_id gives is_an_asset_version; ingestion_chunks.file_id gives has_extracted_text; for supabase, no storage.objects row at storage_path gives body_missing (bodies SFB-03); a key failing the bodies segment allowlist gives not_movable.
The plan phase returns {items:[{id, how:'metadata'|'copy_up'|'share_move'|'none', to_path}]} and writes nothing. The commit takes p_bodies [{file_id, path, thumbnail}] for copy_up and share_move items and re-derives everything. Errors are 'moth_move:<code>' with ERRCODE P0001.
  - *Why:* One transaction validates the whole selection, so the move commits whole or nothing changes. The plan phase lets a desktop do the physical work her answers require before the row changes. The server never takes a destination key or locator on the caller's word, and every hidden cause gives the same not_found (no oracle). Row locks are enough without folder moves: READ COMMITTED re-reads a row after its lock wait.
- **MV-3** Cross-project body matrix, by the source row's storage_provider.
- supabase or s3: metadata move. The body stays (bodies area).
- local_server (a private project's picture or video, answer 1): the destination must be a shared project or the caller's own personal space, else only_on_a_computer. The client must be the desktop that holds the body (localServerProvider.exists); elsewhere it says 'Move it from the computer it was added on.' how='copy_up'. The plan mints to_path in the company's chosen storage. For supabase or s3 the key is projects/<dest>/project/<dest>/<epoch ms>-<name sanitised to [A-Za-z0-9._-]>. For network_share it is nas:<network_share_folder_of(dest)>/<leaf>. The provider comes from fn_moth_body_provider_for(ws): the active provider (mode 'byos' only, otherwise petal) petal→supabase, s3→s3, network→network_share. The desktop uploads (or, on a NAS, copies) the body. The commit checks the key's shape and rabbit_file_key_admissible (unclaimed, object present for supabase), or network_share_locator_ok plus the unique locator index. It then sets storage_provider, storage_path and thumbnail_url (path||'.jpg' only if supplied in that form) in the same UPDATE. After commit, the desktop moves its local copy into <local media root>/.trash for 30 days.
- network_share (answers 2 and 5 by analogy): desktop only, else needs_desktop. how='share_move', to_path = nas:<dest folder>/<leaf>, and name_taken if a row already holds it. The desktop renames the file within the share and checks it arrived. The commit recomputes to_path and must get the same value, else plan_changed.
- google_drive: not_movable.
  - *Why:* Answer 1: a copy-up, only from the computer that holds the body. Answer 2 rejects 'leave the file in the old folder and remember where it is' for one-computer projects, and answer 5 says WILSON acts as a file manager. Applied to the office server, a cross-project move puts the real file in the destination's folder (NAS area NS-D5/D8's moved-in-rows handling becomes unnecessary). A key minted under the destination carries no origin id.
- **MV-4** Row effects. Cross-project: project_id goes to the destination, and folder_id to the destination folder (or NULL). phase_id, asset_id, task_id, scene_id, shot_id, level_id and experience_id go to NULL, and is_core_definer to false. Storage columns are written only for copy_up and share_move items. A legacy thumbnail_url that is not storage_path||'.jpg' goes to NULL, and a storage_gc_queue row is written (reason 'file-purged', kind 'thumbnail', file_id), per bodies SB-10 and SFB-08. Within one project, only folder_id changes and links are kept. is_financial, tags, document_kind and description are never written.
  - *Why:* Entity links have no same-project key (0000/0043 single-column SET NULL), so a link into the old project would dangle. Remapping links would invent relationships. Clearing the core flag stops a moved file steering the destination's agent context.
- **MV-5** Journal: public.file_moves. One row per operation. items jsonb holds, per file: id, how, from and to project, from and to folder, the seven from-links, was_core_definer, and from and to provider, path and thumbnail. Also undo_of, undone_at, txid bigint DEFAULT txid_current(), and the audit columns. It is written before the row updates. Then set_config('wilson.file_move', id, true); after the updates the marker is cleared. fn_checked_move_admits(p_file, p_project, p_folder, p_provider, p_path) is true only when the marker's row has txid = txid_current(), actor = auth.uid(), and an item matching all five values. It is REVOKEd from client roles. ENABLE + FORCE RLS, no policies, ALL REVOKEd from anon and authenticated. purge_file_moves after 30 days, under cron 'wilson-purge-file-moves' at a minute measured free at build (04:41 was free in the tables area's slot list).
  - *Why:* Undo restores exact links from the server's own record (S4c-09). The marker binds each bypass to a single journalled row, so an unrelated or future definer fails closed (SEC-MV-11, bodies SEC-11 and SFB-11). Nobody reads the table, so recording private ids in it leaks nothing.
- **MV-6** public.moth_undo_move(p_move_id uuid, p_plan_only boolean DEFAULT false) RETURNS jsonb. Membership guard, then the journal row FOR UPDATE, requiring workspace = current, actor = auth.uid(), and not undone (already_undone otherwise). Then the read gate on every from and to project and every file (not_found). Then every file must sit at its to_project, to_folder and to_path and be live, else changed_since and nothing moves. The reverse items replay through fn_moth_apply_moves with full validation, links restored only where the target still exists in the origin project. Copy-up items keep their company-storage body. Share items plan from_path for the desktop to rename back first. Then undone_at is set, and the new row carries undo_of. Redo = moth_undo_move(<the undo's id>).
  - *Why:* One function and a server-held record in both directions, and an undo can never reach a project the caller has lost. The local copy of a copied-up body sits in the desktop's trash, so the row does not go back down to the computer.
- **MV-7** Integrity for every writer:
- folders gets UNIQUE (id, project_id).
- files.folder_id: a composite FK (folder_id, project_id) → folders(id, project_id) ON DELETE SET NULL (folder_id), NOT VALID then VALIDATE, replacing 0043's FK (found by catalogue).
- folders.parent_id: a composite FK → folders(id, project_id) ON DELETE CASCADE.
A folder never changes project or id for a client: fn_folders_lock_guard (0111) refuses both with one answer, whatever the folder holds. The FKs stop every other writer while a folder has files or children. Client changes to project_id, folder_id and the locator columns are refused by the bodies guard and the NAS guard. Both admit a server-side change only when fn_checked_move_admits, rekey_file_body or the catalogue writer allows it. RI SET NULL stays admitted.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: folder_id has no same-project check, and files.project_id is client-writable today (FILE_COLUMNS, patchFile). The FKs hold for definers and migrations too. The draft's money-folder arm is dropped (MV-R1).
- **MV-8** Events.
- A project change writes the bodies area's moved_out (old project) and moved_in (new project), with old_path and new_path NULL and details '{}'.
- A folder change within one project writes 'refiled' with details {from_folder_id, to_folder_id}.
- fn_file_events_capture skips its 'moved' arm when project_id also changes.
- Edit history comes from trg_files_edit_history, and the actor is the caller (the definer keeps the JWT).
- Moth history comes from the tables area's capture on files (move, trash, restore).
  - *Why:* No other project's id, path or locator reaches the other side's readers. 'moved' keeps its meaning (a locator change within one project).
- **MV-9** fn_realtime_broadcast, restated from its latest body (0098, or the NAS area's restatement if that lands first). After IF v_project IS NULL THEN RETURN NULL: IF TG_OP='UPDATE' AND (to_jsonb(OLD)->>'project_id') IS DISTINCT FROM (to_jsonb(NEW)->>'project_id') THEN broadcast_changes('rabbit:project:'||old,'DELETE','DELETE',TG_TABLE_NAME,TG_TABLE_SCHEMA,NULL,OLD); broadcast_changes('rabbit:project:'||new,'INSERT','INSERT',…,NEW,NULL); RETURN NULL. The money return stays first, unchanged.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: the files arm routes by COALESCE(NEW,OLD), so the old window kept a stale row and the new topic would have received OLD. realtimeMerge handles INSERT and DELETE with no client change. Writing no 'files' literal keeps the suite 95, 99 and 101 text pins true.
- **MV-10** Cloud delete and restore: moth_trash_files(uuid[]) and moth_restore_files(uuid[]) RETURN integer, SECURITY DEFINER. Membership guard; 1 to 500 ids. For each id, in id order: _moth_target_eligible('file', id, true) (restore needs deleted_at set), else 'moth_trash:not_found'; then PERFORM soft_delete_row('files', id) or restore_soft_deleted('files', id), with fn_trash_authz unchanged. Any raise rolls back the batch.

moth_trash_index(p_limit int DEFAULT 200, p_before timestamptz DEFAULT NULL) RETURNS TABLE (id, project_id, project_title, folder_id, name, mime_type, size_bytes, kind, deleted_at, deleted_by_label, purges_at, can_restore, needs_desktop). Membership guard first. Rows are trashed files with _moth_target_eligible('file', id, true), deleted_at > now()-30 days, newest first, capped at 500. can_restore = moth_can_write_target('project', p). needs_desktop = network_share. No locator columns.
  - *Why:* This follows her ruling that deletes go through the existing paths. Moth adds only money-for-everyone and owner-only personal spaces. Trashed bodies are unreadable (bodies SB-16), so the trash shows metadata only.
- **MV-11** Deleting an office-server file (answer 5).
From the desktop, after the confirmation:
(1) moth_trash_files, or R.A.B.B.I.T.'s deleteFile.
(2) POST /api/rabbit/network-share/bin {file_id, locator}. Main checks the root is reachable and the locator is contained and real-path-inside the root. It renames the file into <its project folder>/.wilson-bin/<file_id>-<leaf> and writes a sidecar <file_id>.json {locator, trashed_at}. It confirms the source is gone and the target is present.
(3) If the rename fails, the client restores the row and says why.
Restore and undo: POST …/unbin. The file is renamed back (refused with a sentence if the folder has a file of that name again), then moth_restore_files.
After 30 days, POST …/sweep-bin removes sidecar entries older than 30 days. It runs on every catalogue walk and when the trash view opens on the desktop; the row is purged by purge_soft_deleted. In a browser: 'Delete it from the desktop app.'
  - *Why:* Her answer: the real file leaves its folder, with confirmation, undo and a trash view. The row is trashed first, so a crash leaves the file where it was, never lost. The bin is a dot folder on the same share, so the catalogue walk skips it (NS-D7) and the rename never crosses volumes.
- **MV-12** Local Server trash (electron/fileTrash.cjs, behind the launch token).
- DELETE …/files/:id: in ONE bundle write, the row moves to bundle.trashedFiles as {row, trashed_at, trash_name, base_kind} and a 'trashed' event is logged. Then the body is renamed to <base>/.trash/<id>-<safe basename>.
- Restore (POST …/files/:id/restore; batch POST …/files/restore): rename back first, then write the row back whole with a 'restored' event. locateTrashedBody checks both places, so a crash on either side is recoverable.
- Managed files keep their deleted_at and gain POST …/managed-files/:id/restore, excluding rows whose asset is gone.
- POST …/files/trash {ids} is the Moth batch, with isMothExcluded giving 404.
- GET …/trash is read-only. POST …/trash/purge-expired unlinks entries older than 30 days and logs 'purged' with blob_removed taken from what unlink actually did.
- relink-apply refuses while trash bodies would be stranded.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: today's DELETE is permanent and managed files have no restore. A separate key keeps every bundle.files reader unchanged. The base dir keeps the body on the same volume and under the same ACLs.
- **MV-13** Local Server physical moves (electron/fileMoves.cjs).
- POST …/projects/:projectId/files/move {moves:[{id, to_folder_id}]}: within one project, folder_id only, one atomic write.
- POST /api/rabbit/files/move-between-projects {from_project_id, to_project_id, moves:[{id, to_folder_id}]} (answer 2). Validate both bundles, excluded rows, destination folders and leaf collisions. Write a journal <rabbit-data>/moves/<op>.json. Copy each body into the destination base dir: rename on the same volume, else copy, fsync, size+sha1 check, unlink. Write the destination bundle with the rows (links cleared, ids kept), then the source bundle without them, then delete the journal. Answer only after reading back.
- On start-up and before each new operation, recovery rolls forward when the destination bundle holds the rows, else back.
- The same journal records cloud share moves and copy-ups, for the renderer to resolve on next boot.
  - *Why:* Her answer: the real file moves into the other project's folder, nothing is uploaded, and it is checked before WILSON says it is done. Two bundle writes cannot be atomic, so a journal makes every crash point recoverable.
- **MV-14** Desktop gates. All new routes are /api paths under applyLocalServerLock. Side effects only on POST and DELETE. No new IPC. Every fs path goes through resolveContainedFilePath against a server-chosen base (and real-path-inside the root for the share). Names are derived from row ids, never from the request. The routes never serve trashed bytes.
  - *Why:* These are the house's existing gates (the S14/S35 containment class). The lax cookie is not sent on cross-site POSTs.
- **MV-15** Moth undo: src/tools/moth/state/mothHistory.js (lazy chunk). An in-memory stack of inverse operations, capped at 10 each way, serialised. Entries name move_id, ids and project ids, never the open project. It is cleared on account, workspace or adapter change (an operation finishing under an older generation is dropped). It survives R.A.B.B.I.T. project switches. Mutators are refused during a replay. A failed undo puts the entry back. Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y work only while currentPage==='moth'. The toast goes through useRabbit().showUndoToast.
  - *Why:* RabbitProvider's stack is per project visit and cleared by a switch. This stack avoids that and the S3b-10, S5c-05, S5d-01 and S5d-02 traps.
- **MV-16** R.A.B.B.I.T. parity: deleteFile and deleteManagedFile push a visit-guarded history step and an undo toast. Their inverses are restoreFile plus the row re-insert, and restoreManagedFile. For network_share rows on the desktop, delete and restore also bin and unbin; in a browser they are refused with the desktop sentence. A new ctx verb, noteFilesChangedElsewhere(projectIds), reloads the open project when it is involved and realtime is not live.
  - *Why:* restoreFile has zero callers today, and FileManager's local delete stops being permanent.
- **MV-17** Client gating and words. Move to, Delete and Restore read moth_islands and canOnProject('project.moth.write', {ready,…}); denials show through GatedAction with the reason. The confirmation lists the links that will be cleared (from rows the person can see) and says 'everyone on <project> will see it' when the audience widens. Moving into a personal space in an office-server company shows her answer 3 sentence: anyone who opens the server directly can find these files. Delete always asks first.
  - *Why:* Rulings 12a.8 and 12a.9 and answer 3: adding files to a personal space includes moving them in.
- **MV-18** Deferred (answer 6), with specs recorded for Session 4: folder soft delete (folders.deleted_at, fn_trash_authz restated from its latest body, purge_soft_deleted restated, a folder trash index), and folder and subtree moves with cycle and path checks, the trashed-subtree refusal and a workspace advisory lock. Session 1 creates only file_moves.
  - *Why:* Board folders are the first real input, and they come in Session 4.

#### Schema changes

- **public.folders** (restates from: new (0041 created the single-column FK)): ADD CONSTRAINT folders_id_project_key UNIQUE (id, project_id). Replace the parent_id FK (found by catalogue) with folders_parent_same_project_fk (parent_id, project_id) → folders(id, project_id) ON DELETE CASCADE, NOT VALID then VALIDATE. A rehearsal first counts cross-project parents on dev and staging. Post-conditions: exactly one FK on parent_id, composite, confdeltype 'c'.
- **public.files folder FK** (restates from: 0043_files_entity_and_folder_links.sql): Drop 0043's folder_id FK (by catalogue). ADD files_folder_same_project_fk (folder_id, project_id) → folders(id, project_id) ON DELETE SET NULL (folder_id), NOT VALID then VALIDATE, after a rehearsal count of cross-project folder_id rows. 0043's post-conditions 2 and 3 still hold (confdeltype 'n'), plus confdelsetcols = {folder_id}.
- **public.file_moves** (restates from: new): id uuid PK, workspace_id NOT NULL FK workspaces CASCADE, actor_user_id uuid NOT NULL DEFAULT auth.uid(), txid bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT txid_current(), items jsonb CHECK (array, 1 to 500), undo_of uuid FK file_moves ON DELETE SET NULL, undone_at timestamptz, created_at, created_by, updated_at, updated_by. trg_file_moves_audit BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE fn_audit_touch(). ENABLE + FORCE RLS, no policies, REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated; GRANT ALL TO service_role. Index (workspace_id, created_at). Suite NNN_file_moves.sql and its RLS_TABLES line in the same commit.
- **public.fn_checked_move_admits(uuid, uuid, uuid, text, text) RETURNS boolean** (restates from: new): SECURITY DEFINER STABLE, search_path=public, REVOKE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated. EXISTS file_moves m WHERE m.id = nullif(current_setting('wilson.file_move', true),'')::uuid AND m.txid = txid_current() AND m.actor_user_id = auth.uid() AND m.items @> jsonb_build_array(jsonb_build_object('id',p_file,'to_project',p_project,'to_folder',p_folder,'to_provider',p_provider,'to_path',p_path)).
- **public.fn_moth_body_provider_for(uuid) RETURNS text** (restates from: new): SECURITY DEFINER STABLE, revoked from client roles. Reads the active provider: a row whose mode is not 'byos' answers 'supabase', and otherwise 'network_share' when the provider is 'network', 's3' when it is 's3', else 'supabase'. A COMMENT ties it to storage/index.js activeWorkspaceProvider and fileProviderFor; for network, a copy-up targets the share and not local_server.
- **public.fn_moth_apply_moves(jsonb, uuid, boolean) / moth_move_items(uuid[], uuid, uuid, boolean, jsonb) / moth_undo_move(uuid, boolean)** (restates from: new): As MV-2, MV-3, MV-4 and MV-6. Error codes: not_a_workspace_member, empty, too_many, not_found, not_allowed_source, not_allowed_destination, only_on_a_computer, needs_desktop, not_movable, is_an_asset_version, has_extracted_text, body_missing, name_taken, plan_changed, already_undone, changed_since. The two public functions are granted to authenticated; the core to none. Pre-flight RAISEs unless all of these exist: projects.personal_owner_id; _moth_target_eligible; moth_can_write_target; rabbit_file_key_admissible; storage_object_current_project named in the four base rabbit_files policies; the 'network_share' enum label; network_share_folder_of and network_share_locator_ok; fn_checked_move_admits named in fn_files_body_guard and fn_files_network_share_guard; the edit_history_select old-side clause. Post-conditions on comment-stripped bodies: has_active_membership, _moth_target_eligible, moth_can_write_target, FOR UPDATE, set_config('wilson.file_move', ingestion_chunks and asset_versions are all present; anon has no EXECUTE.
- **public.moth_trash_files(uuid[]) / moth_restore_files(uuid[]) RETURNS integer; public.moth_trash_index(int, timestamptz)** (restates from: new (0082 milestones_trash_index shape)): As MV-10. They call 0014 soft_delete_row and restore_soft_deleted, and 0092 fn_trash_authz, all unchanged. The index is STABLE. Post-conditions: no result column named storage_path, thumbnail_url or storage_provider; the body has has_active_membership.
- **public.purge_file_moves(interval) + cron 'wilson-purge-file-moves'** (restates from: new): DELETE rows older than p_retention (default 30 days; refuses anything under 1 day). SECURITY DEFINER, granted to service_role only. Scheduled in the 0014 guarded DO block.
- **file_events_event_check** (restates from: 0093_file_gateway.sql): 0093's discovery drop, then ADD with the union of every Session 1 area's terms, in ONE widening: uploaded, downloaded, moved, relinked, trashed, restored, purged, upload_abandoned, viewed_remote, catalogued, moved_out, moved_in, refiled. Post-condition: 13 terms, each once.
- **public.fn_file_events_capture()** (restates from: 0074_file_events_money_arm.sql): ONE restatement of 0074's whole body that carries all of: the bodies area's moved_out and moved_in arms (paths NULL, details '{}'); the NAS area's 'catalogued' and purge-detail arms; and this area's 'refiled' arm (OLD.folder_id IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.folder_id AND project_id unchanged), with the 'moved' arm guarded by project_id unchanged. The EXCEPTION idiom and is_financial on every INSERT are kept.
- **public.fn_realtime_broadcast()** (restates from: 0098_table_views.sql): MV-9's split, restated from the latest body, merged with the NAS area's two additions so that only one restatement exists. Suites 100 #7 and 101 #18 get the new md5; 95 #170, 99 #14 and 101 #16 are re-verified.

- **MV-D**: The core, moth_move_items, moth_undo_move, the trash wrappers, the index, the purge and its cron. Suites NNN_file_moves (RPC probes) and NNN_moth_trash.

#### Risks

- storage_path of a metadata-moved supabase or s3 file still names its origin project's uuid (and its original filename) to destination readers (bodies SEC-6). For personal-space files, this is the main Session 1 flow (acceptance 3).
  - *Mitigation:* Owned by the bodies area as an open question for her. Moves add no other channel (events, broadcast, history). Copy-ups mint under the destination. If she rules no, the bodies area's re-key or neutral key applies, and the move core gains a re-key arm inside the same transaction.
- The physical steps (copy-up, share rename, local cross-project) cannot share the cloud transaction. A crash leaves the body and the row disagreeing.
  - *Mitigation:* Order the steps so that no crash point loses the body. The desktop journal, resolved at boot, puts it right. Refused commits roll back the physical step. An orphaned upload is removed by delete_own within the hour, or by the orphan sweep.
- An office-server file binned by one desktop while another person deletes or recreates the same name directly on the server.
  - *Mitigation:* Unbin refuses an occupied name with a sentence. The catalogue keeps a trashed row's locator, so a recreated file is listed only after the purge. Recorded.
- Answer 5's principle applied to cross-project office-server moves (MV-3) is an inference from answers 2 and 5.
  - *Mitigation:* Stated in the walkthrough. If she prefers metadata-only, MV-3's share_move arm becomes 'metadata' and NS-D5's moved-in handling is restored. Nothing else changes.
- Several areas restate the same functions (fn_realtime_broadcast, fn_file_events_capture, file_events_event_check, the files guards).
  - *Mitigation:* One merged restatement per function in Session 1, with post-conditions naming every arm. The integrator assigns one owner per body.
- Local trash purge runs only when a project is opened or its trash viewed, so bodies of never-opened projects linger.
  - *Mitigation:* Stated in handbook §3.1 and in OUTSTANDING.
- An older R.A.B.B.I.T. undo entry for a moved file replays updateFile with the old project_id.
  - *Mitigation:* The guard refuses it with 42501, and RabbitProvider's undo swallows per-op errors. A test pins that it fails closed. noteFilesChangedElsewhere reloads the open project.

Plan: {items:[{id, how:'metadata'|'copy_up'|'share_move'|'none', to_path}]}.
Commit: {move_id, moved, links_cleared}.
p_bodies: [{file_id uuid, path text, thumbnail text|null}].
