#### Client changes

- **NEW src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/storage/networkShareProvider.js: createNetworkShareStorageProvider({available=hasLocalServer, fetchImpl}), networkShareUrl(locator,{download})**: The six registry functions. get, exists and getUrl call /api/rabbit/network-share/file/<per-segment encoded locator>; exists treats only 404 as false. put and del call the op routes and work on the desktop only. describe returns {provider:'network_share', label:'Company server', reachable}. Off the desktop, reads throw DESKTOP_PREVIEW_SENTENCE and writes throw DESKTOP_ACTION_SENTENCE. *(production caller: supabaseAdapter.js registerStorageProvider at module load; resolveFileProvider(row) in fileUrl, downloadUrl and downloadFile, which FilePreviewDialog and ProjectFilesExplorer call)*
- **NEW src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/networkShareCatalogue.js: refreshOnOpen, refreshNow, useNetworkShareCatalogue, shouldAutoRefresh, runNasOp, settlePending**: Eligibility as in NS-D9. A check posts the walk, then sends one apply per directory batch (p_final on the last), then the desktop removes the purge_ok bin items and moves restore_back items back. runNasOp: RPC, then the desktop op route, then settle ('done' or 'not_moved'), with a plain sentence for each failure. settlePending runs pending_mine and finishes each operation: from present and to absent means rename; to present and from absent means done; anything else is left with a sentence. Failures are never written to the cloud. *(production caller: RabbitProvider.jsx (project open, sign-in), ProjectFilesExplorer.jsx (banner, Refresh, delete, restore, add), the Moth move and trash commands (NAS branch))*
- **NEW electron/networkShare.cjs: mountNetworkShare, checkShareLocator, checkShareBinLocator, shareContain, SHARE_REFUSED_EXACT, SHARE_REFUSED_SUFFIX**: Every route requires isSameOriginRequest and the token, a UNC workspaceRootDir and reach-first, and does its file work in a Worker or with async bounded fs. POST /walk {folder locator} returns {dirs, bin, stopped_at}. GET/HEAD /file/*. POST /op {pairs:[{from,to}]}: each pair is shape-checked (locator or bin shape), contained, the target is stat'ed (ENOENT required), the parent is created and the file renamed, then verified; returns a result per pair. POST /put {source, to}: copyFile with COPYFILE_EXCL, then the size is verified. POST /purge {bin locators}: removes only bin-shaped paths. *(production caller: main.cjs startLocalServer mount; openPath.cjs share arm; networkShareProvider.js and networkShareCatalogue.js)*
- **NEW electron/rootReachable.cjs: createRootReachability({askRoot, waitMs, ttlMs, offTtlMs, maxAsking})**: Only the question machinery and markOff(root), injected. *(production caller: main.cjs, which builds the instance shared by mountCloudBins (when no seam is present) and mountNetworkShare)*

#### Migration order

- **NS-1: <n>_storage_provider_network_share.sql**: ALTER TYPE ADD VALUE 'network_share' and its post-condition, nothing else.
  - *Constraints:* Its own migration. Applied and committed on wilson-dev before NS-2 is rehearsed. It may land early, before the bodies area's migration (that area orders itself after any NAS ALTER TYPE). Free numbers are remeasured on every ref first.
- **NS-2: <m>_network_share_catalogue.sql + suites <a>_network_share_checks.sql, <b>_network_share_ops.sql, <c>_network_share_catalogue.sql (one commit)**: Pre-flight; the helpers; the projects guard and slug CHECK; the files columns, CHECKs, indexes and guard; both tables with RLS, policies and grants; the RPCs; the purge cron; the restatements of fn_realtime_broadcast and fn_file_events_capture, the event CHECK and fn_network_share_checks_broadcast; post-conditions. The same commit adds the RLS_TABLES lines for both tables, all three suites in rls.yml's replay list, the rlsSuiteLists tail pins, and the restated pins in 101 (probe 18), 100 (probe 7) and 95 (probe 170).
  - *Constraints:* Lands after the personal-space migration (personal_owner_id), the repairs migrations A, B and C (edit_history privacy arm), the bodies migration (fn_files_body_guard) and the moves migration. It is the last Session 1 restater of both shared functions and restates from their latest bodies, checked by md5 in the pre-flight. Rehearse as a rolled-back transaction with all three suites (collected = planned), and include a 5,000-entry apply with its time and row counts recorded. Suites 20, 33, 35, 60 and 96 must pass before push.
- **NS-3: desktop and client commits after NS-2 is on dev**: (a) rootReachable extraction; (b) networkShare.cjs, the openPath share arm and the pick-files authorization; (c) the provider, storage/index vocabulary and registration; (d) networkShareCatalogue.js, the adapter methods, realtimeMerge and RabbitProvider; (e) the Files, FileManager, D.O.G. reference and Admin Storage changes; (f) fixtures; (g) handbook §3.1, §4.4, §4.5, §12.1/12.1a, §12.3, §12.7/12.7b/12.7c, §12.9 residual 3 and §17, plus OUTSTANDING (the accepted residuals) and RELEASE_TESTING (a desktop check on a real share).
  - *Constraints:* No export lands before its caller. writeGate.test.js sees ready in both canOnProject calls. Full vitest with --maxWorkers=4. Bundle measured before and after; growth over 20 KB is accounted for. Moth stays lazy-loaded.

#### Tests

- **supabase/tests/rls/<a>_network_share_checks.sql**: Read by a member of a public project; read by the creator and an admin of a private project; read by the personal-space owner. Read count 0 for another tenant, an inactive member, a non-creator of a private project and a trashed project. Client INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE throw. relforcerowsecurity holds. files_seen and last_checked_by change after a second caller's apply. Planted controls: GRANT INSERT plus a permissive policy turns the INSERT probe red; replacing the hop with true turns the private and trashed probes red.
- **supabase/tests/rls/<b>_network_share_ops.sql**: authenticated SELECT and INSERT fail (no grant). RLS is enabled and forced with zero policies. One pending operation per file (unique). The purge removes only settled rows older than 90 days. Planted: GRANT SELECT to authenticated turns the read probe red.
- **supabase/tests/rls/<c>_network_share_catalogue.sql**: Apply: three entries make three rows; a second apply in the same transaction keeps the ids; a size change updates the row; a complete directory without an entry marks it missing and seeing it again clears the mark; a vanished subfolder marks its rows; an incomplete directory marks nothing; a rename keeps the id and records 'moved'; a sequence entry is admitted; the event is 'catalogued' and edit_history is unreadable to a non-creator manager. Refusals: a member seat (not allowed), another workspace, a private project for a non-creator (all the same message), a local root or root mismatch (drive_changed), no folder_root (no_folder), an unplaced claim (folders_unplaced), a nested private project (folder_shared), 5,001 entries, 61 calls (too_soon). Entries with money, legal, _DATABASES, dot, '..', '\\', ':', U+202E or non-NFC are dropped. An entry at a pending operation's locator is not inserted, and a trashed twin plus a rename applies cleanly. Guard: a direct INSERT after an apply in the same transaction gives 42501; client updates of storage_path, deleted_at, project_id or size give 42501; soft_delete_row and moth_move_items on a NAS row give 42501; description and tags edits succeed; folder_id set to NULL by a folder delete succeeds. Operations: trash sets share_bin_path and a pending operation; settle 'not_moved' restores the row; restore when occupied is refused; a move computes the target under the destination folder and clears links; undo replays the origin; adopt_local switches the provider; record_added records 'uploaded'; a purge writes a certificate with body 'company server bin', leaves storage_gc_queue unchanged, and the bin id then appears in purge_ok. Projects guard: overlapping folder_root refused; a member's slug change refused; slug 'a/b' violates the CHECK. anon has no EXECUTE on any new definer. Planted controls: drop the files guard; remove the claims test; make step 7 undelete; revert 'catalogued'; drop the shape CHECK (asserted by conname); remove the GUC reset. Each turns its own probe red.
- **supabase/tests/rls/96, 33, 20, 60, 101, 100, 95 (extend or restate)**: 96: gateway bodies mention no public.files, and no NAS row is served. 33: 'catalogued' is admitted and an unknown term refused. 20: text probes for the files-arm early return, plus a hosted run. 60: workspace_storage_provider_chk is still exactly (petal, network, s3). 101/18, 100/7 and 95/170 are restated to the new body. 101/17, 72 and 99 keep 18 tables (thin trigger).
- **electron/networkShare.test.js, rootReachable.test.js, openPath.test.js, rabbitBins tests, src/.../networkShareParity.test.js**: Walker on a real temp folder: the exclusions at any depth; no link or junction followed; per-directory records; a sequence collapsed to one entry (parity with detectSequence); EACCES gives an incomplete directory; limits and deadline give incomplete; next_start rotation. Routes: cross-origin and same-site requests get 403; a drive-letter root is refused before any fs call; a money or dot locator is refused before stat; the root itself cannot be walked; 503 when the root is off; ranges work; /op refuses an existing target and a non-bin dot path; /put refuses an unpicked source; /purge accepts bin shapes only. A spy finds no sync fs on the main thread during GET, the walk or the open IPC. Parity: the CJS refusal lists equal the SQL function text. The bins tests stay green unchanged. Planted: dropping the _DATABASES suffix, following links, skipping reach-first. Each turns its probe red.
- **vitest: storageRegistry, networkShareProvider, networkShareCatalogue, realtimeMerge, fileTree, filePreview, fileActions, ProjectFilesExplorer (mounted), useReferences, rlsSuiteLists, columnAllowlist, writeGate**: Registry: the label matches NS-1 and is registered. Provider: reads off the desktop give the exact sentence with no fetch; writes off the desktop give the action sentence. Catalogue: eligibility (seat, privacy, unc, not offline, cool-down); runNasOp settles 'not_moved' when the op route fails; settlePending's four cases. realtimeMerge yields files-refetch. Explorer: Refresh is gated for a member and enabled for a manager; the web says Refresh from the desktop app; a failed walk keeps last checked; the personal-space notice is shown in a network workspace. D.O.G.: the 50 MB refusal. columnAllowlist: the new columns are absent. Every new test is shown red first.

#### Risks

- The checker's server permissions decide what the whole project sees (NS-Q1).
  - *Mitigation:* Roster seat only, until she answers. EACCES directories count as incomplete. Plain sentences on Storage and Files. Recorded in OUTSTANDING.
- A crash or failure between the server write and the real file move.
  - *Mitigation:* network_share_ops journal, settle 'not_moved' and settlePending at sign-in. Stale in-folder operations go to check-seat holders after 24 h. Cross-project pending operations stay with their creator for 24 hours, then may be called off, not_moved alone, by whoever may check both ends (0118 as built: the service_role route first written here could not work, because the RPC refuses a NULL workspace; written in the handbook).
- Write volume on a first check (5,000 rows, history and events).
  - *Mitigation:* Realtime is one signal. The rehearsal records time and counts. Calls are per directory batch. edit_history is purged at 90 days, and the moth_history capture labels check causes (interface).
- Several Session 1 migrations restate fn_realtime_broadcast, fn_file_events_capture and the event CHECK.
  - *Mitigation:* NS-2 lands last and restates from md5-checked latest bodies. Post-conditions pin every arm by name, and the pin suites are restated in the same commit.
- Pre-C4 receipts sitting in <slug>_FILES on the share carry no flag.
  - *Mitigation:* Not detectable from metadata. Handbook §12.9 residual 3 is extended to the catalogue.
- Bin items of a project that is purged whole are never walked again, so they stay on the server.
  - *Mitigation:* The certificate says 'company server bin'. Admin Storage notes it. OUTSTANDING records it with the measurement.
- A rename made on the server and split across walk calls, or any move other than an exact rename, gets a new id.
  - *Mitigation:* Labelled 'Not found on the server at the last check'. A wider relink match is a later improvement, written in OUTSTANDING.
- No end-to-end desktop proof against wilson-dev.
  - *Mitigation:* pgTAP on dev, vitest on real temp folders, fixtures for pictures, and a RELEASE_TESTING check for the owner on a real share.

#### Interfaces with the other areas

- **body provider value** (provides): public.storage_provider 'network_share' (FILE_PROVIDERS.NETWORK_SHARE). storage_path 'nas:<root-relative path>'. thumbnail_url always NULL. Never money or Legal. Never gc-enqueued. A partial unique index on live rows by (workspace_id, lower(storage_path)), compatible with the bodies area's partial storage_path index that excludes network_share.
- **files column guard and trusted writers** (provides): fn_files_network_share_guard (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON files). GUC wilson.network_share_writer = 'check:<project uuid>' | 'op:<file uuid>' | ''. Trusted writers: network_share_catalogue_apply, network_share_record_added, _trash, _restore, _move, _adopt_local, _settle and _undo only. It composes with the bodies area's fn_files_body_guard (the client INSERT allowlist excludes network_share; definers pass).
- **move operation for NAS rows** (provides): network_share_move(p_file_ids uuid[], p_dest_project uuid, p_dest_folder uuid) RETURNS jsonb [{op_id, id, from, to}]; network_share_settle(p_op_ids uuid[], p_outcome 'done'|'not_moved', p_facts jsonb); network_share_undo(p_op_id uuid). The moves area's moth_move_items must refuse network_share rows with 'nas_move_on_desktop', and its project_id-change trigger must admit network_share_move (which sets that area's move GUC). Its 'refiled' events cover NAS cross-project moves. The Moth undo stack stores NAS entries by op_id.
- **delete and restore for NAS rows** (provides): network_share_trash(p_file_ids uuid[]) and network_share_restore(p_file_ids uuid[]), returning [{op_id,id,from,to}], then settle. Both use soft_delete_row and restore_soft_deleted underneath (fn_trash_authz unchanged). moth_trash_files and moth_restore_files must route NAS rows here, and moth_trash_index lists them with on_server=true. In a browser: DESKTOP_ACTION_SENTENCE.
- **copy-up and add** (provides): network_share_adopt_local(p_file_id, p_dest_project, p_dest_folder) for answer 1 (a local_server body moved into a shared project or personal space when the company storage is the office server). network_share_record_added(p_project, p_root, p_folder_id, p_entries jsonb <= 50) for adds, the personal space included (answer 3). The bodies and personal areas call these instead of uploadFile's network refusal.
- **file_events vocabulary** (provides): Adds 'catalogued' (check INSERTs). A WILSON add stays 'uploaded'. No 'moved' row is written when project_id changes in the same UPDATE. A purged certificate's details.body is 'company server bin' or 'left on the company server'. The final CHECK list, restated once, holds the 0093 nine plus 'refiled' (moves) plus 'catalogued'.
- **realtime** (provides): Files arm early return when the GUC = 'check:'||row.project_id. A new thin fn_network_share_checks_broadcast sends {id, project_id} to rabbit:project:<id>, so the trigger set stays 18. Workspace channel: this area adds nothing, because neither files nor network_share_checks is on fn_workspace_realtime_broadcast (0018); the repairs area's suppression is needed only for the projects rows of personal spaces.
- **personal-space classifier** (needs): projects.personal_owner_id (personal area), with personal spaces is_private=true. The owner-only write rule (personal_owner_id = auth.uid(), with no admin leg, for both the check seat and file operations). The reserved folder is '_WILSON_PERSONAL/<owner uuid>', computed only by network_share_space_folder. Admin reading from Files is recorded by that area (answer 4).
- **edit_history privacy arm** (needs): The repairs area's Migration B gives edit_history_select a fail-closed project-privacy arm (passes_project_privacy or a live-parent hop, with personal spaces owner-and-admin through is_private). NS-2's pre-flight checks for it by an anchor string that area names.
- **history capture** (needs): moth_history (tables area) captures NAS rows' structure (name, folder, project) and never storage_path or share_bin_path. When the GUC starts 'check:', the cause is labelled 'office server check' and may be coalesced, at that area's choice. 'catalogued' rows count as additions from the first baseline.
- **desktop-only identity** (provides): Moth node key {scope:'cloud'|'local', id}. Local nodes come from the local adapter only, labelled 'On this computer only', with no live features. A migrated local project is hidden by bundle.cloudMigration[ws].carried. Local-to-local moves of the real file (answer 2) belong to the moves area's electron code, not these RPCs.

### 2.5 The Moth tables and history

Findings: 1 partly, 28 taken.

**Scope as resolved:** Moth tables and Moth history: moth_history (built in Session 1), plus the reviewed starting specs for the tables her answer 6 puts in later sessions (S2 views and layouts; S3 tags, links and shared notes; S4 boards and sticky locks).

**Summary:** Session 1 creates one table in this area, public.moth_history. Her answer 6 settles that the other eleven Moth tables arrive in the sessions that first use them. moth_local_scopes and file_body_locations are not created: the NAS area drops local scopes, the bodies area needs no table, and answer 6's Session 1 list names neither. moth_history is cloud-only. A project kept only on one computer shows 'not recorded' and is never mirrored to the cloud.

How history is kept: there is no baseline copy. A 'recording since' mark is written per workspace and per type. Every structural change stores the full metadata projection of both sides, the before and the after, so the past is rebuilt backwards from today's live graph. The 90-day purge is then a plain DELETE.

Capture runs on eleven tables through two triggers per table: AFTER INSERT OR DELETE, and AFTER UPDATE with a pinned list of structural columns in its WHEN clause. Both call one SECURITY DEFINER function. It resolves the workspace the way 0012 does, including the parent join that phases needs, and snapshots each side's audience: shared, private with its creator, or personal with its owner. It never skips a trash, restore, move or privacy change. A failed capture never blocks the user's write; instead it writes a scoped 'recording restarts here' mark.

Money, Legal and locked folders: when capture sees a locked side, it writes nothing new and redacts every earlier row of that entity. That is the one exception to append-only. A file that becomes money therefore loses its history for good, even after it is purged.

Reading: no client role can touch the table. Two SECURITY DEFINER RPCs read it. A side of a row is shown only when the reader is in that side's audience as it was at capture and also passes the Moth project gate now, which is the personal area's _moth_target_eligible: owner-only for personal spaces, creator or admin for private projects. An entity is hidden whole when it is excluded now. A half-visible row comes back as a plain 'added' or 'removed'. No raw ids, counts or hidden scopes come back; paging uses an opaque row_key.

Retention: purge_moth_history runs at 04:45. The migration lands last in Session 1, because its pre-flight requires the personal-space, classifier, folder-lock, moves and NAS objects.

Later-session specs carry the review fixes: every target hop goes through moth_can_read_target and every write through moth_can_write_target; no target ids sit in readable JSON; boards are kept out of locked folders; intra-Moth foreign keys are composite with the workspace; history gains per-side peer scopes before S3 captures any edge; and CHECK widening on moth_history is split into NOT VALID then VALIDATE.

#### Findings and their dispositions

- **SEC-01 [blocker] — taken.** S2 and S3 specs: every target hop (positions, refs, assignments, both relationship endpoints, board files) is moth_can_read_target(kind, typed_id) from the personal area, which is owner-only for personal spaces, has no admin leg and excludes money. Every write is moth_can_write_target. S2 restates _moth_target_eligible to add the phase, asset, task, scene, shot, level and experience kinds. Probes: an admin reads 0 positions or relationships into another person's space. Planted control: swap the hop for plain RLS and the probe goes red.
  - *Evidence:* 0072 projects_select admits current_app_role()='admin' to private projects; the personal area makes a personal space is_private=true with created_by=owner (projects_personal_shape_chk), so caller RLS alone admits admins. The draft hops for moth_layout_positions and moth_relationships had no personal arm.
- **SEC-02 [blocker] — taken.** S2: config may hold no uuid-shaped value (CHECK). References become rows with typed generated FKs, each filtered per reader by moth_can_read_target, live. S4: board_elements SELECT hides an element whose file_id fails moth_can_read_target('file', file_id) for that reader, the write trigger refuses one, and the client draws no tile. Probes: a member of P gets no ref or element into private X or a personal space; an admin gets none after the file is flagged.
  - *Evidence:* Draft moth_views kept refs in config JSON, validated once as the writer, and read by every reader of the view. Draft board_elements.file_id was validated once at write time.
- **SEC-03 [blocker] — taken.** Redact-on-lock replaces the skip. When capture classifies any side of a files or folders row as locked, it UPDATEs every earlier moth_history row of that entity to prior=NULL, state=NULL, locked=true, and writes no new row. Each row is gated on NOT locked, and the entity is hidden when any of its rows is locked. A trash, purge or hard delete of a locked row therefore leaves nothing readable. Probe: create, rename, flag, trash, purge: an admin and a member each read 0 rows, and as postgres every row of F is locked with NULL projections. Planted control: remove the redaction and the probe goes red.
  - *Evidence:* The draft skipped capture when every side was locked, and its gate for a vanished entity read the newest row's last non-NULL side. 0067 purge_soft_deleted hard-deletes trashed files after 30 days, so a flagged file's pre-money rows would come back after the purge. 0088 file_row_is_money confirms the flag axis.
- **SEC-04 [major] — taken.** moth_can_write_scope is dropped. Writes use the personal area's moth_can_write_target, which starts from _moth_target_eligible (current_workspace_id, has_active_membership, a live project in that workspace) and answers FALSE uniformly otherwise. History itself needs no write helper. Later suites keep the cross-tenant probe: a user of workspace B gets FALSE for A's shared project, the same as for a random uuid.
  - *Evidence:* 0013 can_write_project is `current_app_role() IN ('admin','manager') OR NOT project_is_staffed(p) OR project_role_for(p) IN (...)`, with no workspace or existence term. 0082 passes_project_privacy has no workspace term either.
- **SEC-05 [major] — taken.** Gaps are now restart marks (see T-04). A capture failure writes mark_recording with mark_kind='capture_failed', carrying each side's project, class and owner but no projection. The window lists it only to a reader who passes the same side test as the reader, owner-only for personal. A failure with no known scope is stored for operators and never returned. 'baseline' and 'suspended' marks are workspace-wide and say only that recording started or restarted for a type. There are no local marks. Probe: a restart inside a personal space is invisible to an admin and to another member, and visible to the owner.
  - *Evidence:* The draft's moth_history_b_shape_chk forced project ids to NULL on every mark, so every gap was workspace-wide. The local marks came from moth_local_scopes, which no longer exists.
- **SEC-06 [major] — taken.** The source, local_scope_id and 'observe' arms are removed. No caller-reported row can enter moth_history; its only writers are the capture trigger, the migration backfill and the purge. If a later session ever brings local history to the cloud, it must add a CHECK tying local rows to the scope owner and a scope-derived project. Recorded as a deferred constraint.
  - *Evidence:* The NAS design recommends removing moth_local_scopes. Her answer 6 lists Session 1's builds without it, and answer 2 keeps desktop-only moves on the computer.
- **SEC-07 [major] — taken.** A folder lock guard is a hard prerequisite, named in the pre-flight (interface fn_folders_lock_guard, owned by the personal-space and classifier area). It refuses, with 42501, any UPDATE that changes moth_folder_path_locked(path), and any non-root folder whose first path segment differs from its parent's. Probe in this suite: renaming INVOICES to FILES fails, and capture still holds no row for that folder.
  - *Evidence:* 0041: path is computed client-side and stored; GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON folders TO authenticated; folders_update checks only workspace and can_write_project; no folders guard trigger exists besides populate_workspace and touch.
- **SEC-08 [major] — taken.** S4 spec: boards and board_elements SELECT and WITH CHECK require moth_can_read_target('folder', boards.folder_id). That test excludes locked paths and is trustworthy because of SEC-07's guard. A board folder in a locked tree is refused.
  - *Evidence:* 0041 §4: a folder row is visible to any project reader. The draft boards SELECT checked only folders under caller RLS and the projects hop.
- **SEC-09 [major] — partly.** S1 drops overlay_owner_id (no writer; per-row overlays are the wrong shape). The projector emits only columns of the row itself. The S3 spec requires, before S3 captures anything, nullable per-side peer columns (prior_peer_project_id, prior_peer_class, prior_peer_owner_id, and the same for state), added with ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN. It also requires a 'workspace' class with a NULL project for workspace notes and company tags, and the per-side 'personal' class with an owner, never a row overlay. A side is visible only when its peer passes too.
  - *Evidence:* In S1, every captured edge stays within one project, or is already readable on its row. 0096 task_items FKs are composite (id, project_id). The moves area makes files(folder_id, project_id) and folders(parent_id, project_id) composite. The 0043 file entity links and 0084/0099 asset arrays are id-only, but they are columns of rows readers already see. S3 edges and workspace-level notes and tags do not fit one project per side.
- **SEC-10 [minor] — taken.** A new column, row_key uuid NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid() UNIQUE. The reader returns row_key and never the bigint, and pages by p_after row_key. The suite pins pg_get_function_result so that no bigint is returned.
  - *Evidence:* Draft: id BIGINT GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY, returned by moth_history_read and used for paging.
- **SEC-11 [minor] — taken.** S2 and S3 specs: canonical_id and every merge-only column are left out of client grants and written only by DEFINER RPCs. Every intra-Moth FK is composite (id, workspace_id), and a validate trigger raises the same SQLSTATE and message as an FK refusal. The uuid-only 23505 class is recorded as S4b-07's accepted class in each suite header.
  - *Evidence:* Draft grants included client-chosen ids; moth_tags.canonical_id had no stated grant; type_id was an id-only FK.
- **SEC-12 [minor] — taken.** History capture classifies files through a new row-image helper, moth_file_row_locked, which is the same predicate as _moth_target_eligible's file arm, so the two cannot drift (interface). S4 spec: sticky_locks has no client SELECT; claim, renew, release and status go through RPCs only.
  - *Evidence:* The personal area defines moth_can_write_target, and its classifier already adds the legal tag and expenses.file_ids @> ARRAY[f.id] (0037 expenses.file_ids uuid[]).
- **SEC-13 [minor] — taken.** No temp tables. Each distinct side project is checked once, in a CTE. Every new DEFINER runs with SET search_path = public, pg_temp, and every relation and function is schema-qualified.
  - *Evidence:* Draft: a 'temporary map' cache inside a STABLE SECURITY DEFINER with search_path=public.
- **T-01 [blocker] — taken.** Capture resolves the workspace in order: the row's workspace_id, then projects.workspace_id via project_id, then current_workspace_id(), then skip. The workspaces-exists skip runs only after that. Probe: inserting, renaming and cascade-deleting a phase each write a row. Planted control: remove the project join and the probe goes red.
  - *Evidence:* 0000 create table phases has no workspace_id. The 0061 comment says 'phases has no workspace_id (it is a parent-join table)'. 0012 fn_edit_history_capture resolves phases through projects, falling back to current_workspace_id().
- **T-02 [blocker] — taken.** Only action='update' can be skipped, and only when both projections, both project ids, classes and owners are unchanged. trash, restore and move are never skipped, and a privacy flip changes the class. Probes: trash, restore, a project-only move and an is_private flip each write a row. Planted control: skip on projection equality alone and the trash probe goes red.
  - *Evidence:* 0014 soft_delete_row is `UPDATE ... SET deleted_at`. The projector's allowlist has no deleted_at, project_id, is_private or personal_owner_id.
- **T-03 [major] — taken.** moth_history_b_entity_chk admits any '^[a-z_]{1,63}$' name on mark rows, and the 11-type list on change rows. The robustness probe narrows the CHECK, inserts a folder, and finds a capture_failed mark for 'folders'. Planted control: remove the mark insert and the probe goes red.
  - *Evidence:* Draft: the gap row used entity_type = TG_TABLE_NAME under the same entity CHECK.
- **T-04 [major] — taken.** Every gap is a restart. The window returns recording_since = GREATEST(latest visible mark for the type and scope, workspaces.created_at, now() - 90 days). The client labels everything before it 'not recorded'. Interval gaps and the period_from and period_to columns are removed. Probe: after an induced failure in P, the window's folders row for P equals the failure time.
  - *Evidence:* D2's backwards rebuild means one missed row corrupts every earlier state of that entity, so a zero-length gap labels nothing useful.
- **T-05 [major] — taken.** Mark rows may carry project ids, classes and owners, but never projections. Local scopes are removed. See SEC-05 for the window filter and its probes.
  - *Evidence:* Same CHECK text as SEC-05.
- **T-06 [major] — taken.** (2) and (8): after moving a file from X to P, the creator flips X to shared; member M, then manager N, read P and each get 'added' with prior NULL. Plants: a private arm returning true; a manager arm. (6): pg_get_triggerdef equals the pinned WHEN text. (7): the planted control is dropped; the skip stays as an efficiency defence pinned in the post-condition. Personal: the plant replaces the side's present-audience call with passes_project_privacy, and the admin aggregate probe goes red.
  - *Evidence:* Checked against the reader. (2) and (8): a private origin that is still private fails the present-audience gate first, so the class arm is never reached. (6): with T-02's skip, a description-only update is skipped even without the WHEN clause. (7): without the skip, the FK failure is caught by 0012-style handling and the delete still succeeds. The personal class arm is redundant while personal_owner_id is immutable (personal area identity guard).
- **T-07 [major] — taken.** The folders projection carries name = coalesce(label, slug) and slug. path stays out of the projection but in the WHEN clause, for lock reclassification. Probe: a label change writes 'update' whose prior.name differs from state.name.
  - *Evidence:* 0041 folders has slug, label and path; the draft allowlist had none of them.
- **T-08 [major] — taken.** The change was material. Her answer 6 is now the ruling. The plan edits to make (S1 scope, the S2 Database line, §4 suite numbers, the RLS_TABLES count) are listed under consumers for the controller's record.
  - *Evidence:* PLAN_MOTH §5 Session 1 'In scope: All section 4 schemas'; Session 2 'Database — proposed: S1 view/layout/preferences/history'. Her answer 6 (2026-11-02): views and layouts in S2; tags, links and shared notes in S3; boards and sticky locks in S4.
- **T-09 [major] — taken.** Later sessions widen moth_history's entity CHECK in a migration that does nothing else: SET lock_timeout = '5s'; DROP + ADD ... NOT VALID, restating the full list. A following migration runs VALIDATE CONSTRAINT. The projection allowlist lives in an IMMUTABLE function, so widening it is a CREATE OR REPLACE restating the whole latest body, md5-guarded.
  - *Evidence:* The capture INSERTs into moth_history after every structural write on the eleven tables, and DROP CONSTRAINT takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE until commit.
- **T-10 [minor] — taken.** Each table gets trg_<t>_moth_history (AFTER INSERT OR DELETE) and trg_<t>_moth_history_upd (AFTER UPDATE WHEN). The post-condition asserts, per table, the tgtype bits for INSERT, DELETE and UPDATE, and pins the UPDATE trigger's WHEN text with pg_get_triggerdef.
  - *Evidence:* A WHEN clause cannot name OLD on INSERT or NEW on DELETE, so each table needs two triggers.
- **T-11 [minor] — taken.** moth_history_project bounds its own output: text values cut to 300 characters, arrays capped at 200, and truncated=true when it cuts. The CHECK refuses only what the projector cannot emit. Probe: a 5 KB title still writes a row, with truncated set.
  - *Evidence:* 0099 assets.level_ids and experience_ids are uuid[] with no cardinality CHECK (as are 0084's scene_ids and shot_ids). tasks.title, assets.name and files.name have no length CHECK.
- **T-12 [minor] — taken.** Per-table key maps are stated in full (see the projector), including task_items entity_type to item_type and entity_id to item_id. Probe: a task_items create carries item_type and item_id.
  - *Evidence:* 0096 task_items columns are task_id, entity_type, entity_id and position.
- **T-13 [minor] — taken.** When the projects row cannot be read, the side class is NULL, and the reader's CASE treats NULL as not visible. Probe: after a private project's purge, no child row carries the class 'shared'.
  - *Evidence:* 0012 notes that on a cascade the parent row is gone when the child's AFTER DELETE fires.
- **T-14 [minor] — taken.** The CTE cache is kept, as in SEC-13. Paging is newest-first by (observed_at, id), resolved from the row_key. Rows from a long transaction that commits late can fall behind the cursor; this is documented, and the client re-reads the first page when the scrub opens.
  - *Evidence:* A STABLE plpgsql function cannot CREATE TEMP TABLE; PostgREST runs STABLE RPCs read-only.
- **T-15 [minor] — taken.** Later sessions restate fn_realtime_broadcast from whatever is latest at their tip (0098 today; S1's NAS and moves migrations restate it). The class CHECK lets 'private' have a NULL owner, which the reader admits for admins only. A CHECK never makes capture fail on real rows.
  - *Evidence:* 0101 line 37: 'fn_realtime_broadcast stays 0098's (md5 ...)'. 0000 projects.created_by is nullable, and 0007 dropped its FK.
- **T-16 [minor] — taken.** Every later intra-Moth FK is composite (id, workspace_id). D10 now says that typed target FKs are id-only on purpose, unlike 0096, because files move between projects. moth_views uses workspace_id DEFAULT current_workspace_id() for both scope kinds.
  - *Evidence:* 0096 task_items FKs are composite (id, project_id), so the draft's D10 citation was wrong.

#### Decisions

- **H1** Session 1 creates only moth_history in this area. Per answer 6: moth_views and moth_layout_positions in S2; moth_tag_types, moth_tags, moth_tag_assignments, moth_tag_nominations, moth_relationships and workspace_notes in S3; boards, board_elements and sticky_locks in S4. moth_local_scopes and file_body_locations are not created: the NAS and bodies areas need neither, and answer 6 does not list them.
  - *Why:* This is her ruling. It also follows 0041's precedent that a table with no writer is schema debt.
- **H2** History is cloud-only. Desktop-only (Local Server) projects record no history. The S2 Local Server adapter returns no rows and a window of recording_since = now() for every type, which the UI labels 'not recorded for projects kept only on this computer'. If any local history is added later, it stays on that computer and never goes into moth_history.
  - *Why:* Her rulings: desktop-only projects appear with no live features, and missing periods are labelled. Answer 2 keeps desktop moves on the computer. This also removes the forged-local-row class (SEC-06).
- **H3** There is no baseline copy. mark_recording rows with mark_kind 'baseline' are written per existing workspace and type at apply time; a workspace created later starts at its created_at. Every captured change stores full projections of both sides, and the state at T is rebuilt backwards from the live graph. The purge is a plain DELETE where observed_at < cutoff, marks included.
  - *Why:* There is no O(rows) snapshot and no compaction, and after 90 days now() - 90 days takes over from the purged baseline mark, so the rebuild stays correct.
- **H4** Gaps are restarts. A capture failure (mark_kind 'capture_failed', scoped to the failed row's sides) and a migration that suspends capture ('suspended', workspace-wide, inserted by that migration as postgres after it re-enables capture) both mean 'the record for this type and scope is only reliable from here'.
  - *Why:* One missed row corrupts every earlier state of its entity (T-04), and a restart labels exactly that period. Scoping a failure's mark to the failed row's sides leaks nothing (SEC-05).
- **H5** Redact-on-lock. When a files or folders side is classified locked (moth_file_row_locked or moth_folder_path_locked), capture writes no row and UPDATEs every earlier row of that entity to prior=NULL, state=NULL, locked=true. Apart from the purge, this is the only change ever made to a moth_history row.
  - *Why:* Money and Legal stay out of Moth for every role, even after a trash or purge (SEC-03). Money-ness only grows (bodies area: storage_path is immutable and moved rows are never rekeyed), so redaction never has to be undone.
- **H6** moth_history is ENABLE without FORCE, has zero policies, REVOKE ALL from PUBLIC, anon and authenticated (the sequence included), and is read only through the DEFINER RPCs moth_history_read and moth_history_window. It is added to the handbook's FORCE-exception list beside edit_history and file_events.
  - *Why:* This is the 0012 precedent: the only writers are definers. Keeping all audience logic in one body also avoids an oracle helper in a policy.
- **H7** A side is visible only when all of these hold: the row is not locked; the side's project id is not NULL; public._moth_target_eligible('project', side_project, false) is true now; and the class snapshot admits the caller ('shared' means anyone; 'private' means owner = auth.uid() or current_app_role() = 'admin'; 'personal' means owner = auth.uid(); NULL or anything else means false). The entity is hidden when any of its rows is locked, or when its live row exists and fails the live gate: _moth_target_eligible('file' or 'folder', id, true) for files and folders, _moth_target_eligible('project', id, false) for projects, and the live row's project passing for every other type.
  - *Why:* Both audiences, present and original, with the personal arm owner-only and no admin leg (the personal area's helper), and the private arm matching 0072. Trashed items keep their history so the trash view and the scrub agree; a trashed project hides its history.
- **H8** Output mapping: when both sides are visible, actions are reported as added (create), changed (update), moved, trashed, restored and removed (delete). When only the state side is visible the row is 'added' with prior and prior_project_id NULL. When only the prior side is visible it is 'removed' with state and state_project_id NULL. When neither is visible the row is omitted. Rows carry row_key and never the bigint id, and no count is returned anywhere.
  - *Why:* Nothing appears that a person cannot already see: there are no placeholders for hidden places, and the identity sequence does not leak volume.
- **H9** The history migration is the last database migration of Session 1. Its pre-flight requires objects from the other areas (see interfaces). It refuses a re-run once a later migration is recorded (0098's guard), and it checks the capture body's md5.
  - *Why:* Its WHEN lists and classifier depend on the personal-space columns, the classifier, the folder lock guard, the moves composite FKs and files.share_missing_since from the NAS area.
- **H10** NAS catalogue rows are captured like any files row. share_missing_since is projected as the boolean 'missing'. Capture ignores the wilson.network_share_writer setting, which suppresses only realtime.
  - *Why:* A file appearing on or disappearing from the office server is a structural change. Its volume is bounded by the NAS apply cap of 5,000 rows, and later checks write only rows that changed.
- **H11** Later sessions add tables to capture by restating fn_moth_history_capture and moth_history_project from their latest bodies (md5-guarded), and widen the entity CHECK in a CHECK-only migration with DROP + ADD NOT VALID, then VALIDATE in the next migration. Before S3 captures any edge, workspace note or tag, it adds the per-side peer columns and the 'workspace' class.
  - *Why:* T-09: no lock long enough to stall every R.A.B.B.I.T. write. SEC-09: two-endpoint and workspace-level entities need audience columns.

#### Schema changes

- **public.moth_history (table, Session 1)** (restates from: new): Columns:
- id BIGINT GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY
- row_key UUID NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid()
- workspace_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES public.workspaces(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
- entity_type TEXT NOT NULL
- entity_id UUID NOT NULL (no FK; marks use the workspace id)
- action TEXT NOT NULL
- mark_kind TEXT NULL
- observed_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
- actor_user_id UUID (no FK, no label)
- prior_project_id UUID, prior_class TEXT, prior_owner_id UUID, prior JSONB
- state_project_id UUID, state_class TEXT, state_owner_id UUID, state JSONB
- locked BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false

CHECKs, named in sort order so each fixture breaks exactly one (rule 29):
- moth_history_a_action_chk: action IN ('create','update','move','trash','restore','delete','mark_recording').
- moth_history_b_entity_chk: (action = 'mark_recording' AND entity_type ~ '^[a-z_]{1,63}$') OR entity_type IN ('projects','phases','assets','tasks','files','folders','scenes','shots','levels','experiences','task_items').
- moth_history_c_class_chk: each *_class is NULL or IN ('shared','private','personal'); 'personal' requires its *_owner_id; 'shared' requires its *_owner_id NULL; 'private' may have a NULL owner.
- moth_history_d_shape_chk:
  - create ⇒ prior, prior_project_id and prior_class are NULL;
  - delete ⇒ state, state_project_id and state_class are NULL;
  - mark_recording ⇒ prior and state NULL, mark_kind IN ('baseline','suspended','capture_failed'), NOT locked, actor NULL;
  - mark_kind IN ('baseline','suspended') ⇒ both project ids NULL;
  - any other action ⇒ mark_kind NULL.
- moth_history_e_locked_chk: locked ⇒ prior IS NULL AND state IS NULL.
- moth_history_f_projection_chk: (prior IS NULL OR moth_history_projection_ok(prior)) AND the same for state.

Indexes:
- UNIQUE (row_key)
- (workspace_id, observed_at DESC, id DESC)
- (workspace_id, state_project_id, observed_at DESC, id DESC) WHERE state_project_id IS NOT NULL
- the same for prior_project_id
- (workspace_id, entity_type, entity_id)
- (workspace_id, entity_type, entity_id) WHERE locked
- (observed_at)

RLS and grants: ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY, NOT FORCE (the header says why). No policies. REVOKE ALL ON TABLE and on the identity sequence FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated; GRANT ALL TO service_role.

COMMENT: metadata only; 90 days; written by capture; redacted on lock; read through RPCs; two audiences; on no realtime topic and in no takeout.
- **public.moth_history_projection_ok(jsonb) and public.moth_history_project(text, jsonb)** (restates from: new): moth_history_projection_ok(p JSONB) RETURNS BOOLEAN
- LANGUAGE sql IMMUTABLE PARALLEL SAFE, SET search_path = public, pg_temp. Never returns NULL.
- True when: p is an object of at most 4096 octets; every key is in the allowlist below; and every value is a scalar of at most 300 characters, or an array of at most 200 scalars.
- Allowlist: name, title, slug, kind, type, type_label, status, document_kind, mime_type, size_bytes, categories, is_core_definer, missing, number, parent_id, parent_phase_id, folder_id, phase_id, asset_id, task_id, scene_id, shot_id, level_id, experience_id, scene_ids, shot_ids, level_ids, experience_ids, item_type, item_id, position, sort_order, truncated.

moth_history_project(p_table TEXT, p_row JSONB) RETURNS JSONB
- IMMUTABLE. jsonb_strip_nulls over a per-table map. Text values are cut to 300 characters and arrays to 200 elements; truncated=true is set when anything is cut.
- Per-table maps:
  - projects: title, status, project_type→type
  - phases: name, status, parent_phase_id, sort_order
  - assets: name, type, type_label, status, phase_id, sort_order, scene_ids, shot_ids, level_ids, experience_ids
  - tasks: title, status, phase_id, asset_id, scene_id, shot_id
  - files: name, kind, mime_type, size_bytes, document_kind, tags→categories, is_core_definer, folder_id, phase_id, asset_id, task_id, scene_id, shot_id, level_id, experience_id, (share_missing_since IS NOT NULL)→missing
  - folders: coalesce(label, slug)→name, slug, kind, entity_type→item_type, parent_id, sort_order, asset_id, scene_id, shot_id, level_id, experience_id
  - scenes: name, scene_number→number, status, sort_order
  - shots: name, shot_number→number, scene_id, status, sort_order
  - levels and experiences: name, status, sort_order
  - task_items: task_id, entity_type→item_type, entity_id→item_id, position
- Never emitted: storage_path, storage_provider, thumbnail_url, thumbnail_image, description, notes, path, folder_root, any money column (budget_*, bid_days, logged_days), or any person id.
- Both functions: REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated.
- **public.fn_moth_history_capture() and triggers trg_<t>_moth_history + trg_<t>_moth_history_upd on the 11 tables** (restates from: new): LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public, pg_temp; every relation is schema-qualified.
(1) v_old := to_jsonb(OLD) for UPDATE and DELETE; v_new := to_jsonb(NEW) for INSERT and UPDATE.
(2) Workspace: the row's workspace_id, else public.projects.workspace_id via project_id (0119 as built: never current_workspace_id(); see its note). If still NULL, or no public.workspaces row exists, RETURN NULL.
(3) Side projects: for projects, the row's own id; otherwise the side's project_id.
(4) Side class and owner, in a nested BEGIN that leaves the class NULL on error or a missing projects row:
  - personal_owner_id NOT NULL → 'personal', owner = personal_owner_id
  - is_private → 'private', owner = created_by (may be NULL)
  - otherwise 'shared'
(5) Main block, in a nested BEGIN:
  (a) Lock. For files: public.moth_file_row_locked(ws, project_id, id, is_financial, storage_path, tags, folder_id) per side, each side's own project. For folders: public.moth_folder_path_locked(path) per side. Other tables are never locked. If any present side is locked: UPDATE public.moth_history SET prior=NULL, state=NULL, locked=true WHERE workspace_id=ws AND entity_type=TG_TABLE_NAME AND entity_id=id AND NOT locked; then RETURN NULL.
  (b) Projections: moth_history_project(TG_TABLE_NAME, side).
  (c) Action, first match wins:
    - INSERT → create; DELETE → delete;
    - deleted_at NULL→set → trash; set→NULL → restore;
    - a placement change → move. Placement keys: files project_id, folder_id; folders project_id, parent_id; phases parent_phase_id; assets phase_id; tasks phase_id, asset_id; shots scene_id;
    - otherwise update.
  (d) Skip only an 'update' whose projections, project ids, classes and owners are all unchanged.
  (e) INSERT the row, with actor auth.uid().
(6) On an exception in step 5: RAISE WARNING, then, in a nested BEGIN that swallows its own failure, INSERT mark_recording with mark_kind 'capture_failed', entity_type = TG_TABLE_NAME, entity_id = ws, and each side's project, class and owner when known. RETURN NULL. The user's write never fails.
REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated.
Triggers, per table:
- trg_<t>_moth_history: AFTER INSERT OR DELETE FOR EACH ROW.
- trg_<t>_moth_history_upd: AFTER UPDATE FOR EACH ROW WHEN (ROW(<cols>) IS DISTINCT FROM ROW(<cols>)), with these pinned lists:
  - projects: title, status, project_type, is_private, personal_owner_id, deleted_at
  - phases: name, status, parent_phase_id, sort_order, deleted_at
  - assets: name, type, type_label, status, phase_id, sort_order, scene_ids, shot_ids, level_ids, experience_ids, deleted_at
  - tasks: title, status, phase_id, asset_id, scene_id, shot_id, project_id, deleted_at
  - files: name, project_id, folder_id, kind, mime_type, size_bytes, document_kind, tags, is_core_definer, phase_id, asset_id, task_id, scene_id, shot_id, level_id, experience_id, deleted_at, is_financial, storage_path, share_missing_since
  - folders: project_id, parent_id, slug, label, kind, entity_type, sort_order, path, asset_id, scene_id, shot_id, level_id, experience_id
  - scenes: name, scene_number, status, sort_order
  - shots: name, shot_number, scene_id, status, sort_order
  - levels and experiences: name, status, sort_order
  - task_items: task_id, entity_type, entity_id, position
  is_financial, storage_path, tags and path are in the WHEN lists for classification only.
- **Baseline marks (data, in the Session 1 history migration)** (restates from: new): INSERT mark_recording with mark_kind 'baseline', entity_id = workspace id and observed_at = now(), for every existing workspace × each of the 11 types, WHERE NOT EXISTS a mark for the same (workspace, type, 'baseline'). A later migration that adds a type writes that type's baselines the same way. A migration that disables these triggers, or uses session_replication_role = replica, inserts a 'suspended' mark per affected type and workspace after re-enabling them.
- **public.moth_history_read(uuid, timestamptz, uuid, int)** (restates from: new): moth_history_read(p_scope_project UUID DEFAULT NULL, p_since TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NULL, p_after UUID DEFAULT NULL, p_limit INT DEFAULT 1000)
RETURNS TABLE(row_key UUID, entity_type TEXT, entity_id UUID, action TEXT, observed_at TIMESTAMPTZ, actor_user_id UUID, prior_project_id UUID, prior JSONB, state_project_id UUID, state JSONB).
LANGUAGE plpgsql STABLE SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public, pg_temp.

One refusal for every cause: RAISE 'that history is not available' (42501) when auth.uid() or current_workspace_id() is NULL; when NOT has_active_membership; when p_scope_project is given and NOT _moth_target_eligible('project', p_scope_project, false); or when p_after is given and no row of this workspace has that row_key.

Clamps: p_limit to 1..5000; p_since to at least now() - 90 days.

Rows: workspace_id = current workspace; action <> 'mark_recording'; NOT locked; observed_at >= p_since; strictly older than p_after's (observed_at, id); in scope when p_scope is given (prior_project_id or state_project_id = p_scope); the entity gate and the side tests of decision H7, with each distinct project's eligibility computed once in a CTE. Ordered observed_at DESC, id DESC, with the H8 mapping.

REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated, service_role.
- **public.moth_history_window(uuid)** (restates from: new): moth_history_window(p_scope_project UUID DEFAULT NULL) RETURNS TABLE(entity_type TEXT, project_id UUID, recording_since TIMESTAMPTZ). Same opening checks and refusal as moth_history_read.
Rows:
(a) Per type, one row with project_id NULL. The types are the 11 plus any type holding a baseline: recording_since = GREATEST(max(observed_at) of that workspace's 'baseline' and 'suspended' marks for the type, workspaces.created_at, now() - 90 days).
(b) Per (type, project) for 'capture_failed' marks where a side passes the H7 side test for that side's project, and that project equals p_scope when it is given: recording_since = GREATEST(max(observed_at), (a)). The row is returned only when it is later than (a).
'capture_failed' marks with no side scope are never returned.
Grants as moth_history_read.
- **public.purge_moth_history(interval) + cron 'wilson-purge-moth-history'** (restates from: new): SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public, pg_temp. IF p_retention < INTERVAL '1 day' THEN RAISE. Then DELETE FROM public.moth_history WHERE observed_at < now() - p_retention, and return the row count. REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated; GRANT TO service_role. Scheduled in 0012's guarded DO block as cron.schedule('wilson-purge-moth-history', '45 4 * * *', $$SELECT public.purge_moth_history();$$), with a NOTICE when pg_cron is absent and a WARNING on error. It never touches file_events or file_moves. 04:45 is free: 04:39, 04:43, 04:47, 04:51, 04:55 and 04:59 are taken, and the moves area's file-moves job must choose another slot.
- **Session 1 history migration: pre-flight and post-conditions** (restates from: new): PRE-FLIGHT. It RAISEs unless all of these exist:
- projects.personal_owner_id and projects_personal_shape_chk
- fn_projects_identity_guard
- _moth_target_eligible(text, uuid, boolean), moth_can_read_target(text, uuid), moth_file_row_locked(uuid, uuid, uuid, boolean, text, text[], uuid), moth_folder_path_locked(text)
- trg_folders_lock_guard
- the composite FKs on files.folder_id and folders.parent_id (moves area)
- files.share_missing_since (NAS area)
- file_row_is_money, rabbit_money_segment, has_active_membership, current_workspace_id
It also refuses a re-run once a later migration is recorded in supabase_migrations.schema_migrations, and compares the md5 of the capture body (carriage returns stripped) with its own constant.

POST-CONDITIONS. Function text is matched on comment-stripped prosrc. The migration RAISEs unless all of these hold:
- moth_history has relrowsecurity true and relforcerowsecurity false, and zero pg_policies rows.
- anon and authenticated have no table privilege and no sequence USAGE.
- The set of tables with both capture triggers equals the 11-name CHECK list, in both directions. Per table, the tgtype bits cover INSERT, DELETE and UPDATE, and pg_get_triggerdef of every _upd trigger equals its pinned text.
- Every new SECURITY DEFINER has proconfig search_path set and no EXECUTE for PUBLIC or anon.
- moth_history_projection_ok('{"storage_path":"x"}') is false and moth_history_projection_ok('{"name":"x"}') is true.
- The capture body contains moth_history_project(, moth_file_row_locked(, SET prior = NULL and the workspaces-exists skip.
- pg_get_function_result of moth_history_read contains no bigint.
- The cron job exists when pg_cron does.
- **DEFERRED (S2): moth_views; references; moth_layout_positions** (restates from: new): moth_views:
- workspace_id DEFAULT current_workspace_id() for both scope kinds, kept out of the INSERT grant.
- UNIQUE (id, workspace_id); FK (project_id, workspace_id) REFERENCES projects (id, workspace_id) ON DELETE CASCADE.
- scope_kind 'project' requires project_id; 'aggregate' requires owner_id, pinned to auth.uid().
- name, position, 0098-style revision, a cap of 200, config JSONB of at most 32 KB with a CHECK that config::text holds no uuid-shaped value.
- SELECT: workspace AND membership AND (project AND moth_can_read_target('project', project_id)) OR (aggregate AND owner_id = auth.uid()). Writes use moth_can_write_target('project', project_id), or owner for aggregate views.
References (pins and focus):
- Stored as rows, either a moth_view_refs child or flagged positions (S2 picks). Typed generated FKs, the view joined by composite (view_id, workspace_id), and each reader filtered live through moth_can_read_target.
moth_layout_positions:
- Composite (view_id, workspace_id) FK; target_type CHECK; STORED generated typed columns, each with an id-only FK ON DELETE CASCADE (unlike 0096's, on purpose, because files move projects).
- x and y finite, absolute value at most 1e7; pinned; owner_id forced for aggregate views and personal-space targets.
- SELECT: view visible AND moth_can_read_target(target) AND (owner_id IS NULL OR owner_id = auth.uid()). Writes: owner, or moth_can_write_target on the view's project.
- Column grants leave out id, so there is no 23505 oracle (else recorded as S4b-07's class).
S2 restates _moth_target_eligible to add the phase, asset, task, scene, shot, level and experience kinds. Nothing in S2 is captured into moth_history. Views are hard-deleted with re-insert undo.
- **DEFERRED (S3): moth_tag_types, moth_tags, moth_tag_assignments, moth_tag_nominations, moth_relationships, workspace_notes; history extension** (restates from: new): Shapes as the draft, with these fixes:
- Every intra-Moth FK is composite (id, workspace_id): type_id, canonical_id, nomination tag_id and result_tag_id, and assignment tag_id.
- canonical_id and every review or apply column appear in no client grant; they are written only by the DEFINER merge and apply RPCs (live admin through is_live_workspace_admin, never a manager or the JWT claim).
- A validate trigger raises the same SQLSTATE and message as an FK refusal.
- Target and endpoint hops are moth_can_read_target, and company writes are moth_can_write_target. Personal tiers are owner-only with no admin bypass. A refused tier or owner change RAISEs, never reverts silently.
- Manual relationships and workspace_notes soft-delete through restated fn_trash_authz and purge_soft_deleted (latest bodies at the tip; 0092 and 0067 today, or later S1 restatements).
History: before capturing tags, assignments, relationships or note metadata, S3 does the following.
(a) ALTER TABLE moth_history ADD COLUMN prior_peer_project_id, prior_peer_class, prior_peer_owner_id, and the same for state. A side is visible only when its peer also passes.
(b) Widens the class CHECK with 'workspace' (project NULL allowed, for company tags and shared or company notes) and uses per-side 'personal' with an owner for personal tags and notes.
(c) Widens the entity CHECK by NOT VALID, then VALIDATE, in its own migrations.
(d) Restates the capture and projector from their latest bodies, with baselines for the new types. The projection never holds a note body.
- **DEFERRED (S4): boards, board_elements, sticky_locks** (restates from: new): boards: folder_id UNIQUE; FK (folder_id, project_id) REFERENCES folders (id, project_id); SELECT and WITH CHECK require moth_can_read_target('folder', folder_id), which refuses locked trees, plus write through moth_can_write_target; no deleted_at (trashed through its folder).
board_elements:
- FK (board_id, project_id) to boards.
- Same-board connectors by composite FK.
- file_id REFERENCES files ON DELETE SET NULL. A write trigger refuses a file failing moth_can_read_target for the writer, and SELECT hides an element whose file_id fails it for the reader.
- Hard delete; never cascades to files.
sticky_locks: no client grants at all; claim, renew, release and status only through DEFINER RPCs that check the board hop; expiry by the database clock; generation fencing designed with S4's protocol.
Board existence joins moth_history through the S3-extended shape.

#### Consumers to change
